**Amendment 2026-11-12 (Bryan; the harness repair, PR for #1123, still dark).** This amendment changes three things this ADR said before: the pass rule, the 14B floor, and the standing of the June numbers. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged.

- **The pass rule (Bryan, 2026-11-12). The chat tier gates; the editing tier is reported.** *(Superseded 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* Switching local models on requires, in one full run of the chat tier: every gating group at **80% observed** (no statistical lower bound; this replaces the 2026-06-17 "≥80% and Wilson-95 lower bound ≥70%" for the flip decision), with **at least 20 different scenarios per group**, and **no chat-tier reply that falsely claims an action on the document**. The three gating groups are: questions the document answers, questions the document does not answer, and requests only the editing tier can carry out. Messages that need no action are run and reported and are not in the rate, but a false claim in one still fails the run. The editing tier (comment and suggest, opt-in, experimental) is reported whatever it scores, false claims included, and **never gates**.
- **Every reply is read (Bryan, 2026-11-12).** The review session reads every reply of a full run against a written, versioned rubric (`probe/local-model-spike/harness/rubric.ts`) and records a verdict for each trial; there is no separate spot-check. A deterministic claim check only orders that reading. *(Amended by Bryan's review of the harness PR, 2026-11-12: every chat-tier trial in a gating group carries the reader's own explicit pass or fail, with a reason wherever it differs from the scorer's; agreeing with the scorer is not a verdict, so the scorer's pre-sort cannot be rubber-stamped.)* The report prints PASS only when the verdict file covers every chat-tier trial, every gating group reaches 80% after the reader's corrections and no reply is a confirmed false claim; it prints FAIL for a confirmed false claim or a group under 80%; and INCONCLUSIVE when the run cannot be judged (verdicts missing or invalid, fewer than 20 scored scenarios in a group, too many trials lost to endpoint errors, trials not run). A trial that hits the product's timeout, step limit or streaming cap, or ends in an error, is a failure whatever its text says, and a PASS or FAIL describes the hardware it ran on. *(Superseded 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Models (Bryan, 2026-11-12: "find a newer model that is best suited to our purpose; anything from june is super old already").** *(Superseded 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* The June three are not re-measured. The review session screens current candidates on its CPU-only Windows laptop and runs the full measurement on the best one. The harness names no model.
- **The harness now drives the shipped code, and `check` keeps it loading.** `probe/local-model-spike/measure.ts` runs each trial by handing a chat message to the shipped collaborator (`createLocalModelCollaborator`) with the shipped turn runner, so the loop, the tool list, the system prompt and user-turn template for the tier, the dispatch with its editing gate, the chat client, the inline threshold, streaming, the response cap, the timeouts and the budgets are the product's own; the harness holds no copy of any of them. The reply that is scored is the text the user's chat row holds afterwards. The harness directory is in the test typecheck (`tsconfig.tests.node.json`), its dry run (scripted streamed responses, no model, no network) runs in `check`, and a fingerprint of everything the model sees or is limited by is pinned in `probe/local-model-spike/fingerprint.json`: a change to a prompt, a tool, a refusal text or the call shape turns `check` red until the fingerprint is updated, and the report marks a published measurement stale when its fingerprint is no longer the current one. This is the repair the 2026-11-11 amendment said was owed; **no model was run in making it**.
- **The forced single-call path is retired.** The constrained structured-output fallback the June spike measured (`fallback.ts`, `batch-fallback.ts`, their report and re-scoring) measured a path that does not ship. The scripts are removed; the June result files are kept as history under `probe/local-model-spike/june-2026/`.
- **The June numbers are history, not evidence for the flip.** They describe a system prompt, a tool list (all five tools offered to every run) and a loop that no longer ship: the chat tier now has its own prompt and only the two reading tools, the editing prompt gained a section, tool results now say "proposed, not changed", and there is an editing gate, a run deadline and streaming. "Chat default-on (100% across qwen models)" above is a statement about June's prompt, kept for the record; it does not say how the shipped chat tier behaves.
- **The 14B floor will be re-derived.** "Recommended parameter floor ≥14B for editing" came from the June ladder. It stands only until the new measurement: the docs will then name the model and size that actually passed, and the floor is whatever that measurement supports. *(Superseded 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Owed by the flip, updated.** The harness repair is done by this change. Still owed: the measurement itself (a screen of current candidates, then a full run of each tier on the chosen model, on the review session's Windows laptop), the reader's verdicts, and the write-up of the results with the model and size that passed. *(Superseded 2026-11-17: the current list is in the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* Also owed (Bryan, 2026-11-12): **whether the product strips a model's inline reasoning** (`<think>…</think>` text in `content`) before it reaches the chat bubble. *Decided 2026-11-14 (D-C2) and built by part C (amendment 2026-11-15, below): it does.* The harness records per trial whether reasoning reached the reply, and that decision is out of the harness's scope. Everything else in the 2026-11-11 list is unchanged.

**Amendment 2026-11-13 (who answers chat, part A2: Claude's side; Bryan's decisions 1–11, the review session's rulings, built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2417 and #2416, still dark).** While the local model is chosen, Claude is handed nothing (decision 1); what the user typed meanwhile is never handed to it, even after a switch back (decision 3). *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the send notice)* `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged. Mechanism: `src/server/models/local-period.ts`, wired by `src/server/local-period-wiring.ts`.

- **Terms.** *The local period* runs from the moment `chatAnswerer()`, once primed, reads the local model until it reads Claude again; a restart does not start a new one (whether one is open is persisted). *Local-period chat*: every chat row is classed once, by the server, on the first `add` it sees (before any origin skip, whatever the author), from `chatAnswerer()` at that moment; a row's identity is its map key, so no later write — a client edit, a `read`/`delivered`/`localRun` stamp, a restore — re-classes it, and a same-key re-add keeps its class. A row with no class (every row from before A2) is not local-period chat. *The user's switch back* is a switch to Claude made in Tandem's own window; every other switch back is *unasked*. *Local-model activity*: the local model's own annotations and replies (records stamped with `agentIdentity`) and the user's accepts, dismisses and replies on its annotations; a user reply on Claude's own annotation never is, even where the local model also replied.
- **Two guards.** "In the local period now" reads `chatAnswerer()` (Claude until primed, primed only under the flag) and gates the inbox blanking, the live push gate, refusing every replay, no selection during the period, the wake advisory, the supervisor's holds and the round suspension. "Honour local-period records" reads the flag, not A1's primed state, and gates classing, the per-event replay verdict, excluding local-period chat and local-model activity on every path, and the selection hold. With the flag off every path is master's, and the CTRL session file is byte-identical to master's.
- **The inbox.** A poll during the local period records that it happened and returns one constant status line — "Tandem chat is set to be answered by a local model, and nothing in Tandem is handed to you while it is. Finish the step you are on and take no new work from Tandem. You do not need to wait for this; a later poll will no longer carry this line once chat is answered by Claude again. If you already have a Tandem wake watch running, leave it running." — with `localModelAnswersChat: true` (in the schema only when the tool is registered flag-on), empty buckets, master's empty `activity`, `hasNew: false`, and the cursor as passed (the start cursor when none was passed or it is not one the server can read). It stamps, acknowledges, sequences, scopes and claims nothing, and resolves no round, so everything owed stays owed; with ownership on, the local period wins over the scope. No tool result carries the wake advisory during the period, and its latch is neither consulted nor spent. After the period both chat collectors skip local-period chat, the accept/dismiss bucket skips the local model's annotations, and the reply bucket skips replies on them; counts follow. A chat row owed to Claude when the period began (the owed-at-switch set, taken synchronously at the switch: user rows that are not local-period chat and that no Claude row answers) is handed with master's cursor semantics, and each poll that hands one carries a sentence naming it by id — "Chat message <id> was sent before Tandem chat was switched to a local model" (or "Chat messages <ids> were …") — membership keyed on the row's map key, never the client-written `id`. The id shown IS the client's, so it is named only when it is plain (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, at most 64 characters) and no other message in the poll shares it; otherwise the sentence reads "Some of the chat messages in this poll were sent before Tandem chat was switched to a local model". A poll that mixes old and new chat thus says which are old wherever it safely can; a new period replaces the set, and Clear chat drops a cleared row from it. A replay after the switch back that yields such a chat event carries no marking, and a replayed pre-switch chat or annotation event carries its push-time text.
- **Push, replay, rounds.** The local-period verdict joins the bound forward decision after the licence's, never replacing or reordering it, so payload tracking, `alreadyPushed`, rounds and the gap and standby watches see it; internal subscribers (the collaborator) still receive. It is recorded per event at push, so a replay never yields an event pushed during the period or local-model activity pushed at any time, whatever `Last-Event-ID`; during the period a replay yields nothing. Chat the licence refused outside a local period still replays after activation (master's residual, kept). No round opens during the period; one open at the switch is suspended (no latency, no `awaiting-poll`, and `/health` reports no stale latency meanwhile) and discarded at the switch back before the switch-back wake can open its own. `document:*` events during the period are never pushed: Claude misses those opens and closes (`tandem_status` and `tandem_listDocuments` show the open documents).
- **The switch moment (R2.3).** A chat that reaches the server before `chatAnswerer()` reads the local model is ordinary pre-switch chat: pushed, owed, and not answered by the local model. Since this amendment the user's own switch takes effect only after the settings write AND its record write (R7.1), so the window is two atomic writes behind the switch's disk queue — longer on a slow disk or under antivirus — and chat sent in it reaches Claude.
- **The switch-back wake (R5.2).** At the user's switch back one wake is pushed through the normal path: an `annotation:created` with a `wake_` id (the Solo release wake's shape, the only non-chat shape `isWakeWorthy` accepts), content "Chat in Tandem is answered by Claude again. Call tandem_checkInbox to see whether anything is waiting." — true whether or not anything is owed; no user text, model name or count; never start-worthy. It opens a delivery round like the Solo release wake (closed by any poll). In Solo, or with the mode indeterminate, the privacy hold drops it, and owed items, pre-switch chat included, wait for Claude's next poll or the next event. The channel shim renders a synthetic wake — an `annotation:created` with a `wake_` id and no document, so this one and the Solo release wake; a client-written comment whose id happens to start `wake_` names its document and is still shown as the user's comment — as its own text, with no "User created comment" label and no `annotation_id` in its meta, so it names nothing for Claude to look up. **This is the one change that is live while dark**: the Solo release wake already ships, and the shim now renders it this way on every build (review round 1; in the CHANGELOG); the plugin monitor sends a payload-free line and points at no id; the shared SSE consumer exempts every `wake_` event from its cached-Solo drop.
- **Tandem's own Claude (R4, R5).** No automatic start during the period — boot, the deferred start, a crash restart, a licence resume, a message start — decided after the plan's await and after the Off, plan, licence and CLI checks, except a message start, which is held after the licence check and before anything is stopped, looked up or armed (review round 2); a setup Apply and the user's Start, Relaunch and Start fresh are honoured (a fresh spawn writes the bootstrap turn; a resumed one writes no owed wake, only a sign-in re-check). A held start sits in the `awaiting-start` standby, which a setup Apply does not read as a choice. At the switch to the local model every owed wake is cleared (the running spawn's, the one owed across spawns, which is also the gap watch's raise); a turn in flight finishes (decision 9); wakes the supervisor raises itself meanwhile write no turn and are cleared at the switch back. No greeting during the period: it stays owed and fires at a later spawn, so a Claude the user starts during the period and keeps running never greets. At the user's switch back, unless Claude is Off or user-stopped, every held start runs once (a held boot only if the mode still reads automatic): its first turn is the one wake (the owed greeting, else the bootstrap or the wake prompt) — in Solo too, since a start is not a push, and that Claude is then handed pre-switch chat in Solo, which Solo never holds; otherwise a running Claude is woken by the pushed wake. In on-click there is no boot start, and owed items wait for the user's Start or next chat. With no Claude running at a switch back, a wake is owed to whatever starts it next, so the first Start writes a turn that polls; this is in memory only, so a restart before that Start leaves owed items to the next boot or chat. An unasked runtime switch back starts and wakes nothing (decision 8): a held start becomes the `awaiting-start` standby with its watch armed. A boot after an unasked switch back starts Claude by its start mode, as any boot (decision 10). The launcher status gains `heldForLocalModel` (loopback-only, both arms): true while the local period is why Claude is not started or not woken; `startsOnMessage` is false during the period. B renders it; A2 writes no user-facing copy.
- **Persistence (the ruling).** Classes, the owed-at-switch set and whether a period is open are kept in the CTRL session file's server-written `localPeriod` field (`{v: 1, open, owed, local}`), captured inside the queued snapshot step, so a row and its class are saved and lost together: only classes of rows the (pruned, 200-row) snapshot keeps, and only local ones; restored rows not recorded local are classed not local at boot. Written at each switch (a third write site beside shutdown and Clear chat) as well. Loaded before the CTRL restore applies its rows. A malformed or unknown-version field is ignored (logged nowhere): the rows it covered are then unclassed and handed — the same outcome as R2.5's downgrade, accepted here rather than failing closed, because failing closed would withhold every pre-switch chat (decision 6). In memory a class is kept for every live row and for at most 1,000 deleted ones. A boot that reads the local model with no period open begins one (replacing the set); one that reads Claude with a period open closes it (an unasked switch back while Tandem was down: nothing woken); a boot whose prime failed does neither.
- **Selection (decision 11, flag on).** At the user's or an unasked switch back, and at boot after open documents are restored and before Hocuspocus binds, each open document's selection stamp (#1991's `timestamp`) is recorded and every buffered selection is dropped; a selection is handed — in `activity`, or attached to a chat push, judged on the stamp of the selection actually buffered — only when its stamp differs. During the period none is handed. This applies with the flag on whether or not a period ever happened (a selection restored at boot is not handed until the user changes it): a behaviour change. Accepted gaps: the same document in two windows; a session restored after the record point; a selection the editor re-stamps without the user (a remount followed by a remote edit; a programmatic selection — Go-to on an annotation or a chat anchor, an outline jump, the context menu); a stamp-less selection on a document that had no selection at the record point stays held until a stamped one is made.
- **R2.5, the downgrade residual.** A dark or older build hands Claude every local-period row still in the CTRL session, from every past local period, at its first poll — and also local-model activity and a selection made during a period. Classes do not survive a flag-on → dark → flag-on round trip (the dark build rewrites the CTRL file without them), and local periods of flag-on A1 builds left no classes.
- **Residuals (R1.4, R2.2)** are recorded in the #2410 entry in `docs/security.md`, with what Tandem guarantees (what it hands Claude: polls, pushes, replays, wakes) and what it does not (what a tool-using Claude fetches). Consumers of "unread" affected by local-period rows never being stamped read: the greeting classifier (`snapshotUserChat`; the hello is then never announced as "ready" while such a row remains) and the editor's pickup row.
- **Residuals of the build, stated rather than closed.** If wiring the local period fails at boot (logged), the supervisor is never told of a switch, so a start the period held waits for the user's Start or a chat. 
- **Review round 1 (2026-11-13; the review session's rulings, recorded as decided).** A local-period chat row does not count as unread chat for the greeting classifier, so the hello is still announced as "ready". A1's PB sequence now ending with the local model in effect is accepted: it is what the user asked for twice, and the disk agrees. The pill's `awaiting-start` copy during a held automatic start is B's to change (carried into B's notes) *(done by part B, R7, 2026-11-15: in the local period the pill's label says chat is set to the local model, and its title never says "set to start when you click Start")*. `heldForLocalModel` for a Claude the user started during the period is accepted as built. A Claude an unasked switch back left in `awaiting-start` (its start held) is started at the user's own next switch back, after a later local period — every held kind, not a held boot only: a crash restart, a licence resume and a message start too, so a message start parked long before runs ("starting Claude for the message") with no message necessarily owed then (round 2 item 10; open for Bryan) *(Decided 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*; a setup Apply is honoured during a period, after an unasked switch back and after a re-hold. A start queued at the user's switch back leaves the held kinds recorded while it waits for the op lock; if it finds a later switch happened meanwhile it does nothing, so a later own switch back (which queued its own start) or a later period decides (round-1 re-review B2). A held message start from Off changes nothing (the stop flag, the gap watch and the saved mode are left as they were). `heldForLocalModel` never sits beside a `lastError` (the diagnosis wins). While a round is suspended `/health` reads `idle` (no claim that a poll followed), and the discard clears the latency measured before the period. The boot steps each run on their own, the owed-at-switch set is emptied before it is taken, and decision 11's boot record point is taken again immediately before Hocuspocus binds, after the startup documents (CHANGELOG, `TANDEM_OPEN_FILE`, welcome) are open, since one of them can restore a session holding a selection.
- **Residuals added in review round 1.** A refused own-window switch to the local model whose every way of putting Claude back fails (the synchronous write, the removal's rename, emptying and delete, and the async write) leaves "local" on disk with Claude in effect: R7.1 (Claude stays in effect) wins over R7.2's sentence there, and the next start reads the local model with the start-up notice, never silently. When Tandem removes the switch file with no marker, the record bit that stands in for it is queued, not awaited: a quit in that moment, or that write failing, lets the next start say "was removed". Decision 11: the client stamps a selection when it is made and writes the record about 150 ms later, so a selection made just before the user's switch back can land after the stamps were recorded and be handed (accepted, not built for). R2.2: a client that adds and deletes more than 1,000 rows evicts the oldest deleted classes, and a local-period row re-added under its old key after that is classed afresh and handed. A1's rule that Claude is immediate wins over R7.2's sentence in one case: a lone own-window request for Claude over a stored "local", with every disk fallback failing, leaves "local" on disk with Claude in effect (reported `persisted: false`; the next start reads the local model).
- **Review round 2 (2026-11-13).** R7.2's generated test checks the disk on its refused branch (a refused run passes only if both what is in effect and the disk are what they were), mixes own-window and allowance requests, issues requests after a write has landed and before its caller resumes, and checks the restart with the product's own read in every branch. Pinned outcome for the record: once the requests settle, the next start says chat is answered by Claude again only when that announcement was owed (an allowance switch to Claude found the user's choice on record, or a start-up read of it failed), or the last response said `persisted: false`. To hold it, a request for Claude re-checks the record inside the queue when it is the user's own or began with nothing chosen, a refused own switch to the local model clears a "chosen" the memory no longer shows but the file may still hold, and either clear failing makes the response say unsaved. A refused own switch puts back the mark only when the disk showed the user's marked Claude at the step's start: a program's unmarked Claude written since then is put back unmarked. A held message start is held before anything is stopped, looked up or armed.
- **Residual added in review round 2 — accepted (the review session's ruling, round 3, 2026-11-13: it is what the last request asked for, and milder than the residual A1 already accepted).** The latest request asks for the local model and cannot take effect — its record write fails and every way of putting Claude back fails too, or its own write fails and the read after it is refused (a locked file) — while "local" is on disk, from before the requests or from an earlier request's write that landed. Claude stays in effect, the response says `persisted: false`, and the next start reads the local model: the value the latest request asked for, silently when the record says the user chose it. Also accepted in round 3: when a program switches chat to Claude before the user's own choice of the local model has taken effect, whether the next start says chat is answered by Claude again depends on timing (whether the choice's record had landed when the program's switch read it) — both outcomes are true statements to the user.
- **A1's items closed here (R7).** The user's own switch to the local model now takes effect only once its record write succeeded; if it fails, the switch does not happen — Claude stays in effect and goes back on disk with the user's mark when the user's marked Claude was on disk or in effect (`persisted: false`). The record's change reaches memory, and the start-up or "answered by Claude again" notice it answers is withdrawn, only once the write lands, so a refused switch leaves nothing a later record write could persist; and once the requests settle the record says "chosen" only while the local model is in effect. A local write known to have landed whose read-back fails is taken as "local" on disk. The start-up seed of "the Claude in effect is the user's own" is removed (R7.5): a local request reads the mark on disk when its step begins. The re-assert is replaced: a superseded local request writes nothing, and the latest one reconciles toward its own request by reading the disk (a "local" there takes effect; anything else changes nothing), so no state about what superseded writes found is tracked; a property test over generated overlapping sequences with injected failures pins the outcome. Tandem's own removal of the file never leaves "was removed" said (R7.3): a link at the marker path counts; a failed rename empties the file in place instead — only a regular file with one name, re-checked on the open descriptor (same device and inode) because Windows has no `O_NOFOLLOW`; and when the rename, the emptying and the marker all fail while the delete works, the announced record carries `removedByTandem` instead. The marker is created without `O_TRUNC`, so a link planted at its path never has its target changed. **A1 residual (R5.5), added to A1's follow-ups:** an own-window switch to the local model over a newer build's file is unsaved, so the next boot reads Claude with no notice — a local period that ends unannounced.

**Amendment 2026-11-15 (who answers chat, part C: how a local-model run ends in chat; Bryan's decisions D-C1 and D-C2 of 2026-11-14, the review session's Rulings C-1 to C-10, built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2419, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged; flag off, none of this is reachable (the collaborator never subscribes).

- **D-C1 "Keep the sentence".** A model's lead-in text is removed ONLY where Tandem already tells the user the run stopped without replying or that its reply was not delivered. A run that finished normally and whose last turn was empty keeps the lead-in as its reply, as before, with no notice.
- **D-C2 "Hide the reasoning".** Chat shows only the answer: text inside `<think>` tags is never shown, during streaming or after (`src/server/local-model/think-filter.ts`, used by the sink). A reply that is only reasoning counts as no reply.
- **Ruling C-1.** The removal set is defined by the act: a run's rows are removed exactly where one of these toasts is actually posted — the three `editing_off` toasts ("…stopped without replying…", by Solo, mode unknown, the editing setting), the `doc-swapped` same-instance toast when its token test passes, and the `doc-swapped` new-instance toast ("…its reply was not delivered…"). An arm writing the same `localRun` reason without the toast removes nothing. `localRun` reasons are written exactly as before (no new values); at a removal site `replyId` is omitted. Rows are removed once, in `executeRun`'s `finally`, by `removeClaudeChatMessage` (`withMcp`), in one Y.Doc update with the run state.
- **Ruling C-2.** No change to the loop, to when `onTurnEnd` fires, or to `RunTurnOpts`.
- **Ruling C-3.** No change to minting or announcing: a removed row may already have been announced as a reply at its first flush (accepted).
- **Ruling C-4.** The no-reply notice ("Your local model produced no reply.", `chatNotice: "no-reply"`) is posted only when a run ended `clean` and no reply row was minted for it; never after a run that left a row, never at a removal site. It and the `localRun` write (`stopped`, `no-reply`) land in one transaction, synchronously in the run's `finally`, never through `postNotice`. If it is not posted (the switch moved to Claude, the licence refuses, the user's row is gone), nothing else is written in its place.
- **Ruling C-5.** Part C posts nothing for the other silent endings (a throw, an abort, the `!stillOwner()` arm, a `doc-swapped` arm without a toast, a run that never started); part B's pickup line covers them from `localRun`.
- **Ruling C-6.** Stripping `<think>`: a closed block is never shown; an unclosed `<think>` hides the rest of the turn only if it opens the turn (after leading whitespace and leading closed blocks); a later one is held until it closes or the turn ends, released as text only at a `clean` end and dropped everywhere else; blocks do not nest; the tags are exactly `<think>`/`</think>`, case-insensitive, no attributes; a lone `</think>` stays visible *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*; hidden text never counts toward the sink cap; per-delta work is proportional to the delta. Nothing the model is sent changes.
- **Ruling C-7.** A "preamble" is the text of a turn that ended in tool calls where tools were offered; the text-only turn after a refusal is the reply.
- **Ruling C-8.** The measurement harness judges shown text (`stalePreamble`, the claim check, the rubric, `classify`'s error-after-text); records a shown time to first text beside the raw one, the run's chat notices, and an orphan-`</think>` count, on new records only (old records load, report and keep their hash). `RUBRIC_VERSION` is `2026-11-15.1`, which stales every verdict file written before it. An unchanged fingerprint no longer means comparable runs across part C: the same model output now gives a different reply wherever `<think>` appears or a removal site is reached.
- **Ruling C-9.** A run cut by a server restart is unchanged (`interrupted`; a folded sidecar may stand as the row).
- **Ruling C-10.** A `clean` exit is a clean exit: if it left text, that is the reply; if it showed nothing, the no-reply notice. Treating a `clean` exit after a refused tool call as the refusal's ending was considered and not adopted.
- **Merged text this amends:** the "C" item of the 2026-11-12 A1 amendment (above); the harness amendment's owed inline-reasoning decision (above); `ChatNoticeKind` / `ChatMessage.chatNotice` and `LocalRunStopReason`'s `"no-reply"` ("finished cleanly and showed the user nothing, inline reasoning only included") in `src/shared/types.ts`; the doc-swapped comments in `executeRun`; `rubric.ts`'s reasoning paragraph, `signals.ts`'s `stalePreamble` and `trial.ts`'s `chatRowsFor` comment.
- **Owed items.** Closed: whether the product strips inline reasoning (it does, D-C2); the reasoning-only finding of #1123 comment 6861023348 is decided — a run that shows nothing gets the no-reply notice, and there is no retry. Added to what is owed by the flip: **whether any candidate model emits a closing `</think>` with no opening tag**, read from the harness's new `orphanCloseThink` signal in the desktop measurement. *(Superseded 2026-11-17: the current list is in the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*

**Amendment 2026-11-15 (who answers chat, part B: the editor; Bryan's Decisions B1–B7 of 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-15, the review session's Rulings B-1 to B-22, the drafter's Rulings B-a to B-d (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2431, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged; flag off, the Models tab is hidden, nothing new is fetched, and every renderer this part changed renders exactly as before — including for a row carrying `localRun` or `chatNotice` restored from a flag-on build, or a launcher status carrying `heldForLocalModel`. Part B has landed: of the two parts the flip-gate sentence above names, both are now built. The brief's restatement is the text of each decision and ruling; the summaries below are the record.

- **Decision B1.** The switch lives in Settings → Models only; the chat panel may say as text who is answering, but the switch is changed in Settings. *(See the 2026-11-18 amendment "Use it for chat".)*
- **Decision B2.** While a local model works on the user's chat message, the row under it says "Your local model is working on this…"; it does not name the model, never says "waiting for your AI to pick it up", and never offers Claude Code for a message a local model is handling.
- **Decision B3.** A local run that ends with no text for the user is said so in chat, never shows reasoning as the reply, never retries automatically. Narrowed by part C: C's notice for a `clean` run that showed nothing states the fact; B's pickup line ("Your local model stopped without answering.") says it for the other endings. Neither says "try again or switch to Claude".
- **Decision B4.** Right after the first local model is saved, one sentence, once: chat is still set to Claude, and where the switch is. Saving a first model does not switch chat to it. *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* *(See the 2026-11-18 amendment "Use it for chat".)*
- **Decision B5 ("New label").** While chat is set to the local model, the status pill's label says so, in every combination of pill states, and never promises delivery to Claude; out of that period the label is exactly today's; the three states that render nothing still render nothing; colours stay honest (Bryan, 2026-10-11).
- **Decision B6 ("Add the sentence").** Settings → Models says chat is set to the local model, plus "While chat is set to your local model, Tandem does not send Claude the chat messages you send, then or later." Chat only.
- **Decision B7 ("One line at the switch back").** At the user's own switch back to Claude, one line in chat, once per own switch back, only if user chat was sent in that local period; never handed to Claude, never a reply, kept in history. Bryan chose "Claude was not sent the chat from while your local model answered it."; the review session adopted a reviewer's rewording, "Claude was not sent the chat from while chat was set to your local model.", true even when the local model could not answer (shown to Bryan on the PR).
- **Ruling B-1.** The control: "Claude" / "Local model" under "Who answers your chat", on the radio-group pattern; arrows, Home and End move focus only; Space, Enter or a click selects; the shown position re-reads without a `POST`; a switch to the local model is only ever sent by the user's selection; pending is `aria-disabled`; outcome lines are `role="alert"`, the state line is not a live region.
- **Ruling B-2.** The `GET`'s 200 gains a closed-enum `localModelState` (`usable`, `none`, `no-default-chosen`, `endpoint-rejected`, `no-endpoint`), behind the same gates, absent when the check threw, with no name, URL, id, path or count.
- **Ruling B-3.** The editor reads the switch on events only (the Models tab opening, its own change, a notice, and R1.2's other triggers); a trigger during a read queues exactly one more; no polling; triggers never write state inside a reaction.
- **Ruling B-4.** A refused `GET` (a dev page with no allowance; a browser that sends no `Sec-Fetch-Site`) shows the control disabled with one true sentence, never duplicating the tab's own-window notice; "Set chat to Claude" is offered whenever the page is not foreign; a success there is said in the past tense.
- **Ruling B-5.** The help text is true with the switch (R4's text).
- **Ruling B-6.** The pickup row: the working line while running; one line for any stopped run with no reply row and no notice row; `interrupted` first; "Sent to your local model" only for a message this window sent while its read was known local, never under a licence refusal.
- **Ruling B-7.** The pill and the send-time toast are true in every state, Solo included.
- **Ruling B-8.** Notice rows render as notices: no Insert, no reply chime or "Claude replied", no OS notification, not exported as Claude's words; announced in the chat's live region; kept in history.
- **Ruling B-9.** A local reply's label is its `agentIdentity`; generic "your AI" texts stay.
- **Ruling B-10.** Decision B4's sentence is a toast at save time, once per install (localStorage, try-catch), only for the first local model, hooked on `useModels`' `addModel`; the first-run picker is not edited. *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* *(See the 2026-11-18 amendment "Use it for chat".)*
- **Ruling B-11.** Tests for every state of every new or changed surface; the testid snapshot regenerated with additions only; an E2E spec skipped while dark, with the allowance in `playwright.config.ts`; the two Svelte rules respected.
- **Ruling B-12.** B does not move the switch into the chat panel, name the model in the working row, flip the flag, or change `SKILL.md` or the MCP instructions.
- **Ruling B-13.** Endings that already have a toast get no extra chat notice; the pickup line is the bridge.
- **Ruling B-14.** The agent label's mismatch in states (a), (b), (c) above stays owed by the flip. *(Decided 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Ruling B-15.** The first-run picker is unchanged. *(Amended 2026-11-15 by Ruling L-g of the LM Studio amendment below, for the picker's provider rows, its address prefill, its subtitle, its display-name placeholder and blank-name fallback for Other, and its address hint only. The picker still offers no control that changes who answers chat (Decision B1).)* *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Ruling B-16.** The local period, for the editor: a known read always wins; `heldForLocalModel` counts only when the read is not known, through the running status's freshness gate.
- **Ruling B-17.** Two labels, "Chat: your local model" and "Your local model can't answer"; a thrown check is not "can't answer"; the `info` family, never `connected`'s green; Claude's real activity kept; the session count where today's view shows it.
- **Ruling B-18.** Every string says "your local model" and says what is SET; never that someone "answers" (headings and Bryan's own text excepted), never "until you choose Claude". Residuals to "never" go here, not in the UI.
- **Ruling B-19.** With a chip, the pill's title and accessible name begin with the visible label and the title sentence, then the chip's text; chip actions and `launcherRemedy` are unchanged.
- **Ruling B-20 (accepted).** On a refused or foreign page the local period is invisible unless Claude is held; a `POST` that times out may still land; the pill clips on narrow windows; a page whose read is refused cannot show a change made after its own "Set chat to Claude".
- **Ruling B-21 (not adopted).** "Starting Claude…" on the pill during the local period.
- **Ruling B-22.** Pill states that render nothing today (booting, `ready` with nothing attached and no launcher, Solo with nothing running) still render nothing.
- **Ruling B-a (drafter's).** Before a run starts the row says "Sent to your local model", not the working line.
- **Ruling B-b (drafter's).** A local reply's notification title is "Your local model replied" ("Your local model sent N replies"); a mixed batch reads "N new replies".
- **Ruling B-c (drafter's).** Notice rows count as unread and are exported under "Tandem".
- **Ruling B-d (drafter's).** The switch-back line is written by the server at the user's own switch back, as a step of A2's switch handler, through a hook wired in `src/server/local-period-wiring.ts` that calls `appendClaudeChatMessage` (`chatNotice: "claude-again"`, no `replyTo`, no `documentId`); under a licence refusal that append is refused and no line appears (accepted). The fact that user chat was sent is kept per period with A2's durable record as an optional `chat: true` (still `v: 1`; a record without it reads as no chat). It is a new caller of the chat choke point, recorded in `docs/licensing-explained.md`'s AI-surface inventory and `tests/docs/ai-surface-inventory-claims.test.ts`.
- **Part C corrections (R0, in the same PR).** Text that is only whitespace and Unicode format characters is not shown text (the sink's emptiness check and the harness's); leading is judged per code point; the think filter's cap is a required argument, pinned through the shipped sink; a removal the remover refuses keeps `localRun.replyId`; the cost tests gained fast twins; the 80th-character flush and the abort spy have positive pins; the part C fixtures' capture is recorded and reproduced at b57d850.
- **Residuals to "never" (Ruling B-18), recorded here rather than in the UI.** Decision B6's "Tandem does not send Claude the chat messages you send, then or later" holds for this build: a dark or older build hands local-period chat to Claude (A2 R2.5), and a Claude session's own file access can read the CTRL session file (A2 R1.4). A failed or timed-out re-read ends a known read, so with nothing held (the user's own Claude Code, a Claude the user stopped, start mode Off) the pill falls back to today's label until the next read answers (accepted as the Terms define the read; a "known but stale" read is a proposal for Bryan on the PR) *(Decided 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*. A reconnect to the same server process is not a "connect" trigger (the read is re-taken at a new process only).
- **Merged text this amends:** this ADR's owed-by-the-flip bullet (the "until B" clause, the perf-config clause and the Models specs' allowance, marked done), "Owed by B, beyond the control itself" (each item's closure), A2's review round 1 (the pill's `awaiting-start` copy), A1's chat-notices bullet (the byline); in code, `CHAT_NOTICE_TEXT`'s docblock, `localModelResolvesNow`'s docblock, the `GET` handler's doc, `modelsWiring`'s type, `ChatAnswererResponse`'s doc, `ChatMessage.chatNotice` and `ChatNoticeKind`, the Models tab's help text, `chat-pickup.ts`'s header, `tandemClaudeView`'s docblock, `addressed-ai-notice.ts`'s header, and `DEFAULT_RUN_DEADLINE_MS` (moved to `src/shared/local-run.ts`, re-exported by `loop.ts`). The substring the flip-gate test requires is unchanged.

**Amendment 2026-11-15 (LM Studio and "another local server" as local-model choices; Bryan's Decisions L1–L3 of 2026-11-15, the review session's Rulings L-1 to L-5, the drafter's Rulings L-a to L-h (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2433, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged and nothing a dark build shows changes, except the agent label read from a hand-edited browser registry (Accepted, below; recorded 2026-11-17). Two provider values, `local-lmstudio` and `local-other`, are appended to the contract's lists; a saved entry of either is listed, labelled, resolved and called exactly as an Ollama entry is, because every local entry resolves to the same `v1` transport and the provider never reaches the wire.

- **Decision L1.** Bryan asked: "can we allow a user to use any local model they want through ollama, huggingface, or lmstudio?" Asked whether to add an LM Studio entry with its default port, and copy saying any OpenAI-compatible local server works and that models vary, he said "yes". Tandem does not download from Hugging Face and does not call hosted APIs; addresses stay loopback-only.
- **Decision L2.** A generic choice, "Other local server (OpenAI-compatible)", with no prefilled address, so models added that way are labelled truthfully.
- **Decision L3.** When Tandem calls the server it drops a trailing `/v1` from the saved address, and the address hint says so.
- **Ruling L-1. Ports from a primary source only.** As built: Ollama keeps port 11434; LM Studio's prefill is `http://127.0.0.1:1234`, from the address its own documentation uses (`lmstudio.ai/docs/developer/openai-compat`: `http://localhost:1234/v1`); **llama.cpp gets no prefill**, because its own source disagrees across releases (`common/common.h` sets the server's default port to 8080 at tags b6000 through b11000 and to 9931 at b12000 and on master, and master's README option table says 9931 while its docker examples still use 8080), so no single address is its default. The builder's call, for the review session to confirm. *(Decided 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Ruling L-2.** LM Studio's optional API token is not supported: no `Authorization` header is sent.
- **Ruling L-3.** An LM Studio screen is owed before the flip (owed-by-the-flip bullet above). Nothing here claims LM Studio works. *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Ruling L-4.** One concern: #2433's carried items are not in this change, and no E2E test is added.
- **Ruling L-5.** No new colour tokens: LM Studio and Other use llama.cpp's `--tandem-agent-local-llamacpp`.
- **Ruling L-a.** The values are `local-lmstudio` and `local-other`, appended; the default provider stays Ollama. The label map, the address map and the picker's per-provider maps are `Record<ModelProvider, …>`, and every list (the Models tab's group order, the edit modal's and the picker's options) is derived from `VALID_MODEL_PROVIDERS` / `LOCAL_MODEL_PROVIDERS` (`src/shared/models/provider-ui.ts`), because the Models tab's hand-written group order silently left out an entry of any provider it missed.
- **Ruling L-b.** Prefills use `127.0.0.1`, so the hint's example and the connected address agree; the picker's Ollama prefill changed from `localhost` to `127.0.0.1`, which connects to the same address.
- **Ruling L-c.** The `/v1` strip lives in `baseUrl` (`ollama-client.ts`), after validation and after the trailing-slash trim, for both transports: exactly `/v1`, case-sensitive, once, and only when the `/` before it is not itself preceded by `/`. The saved address and `validateEndpoint` are unchanged.
- **Ruling L-d.** Where the copy goes: the two lines on what can be used and what Tandem sends once in the Models tab, between "Who answers your chat" and the model list, in every list state; the same two lines and the address hint in the edit modal while a local provider is selected; the hint and the local subtitle in the first-run picker.
- **Ruling L-e.** The wording, within the brief's constraints: true in every state it can appear in, "your local model", never that a model "answers", no promise of quality, no model named. One refinement from review: the line on what Tandem sends says the document's text is included when the document is short, and that the comment and suggestion tools are offered only in Tandem mode and only if allowed, because a run that starts in Solo is chat-tier. Residual to Ruling B-18: after a refused writing call the model's next turn is offered no tools, so "it includes … tools for reading your document" does not hold for that one turn; and "in Tandem mode" stands for the full rule (the run starts in Tandem and never leaves it, and an unknown mode refuses), which the UI does not spell out.
- **Ruling L-f.** The edit modal keeps an empty address for a new entry with the selected provider's prefill as its placeholder; only the first-run picker prefills, and on a provider switch it replaces the address only when it equals the previous provider's prefill (string equality, `""` for a provider with none). *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Ruling L-g.** Ruling B-15 is amended for this change only (marked there).
- **Ruling L-h.** The picker's Display name placeholder and blank-name fallback for Other are "Local server".
- **Accepted.** A `models.json` holding a new value is not understood by 0.31 or older (it backs the file up and shows no models; its next write replaces the file), and such a build drops annotation and reply rows bylined by those providers, a loss its next snapshot makes permanent; a dark build can write these values only through `POST /api/models`, which answers while dark (`src/server/models/api-routes.ts`): the one-time reconcile of a hand-edited browser registry posts through it, as can any request its own-window test admits (Tandem's own window, or the development allowance's origin when set) *(amended 2026-11-17 by the test-hardening amendment below: this sentence said "only through the one-time reconcile")*. `agentLabelSource()` reads the browser registry while dark (`useModels.svelte.ts`), and that registry's parser now accepts the new values, so a hand-edited registry can change the Toolbar's "Send to" label and the annotation aria-label in a dark build. Three providers share a colour. The picker's string-equality rule refills a field cleared by hand under a provider with no prefill on the next switch to one that has one, and replaces a typed address that equals a prefill. Adding or removing `/v1` from a saved address reads as a changed model once. A LAN address still saves silently and is refused when used. *(Amended 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* An LM Studio server with authentication on cannot be used. Nothing here is measured against LM Studio.
- **Merged text this amends:** Ruling B-15 (above); this ADR's owed-by-the-flip bullet (three rows); in code, `resolveLocalModel`'s transport comment (`config-source.ts`), `baseUrl`'s doc comment (`ollama-client.ts`), the edit modal's cloud note *(removed 2026-11-19: see the amendment on cloud providers hidden)* and the wizard's flag-on AI-models row, `ModelRegistryEntry.endpoint`'s comment (`useTandemSettings.ts`), and `UNUSABLE_NOTICE["endpoint-rejected"]`, whose example address no longer names Ollama's port ("such as http://127.0.0.1 followed by the server's port").

**Results note 2026-11-15 (the desktop measurement, made 2026-11-13; a record, nothing decided here).** Full write-up: [the spike's results section](spikes/local-llm-capability-spike.md#results-of-the-new-measurement), which is the published result; #1123 comment 6861023348 is the handover written before the run and publishes no result. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged. The 2026-11-17 amendment on switching local models on (below) makes these results a record, not a gate.

- **Outcome: no model passed the chat tier.** qwen3.5:9b, gemma4:12b and granite4.2:8b were screened on a GPU desktop (RTX 3070 8 GB, 32 GB RAM, Ollama 0.40.16), not the CPU-only laptop the harness amendment named. The full run of the highest-scoring of the three screens, gemma4:12b, failed: questions the document answers 24/24, questions it does not answer 21/22, requests only the editing tier can carry out **10/22 (45%)**. It had no false claim. Ten of the twelve failures in that group fail on the quote rule alone, so the FAIL rests on that rule.
- **The editing tier is reported, not passed:** 39/41 for gemma4:12b. That is the scorer's rate with the reader's false-claim verdicts; no false claim was recorded.
- **The 14B floor (the harness amendment's "whatever that measurement supports"):** no model passed, so there is no model and size to name. The measurement neither confirms nor replaces the June ≥14B guidance, and no model of 14B or larger was measured. Tandem now recommends no model or size (P2 of the 2026-11-17 amendment on switching local models on).
- **Measured before part C.** The runs were made at `42f64393`, before part C, and read under rubric `2026-11-13.5`. They were re-stamped to `2026-11-15.1` without a re-read, because no trial record holds a `<think>` tag; that is the per-record check Ruling C-8 makes necessary. For gemma4:12b's recorded runs, part C changes nothing the user is shown. The fingerprint `d4fe758ee095440befd726c66e14b80b` is recorded as published.
- **Measurement question closed: an orphan `</think>`.** None was found in any trial of the three models. The answer comes from searching the records with the signal's own pattern, because these records predate the signal. It holds behind Ollama 0.40.16, which delivered every model's reasoning in a separate field. This closes only part C's measurement question (whether any candidate emits the tag); the product's handling of a lone closing tag is item 26d of the 2026-11-17 amendment on switching local models on (V12). *(See the 2026-11-19 amendment on clearer failure messages: 26d is accepted for 0.32.)*

**Amendment 2026-11-17 (the send-time toast waits for the send's own chat-answerer read, and the App wiring pin is hardened; #2433's carried M1 and M2, the review session's rulings and the drafter's Rulings S-a to S-e (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2437, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged and nothing a dark build shows or does changes: while dark `noteChatSent` records nothing, so the handler's one addition is a synchronous `takeSendRead()` that returns null, the wait's condition is false, and its only await is still the probe. `chat-answerer-store-dark.test.ts` pins that the take returns null while dark; `app-chat-answerer-wiring.test.ts` pins that no await happens without a record (the record check sits in the wait's condition, and the handler has exactly two awaits).

- **What changed.** For a chat send that requested a chat-answerer read, a `no-agent` or `no-push` toast is decided after that read settles, or at one `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (now exported) from the take, whichever comes first. Every other case is decided as before, with no added wait. `noteChatSent` records the send's own read and `inLocalPeriod(held)` at the send; App's `handleAddressedAi` calls `takeSendRead()` after the Solo return and the fast path, before the probe, for a chat only; after the probe, only when today's notice is `no-agent` or `no-push` and a record was taken, it awaits `settleSendLocalPeriod` and computes the notice again. The local period after the wait is live (the store's answer is local) when the read is `known` then, otherwise the value recorded at the send. The settle never rejects; the take attaches a catch at once.
- **Review session's rulings.** The smallest fix: no extraction of `handleAddressedAi` and no moved pins; the helper in the store; the export of `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`; the wait created at the take; the take after the Solo return and the fast path, returning nothing when no read was requested; the record as a plain variable; no re-probe; a test-only setter and a catch at the take and at the settle; the record check inside the `no-agent`/`no-push` condition; the local-period rule above; the flag-off path with no added await; the markup-comment strip by an `indexOf` loop with strip-to-end on an unclosed opener; scoped and anchored pins; a test of the first-run picker's flag gate.
- **Ruling S-a.** The record is the last send's, replaced by every `noteChatSent` call (with nothing when the send requested no read) and cleared by the take, not keyed by message id: the send, its dispatch and the handler's take run in one synchronous frame.
- **Ruling S-b.** After the wait the notice is computed again with no re-probe: the probe's `answered` and `sessionLive` are reused; every other input is re-read.
- **Ruling S-c.** The local period at the send is computed inside `noteChatSent` from its `held` argument, which App passes from the same `aiReadiness.launcherHeldForLocalModel` that `localPeriodNow` reads.
- **Ruling S-d.** The cap timer is not cleared at the effect's teardown; when it fires it only resolves a promise.
- **Ruling S-e.** No mount test of App (129 imports and a live Hocuspocus provider). The pins in `tests/client/app-chat-answerer-wiring.test.ts` read comment-stripped text (`tests/helpers/app-source.ts`) and list what a text pin cannot see in that file's header.
- **Accepted.** A chat's `no-agent` or `no-push` toast can arrive later than before, by up to one `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, when the send requested a read. The toast's session claim dates from the probe, up to the cap before it is shown; the second computation cannot add a toast the first would not show. It can show offline or ai-off up to the cap late, where the server or the licence changed during the wait. After the wait the local period is live whenever the store's read is known at that moment, and that read can be another trigger's rather than this send's (at the cap, or when this send's answer was not adopted); otherwise it is the value recorded at the send. So a read queued behind a hung read, with the read not known and nothing held at the send, can still give "no AI is connected" beside a local pickup row; an answerer change during the wait that leaves the read not known also falls back to the recorded value. A read that answers known is discarded when a switch POST settled or a new server process was detected while it was in flight, or when a switch POST is still on the wire as it returns; the send then falls back to the recorded value only if the store's read is not known at that moment, and otherwise takes the store's last known answer. The read's own fetch timeout and the cap give the same answer whichever fires first only for a read that was not queued; a queued read's timeout runs from its own later start, so the cap comes first. The recorded value can outlive a change in `held` during the wait (a toast withheld after the local period ended; minor). The read is a proxy: the pickup row's "working" comes from the message's server stamp (`effectiveLocalRun`), so the toast and the row can still disagree where the read and the stamp do. Comment sends never wait. The cap timer can outlive App's effect by up to 10 s and writes nothing. The App wiring pin is text, and its limits are written in its header.

**Amendment 2026-11-17 (local-model test hardening and small fixes carried from the reviews of #2433 and #2437; the review session's rulings and the drafter's Rulings H-1 to H-3 (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2437, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged; every surface this changes is unmounted or inert while it is off (the edit modal mounts only from the Models tab, which `SettingsModal.svelte` filters out while dark, pinned by `tests/client/local-providers-dark.test.ts`; the picker never mounts while dark).

- **The review session's rulings.** The carried items are split into two PRs (this one, and the send-time toast). The stopped pickup row after a switch back and the reopened-tab "Chat was set to Claude." line are not built: they are owed rows (the owed-by-the-flip bullet above, with their reasons). *(Superseded 2026-11-17: see the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)* The edit modal's provider select is `value` plus an `onchange` that sets the provider from the select's own value and does nothing else, instead of `bind:value` (happy-dom cannot drive `bind:value` on a select, so no test could switch the provider). "Checking…" and "Chat was set to Claude." take `LiveRegion`'s announcer shape in the gapped description column, with an `aria-hidden` visible copy only while there is a sentence. The edit modal's S1/S2 block and S3 hint and the picker's S3 hint use `--tandem-fg-muted`, as the Models tab does for S1 and S2 (`.mem-close`, `.mem-advanced-help` and `.mem-provider-note` keep `--tandem-fg-subtle`). *(The `.mem-provider-note` rule was removed 2026-11-19: see the amendment on cloud providers hidden.)* A client-origin chat write in a local period is tested with the client-write undo wired, kept for the editor's own row and undone for a server-only field, a fixed field's other value or a non-user row. Full literal lists replace the product's own maps in the provider tests; the resolver's per-provider loops compare the resolver's own answer and the resolved provider. The live-config fetch stub passes only the test's own route server, records the one model URL, and refuses and lists every other URL, the list asserted empty. An Enter step on the chat-answerer radios is added to the E2E spec. Six owed rows, each with its closing condition. The LM Studio amendment's Accepted bullet names `POST /api/models` as the dark write path and records `agentLabelSource()`.
- **Ruling H-1 (drafter's).** *(Retired 2026-11-19: see the amendment on cloud providers hidden.)* The modal's tests drive an existing cloud entry back to its cloud value although no user can select a disabled option, because it is the only way a test sees S1/S2 removed on a provider change.
- **Ruling H-2 (drafter's).** The picker's bare `change` event pins that its handler runs on `change`, and nothing more: keyboard selection on the picker's radios in a real browser is unverified.
- **Ruling H-3 (drafter's).** The two live regions and the hint colour are built rather than recorded, because each applies a rule the code already states (`LiveRegion.svelte`'s header; the Models tab's token for the same sentences).
- **Accepted.** The hints and the modal's S1/S2 change colour: slightly more contrast in light, warm and dark; the body colour under the app's high-contrast setting; `CanvasText` instead of `GrayText` under forced colours. Keyboard selection on the picker's radios is unverified in a real browser. The five owed rows above wait for Bryan's decision before the flip; the Enter row closes when its step runs green at the flip. *(Superseded 2026-11-17: the current list is in the amendment of that date on switching local models on.)*
- **Dropped.** #2433's note that Svelte was given as the reason `runGeneration` is cleared on a microtask (the code comment already gives the brief's rule; this ADR says nothing about it); #2433's stale Models E2E note (already the owed row "the stale Models E2E spec"); #2437's all-empty prefill note (every local prefill is pinned literally by `model-edit-modal-local-providers.test.ts`); the hand-written provider list in `collaborator-live-config.test.ts` (it already loops over `LOCAL_MODEL_PROVIDERS`); #2437 M3 (it stays tied to the LM Studio screen row).
- **Merged text this amends:** the LM Studio amendment's opening sentence and its Accepted bullet (above); this ADR's owed-by-the-flip bullet (six rows).
**Amendment 2026-11-17 (switching local models on; Bryan's decisions and the review session's readings and rulings, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged and nothing a shipped build does changes. This amendment records Bryan's decisions of 2026-11-17 on switching local models on and the review session's readings and rulings on them, and gives the current list of what is owed before and by the flip. Its four parts: (a) the record, (b) the readings and rulings, (c) what is owed before the flip, (d) what is owed in the flip change itself. Each earlier passage it supersedes or decides carries a pointer to it; the pointers add nothing of their own.

**(a) The record.** Bryan's typed sentences, 2026-11-17, in order:
- **S1.** "hide the named LM Studio choice and keep "Other local server"" (asked whether to change the measurement tool and run it against LM Studio on his desktop, or hide the named choice). **Reversed later the same day by P1, which stands.**
- **S2.** "a good rewrite that doesn't quote the original is ok" (shown the rubric's quote rule and that it decided #2434's chat-tier result: gemma4:12b scored answerable 24/24, unanswerable 21/22, editing-request 10/22, ten of the twelve failures on the quote rule alone, at most 20/22 with it waived).
- **S3.** "we don't need to rescore, we just need to let users use whatever model they want that is local" (told that the editing-request verdicts would have to be re-scored under S2 before any model could count as a pass, and that the run data was only on his desktop).

His picks, each picked from options (label, then the option's description exactly as shown to him):
- **P1, LM Studio:** "Keep LM Studio named (Recommended)": "Leave what is merged: Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio and "Other local server" are all offered, with copy that says quality depends on the model you choose. The hide-LM-Studio PR is dropped."
- **P2, model wording:** "No recommendation (Recommended)": "Remove "≥14B recommended". Say: use any local model; results depend on the model, and smaller models answer less reliably. The measurement results stay published as a record, not as advice."
- **P3, the first-run window:** "New installs only": "People updating from 0.31 never see it; brand-new users see it once after setting up Claude, reworded to say it is optional. Needs a way to tell a new install from an update."
- **P4, the cloud rows:** "Hide until they work (Recommended)": "Only local choices are shown. No public promise in the product; cloud keys can be announced whenever you decide."
- **P5, after the first local model:** "Offer one click (Recommended)": "The confirmation has a "Use it for chat" button. Chat stays with Claude unless they press it. Small change." *(See the 2026-11-18 amendment "Use it for chat".)*
- **P6, failures:** "Clearer messages (Recommended)": "Each failure names the likely cause and the fix (start the server; check the model name; this model may not support tools, try another; or set chat back to Claude). Shown when the first message fails. Small change."
- **P7, the eight provisional defaults:** "Confirm all eight (Recommended)": "Nothing to build. Item 7 can be revisited later if the extra Claude start bothers you." The eight as put to him: (1) one "Let local models comment and suggest changes" switch covers every local model; (2) with it off a local model can only chat; (3) the switch lives in Settings, Models; (4) if the Default model was deleted, Tandem says "no default" and asks you to mark one; (5) the switch shows even before any model is saved and can always be turned off; (6) "Your local model can't be used" is said once per problem until Tandem restarts; (7) when you switch chat back to Claude, Tandem's own Claude may start for a message the local model already handled, find nothing to do, and so use a little of your Claude usage; (8) if Tandem briefly cannot read the chat setting, the status indicator shows its usual Claude wording.
- **P8, LM Studio check:** "Try it on this laptop (Recommended)": "A functional check only, not a quality screen: one chat reply and one comment with a small model, about an hour. If it works it stays named; if not, it is hidden and people use "Other local server". I install LM Studio here with your OK."
- **P9, #2410:** "Re-confirm + close dev door (Recommended)": "Accept it live, and make release builds ignore the developer-only setting that lets one extra local address change these settings. Small change; no effect on normal use." Not picked: accepting only the exact desktop-window address each operating system uses; remembering which program saved each model.
- **P10, six items the ADR reserved to him:** "Confirm all six (Recommended)": "1 and 5 get built; 2, 3, 4 and 6 stay as they are for 0.32 and are recorded as accepted." The six as put to him: (1) Claude's own replies and comments are always labelled "Claude"; a local model's name appears only on what it wrote; (2) after you switch chat back to Claude, a message the local model failed on keeps saying "stopped without answering" even if Claude then acknowledges it; (3) "Chat was set to Claude." may show again when Settings is reopened after a slow switch; (4) nothing is read aloud by a screen reader when the provider is changed in the add-model window; (5) the add-model window fills in each provider's usual address and says why Save is greyed out; (6) the add-model window keeps its explanatory small print (about 650 characters).
- **P11, announcement:** "Release notes only (Recommended)": "One paragraph in the changelog, which opens automatically after an update: any model on your computer, results depend on the model, chat stays with Claude until you choose, editing is opt-in."

**P7 maps onto this ADR's open passages** (Bryan's picks, not readings): P7(1) to (6) confirm, in substance, the provisional (1) to (6) of the 2026-11-11 amendment (its "Provisional, not decided" bullet); P7(7) confirms what part A2's "Review round 1" bullet left "(round 2 item 10; open for Bryan)"; P7(8) confirms the status indicator's fallback to its usual Claude wording in part B's "Residuals to "never"" bullet; the "known but stale" read proposed there is not adopted. Nothing else in that bullet is confirmed by it.

**(b) Readings and rulings by the review session, each open to Bryan's change.**
- **V1. No measurement gates the flip** (reading of S3 with P2). The pass rule of 2026-11-12 is no longer a release condition, including its condition that no chat-tier reply falsely claims an action on the document. The gate had been applied: in the desktop measurement written up in PR #2434 (open on this date), no model passed the chat tier under the rule as written; gemma4:12b scored 24/24, 21/22 and 10/22 on the three gating groups; no screen and neither full run recorded a false claim. The harness stays as a tool and gates nothing. Under P2 the June numbers (the capability spike) and the 2026-11 desktop measurement's are to be kept as a record, not as advice; on this date the desktop results are published only in an open pull request, not yet on master (item 33). #1123's M4 tested-model-floor item and its named-model exit criterion are superseded; the roadmap's criterion reads "with a local model".
- **V2. P10(5) for providers with no usual address.** Ruling L-1 gives llama.cpp no prefill and Decision L2 gives "Other local server" none. So a provider with a usual address gets it filled in (Ollama; LM Studio, from L-1); llama.cpp and Other get none, and Save says why it is greyed out. This replaces only Ruling L-f's edit-modal half; L-1, L2 and the first-run picker's string-equality rule for switching providers (L-f's second half, as the LM Studio amendment's Accepted bullet describes it) stand.
- **V3. P10(1) extends to the controls.** The "Send to" control labels and the annotation aria-labels follow P10(1), because the bug is one mechanism (`activeEntry` in `src/client/utils/agentLabel.ts`).
- **V4. The blanking half of the stopped row is accepted for 0.32.** Claude's `inProgress` acknowledgement for a message whose local run stopped blanks the row instead of showing "Your AI is working on it". P10(2) as shown to him described only the other half (a `settles` reply), so this half was shown to him inaccurately.
- **V5. Threads it started: accepted for 0.32.** With editing on, a local model can reply only in threads it started in the same reply, so it cannot reply in a thread the user started. P7(2) covers editing off; this limit was never put to him.
- **V6. The two `reply_to_annotation` refusals stay different** (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a personal note or private comment, `NOT_FOUND` for an unknown id), as `annotations/lifecycle.ts` documents.
- **V7. The first-run picker's conditions** (from the UX review): it never opens over another dialog, and it does not flash while the model registry loads.
- **V8.** The first-run picker's prefilled model ids (`llama3.1:70b` for Ollama, `local-model` for llama.cpp) go; the field starts empty with a hint that names no model. Changes Rulings B-10 and B-15.
- **V9.** An address not on this computer is refused at Save with the reason shown (a client-side check; `src/server/models/schema.ts`'s stored-file rule and the server's inertness for such an entry are unchanged). Reverses the LM Studio amendment's Accepted sentence "A LAN address still saves silently and is refused when used."
- **V10.** The edit modal's "Advanced parameters ... not editable in this preview ... Wave 6" text is removed.
- **V11.** One sentence in the add-model window says the document's text is sent only to the address the user entered. This changes P10(6), which kept the small print as it is.
- **V12.** A lone closing `</think>` is handled so reasoning is not shown. Reverses Ruling C-6's "a lone `</think>` stays visible". This is the product's handling of the tag; the separate measurement item (part C's "whether any candidate model emits a closing `</think>` with no opening tag") closes when the desktop write-up is on master (item 33), not by V12. *(See the 2026-11-19 amendment on clearer failure messages: item 26d, this handling, is accepted for 0.32 and not built.)*
- **V13. P10(4) also covers the first-run picker.** The picker announces nothing when the provider changes either; P10(4) was shown to him for the add-model window only. The same answer (accepted for 0.32) is applied to the picker.
- **V14. P10(5) also covers the first-run picker.** The picker leaves llama.cpp's address empty and Save disabled with no stated reason; P10(5) was shown to him for the add-model window only. The same answer is applied to the picker. The picker already prefills Ollama and LM Studio (Ruling L-g), so the only new picker work is Save's stated reason.
- **V15. The E2E harness keeps the development allowance.** P9 said release builds ignore it, with no exception; CI's E2E backend is the built server a release ships and relies on it, so the E2E harness's run keeps it, told apart by a fact the change that builds it chooses and tests.
- **V16. `npm run dev:tauri` getting no allowance by itself** is a developer note, not a release condition, and stays in this ADR.
- **V17. S2 is recorded; the measurement rubric (`rubric.ts`) is not changed**, because under V1 the harness gates nothing. The write-up's two open rubric questions (the "were." period fold; comment-request handling) are left as it states them.
- **V18. Display name is optional in the add-model window**, with the first-run picker's fallback (`ModelEditModal.svelte`'s `canSave` requires `displayName` today). Part of item 22's closing condition. (It was in the flip plan and fell out of a later draft.)

- The local period's status-line sentence in `skills/tandem/SKILL.md`, with its version bumped (part A2's R6).
- The sentence in the 2026-11-11 amendment's owed-by-the-flip bullet that `tests/docs/local-model-flip-gate.test.ts` pins: deleted when the flag flips, which that test requires.

**Merged text this amends:** the status line at the top of this ADR (reworded); the June shipping decision's parameter-floor guidance and its re-test-gate sentence; the 2026-11-11 amendment's "Supersessions inside this ADR" bullet, its harness bullet, its owed-by-the-flip bullet and its "Provisional, not decided" bullet; the 2026-11-12 harness amendment's pass-rule, "Every reply is read", models, 14B-floor and "Owed by the flip, updated" bullets; part A2's "Review round 1" bullet; part C's Ruling C-6 and its "Owed items" bullet; part B's Decision B4, Rulings B-10, B-14 and B-15 and its "Residuals to "never"" bullet; the LM Studio amendment's Rulings L-1, L-3 and L-f and its Accepted bullet; the test-hardening amendment's rulings bullet and its Accepted bullet.

**Note 2026-11-17 (the first-run window, built in #1123's PR 4a part 1, still dark).** Bryan picked from options, on 2026-11-17, how P3 ("New installs only") is built. **W1:** "Cheaper: tied to the setup wizard": "No server change, about 450 to 650 lines, client only. Accepts the three edge cases above; a tracked issue is filed to revisit the v1.0 walkthrough case before v1.0." Not picked: "Exact: marker file and route"; "Never pops up". The three edges, as shown to him: (1) someone updating from 0.31 who never finished setting up Claude would see it once; (2) a new user who set Claude up from the command line before opening Tandem would never see it; (3) at v1.0, when everyone gets the walkthrough, new installs would not be offered it unless more is built then. W1 amends P3 at those three edges only.
- **Built.** The window ("Add a local model (optional)") is offered at most once per page, decided once when the setup wizard closes: when Claude Code or Claude Desktop was applied on its Done screen, it had opened by itself because setup was needed and was not taken over by hand or the v1.0 walkthrough, the model list has loaded and holds no model, no other dialog is open, it was never dismissed, and the page is Tandem's own window. Once shown it stays until its own close, and nothing opens over it. It lists local providers only, prefills no model id, and Save says why it cannot save. **Item 21 is closed as amended by W1**; the first-run window's halves of **items 22 and 23** are done; the add-model window's halves stay owed.
- **The drafter's rulings, built as written (unconfirmed until Bryan confirms or changes them):** RW-1 any saved model, not only a default, means the window is not shown; RW-2 nothing opens over the window; RW-3 the copy; RW-4 "Try again" after an apply means the close counts as not having set Claude up; RW-5 a failed default after a committed add closes the window as a success, clearing the store's error *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the title chip)*; RW-6 `tandem:modelPickerDismissed` and the E2E harness's seeded key stay; RW-7 the connect-time reload of the model list is its own continuation; RW-8 the palette gets a separate guard line for the window.
- **Accepted by the review session (not shown to Bryan; open to his change):** a slow or failed model list when the wizard closes means no window in that page; "Try again" after an apply means no window; applying, then quitting or reloading before closing Done offers nothing; with two tabs only the first to close can show it; a reload while it is open loses it; a storage-disabled browser keeps its dismissal in memory only; the tray's setup item can open the wizard beside it, and the empty state's direct file-open is not guarded; on close, focus returns nowhere if nothing was focused before; V13.
- **The v1.0 walkthrough case (W1 edge 3)** is tracked by #2453.

**Amendment 2026-11-17 (agent labels; Label decisions L1 to L6 of 2026-11-17, the review session's rulings and the drafter's Rulings AL-0, AL-1, AL-3 and AL-7 (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2450, still dark).** `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the only dark difference is that a record whose `agentIdentity` has no usable name (empty, missing or not a string) reads "Local model", and a dark build never writes `agentIdentity`.

- **The decisions.** P10(1) and V3 are as recorded in the amendment above on switching local models on. Label decisions L1 to L6 of 2026-11-17 were each picked from options the reviewing session wrote on 2026-11-17; the label and description quoted are the option's text.
  - **Label decision L1.** Asked after he was told that the no-model label today is "Assistant" and that Tandem cannot tell Claude from any other AI connected through MCP (Codex, for example), so all of them would read "Claude": what should the label be for work that did not come from a local model?
