Picked: ""Claude" unless told otherwise": ""Claude" by default; if the connected client names itself as something else, use "Assistant". More to build and test; the client's self-reported name is not always present." Not picked: ""Claude" (as you picked)"; ""Assistant"".
  - **Label decision L2**, the title bar's "Default model: <name>" chip. Picked: "Only while chat uses it (Recommended)": "Shown only while chat is set to the local model, so the title bar tells you who is answering. Hidden while chat is with Claude." Not picked: "Hide it"; "Leave as it is".
  - **Label decision L3**, the comment filter's "Claude" chip, which also matches local-model comments. Picked: ""AI" (Recommended)": "One chip for comments from any AI, named honestly. Each comment still shows who wrote it." Not picked: "Keep "Claude""; "Separate chips".
  - **Label decision L4**, when to build L1's "unless told otherwise" (he was told it needs the server to stamp every AI-written comment and chat row, a new stored field, and schema changes). Picked: "After 0.32 (Recommended)": "0.32 ships with plain "Claude" for anything not written by a local model (a different MCP client would read "Claude" too). The "unless told otherwise" part is tracked as an issue for a later release." Not picked: "Before the flip, in 0.32"; "Drop it".
  - **Label decision L5**, if it is built. Picked: "The AI that wrote it (Recommended)": "Each new comment is stamped when written. Comments written before the change keep reading "Claude". Labels never change after the fact."
  - **Label decision L6**, the chip's source. Picked: "First model becomes the default (Recommended)": "When you save your first local model it is marked as the default automatically, so the chip (and anything else that reads the default) works. You can change the default later." Not picked: "Chip names whoever answers"; "Leave it hidden".
- **What this PR builds.** With the flag on, every surface that labels Claude-side work (a record or control that came through MCP, from any MCP client) reads "Claude", whatever the Models store holds and whoever answers chat: the chat, reply and card bylines, the card's "working" indicator, the editor's comment aria-label, the status bar's working pill, the composer's strings and the card and batch "Send to" controls. The rule lives in `createAgentLabel` (`src/client/hooks/useAgentLabel.svelte.ts`) and in `readAgentFamilyLabel` (`src/client/editor/extensions/annotation.ts`); with the flag off both keep the earlier registry rule (`resolveAgentLabel` over `agentLabelSource()`, unchanged). A record a local model wrote reads its stamped `agentIdentity.displayName`, and "Local model" when the identity has no usable name (empty, missing or not a string), in both flag states (`agentBylineLabel`, `src/client/utils/agentLabel.ts`, used at the three byline sites and the editor aria-label). With the flag on, the comment filter's agent chip and its summary read "AI" (L3); the chip's value, predicate and coral pip are unchanged. Tests: `tests/client/agent-label-lit.svelte.test.ts`, `agent-label-surfaces-lit.test.ts`, `agent-label-decoration-lit.test.ts` (lit), and `agent-label-surfaces-dark.test.ts`, `agent-label-decoration-dark.test.ts` (dark, literal expected strings).
- **Closed and standing.** The agent-label clause of the 2026-11-11 amendment's owed-by-the-flip bullet (states (a) to (c)) and Ruling B-14 are closed while the flag is on, because the label no longer reads the registry there; they stand while it is off, where the registry rule is kept on purpose.
- **The review session's rulings.** Part (b) (L1's "unless told otherwise") is split off; the lit-only placement in `createAgentLabel` and `readAgentFamilyLabel`; the empty-name rule has no flag (worded here as an identity without a usable name, review round 1); V3 reaches every "Send to" control; "the chip and the default" and "the send notice" are later PRs before the flip *(amended 2026-11-18: "the chip and the default" is three PRs, the automatic default's server half, its client half and the chip; see the amendment of that date on the automatic default)*; the flag-neutral test shape of #2449.
- **The drafter's rulings (unconfirmed).** **AL-0:** the dark proof is on rendered surfaces with literal strings. **AL-1:** the status pill's model name reads "Claude" lit, not a registry model name, because a registry entry is no evidence of which client is connected. **AL-3:** the filter summary reads "AI", as the chip does, because it describes the same filter. **AL-7:** a local-model record whose identity has no usable name (an empty `displayName`, a missing one or one that is not a string; no shipped writer produces the last two) reads "Local model", the provider family the registry already uses.
- **Accepted.** In 0.32 every MCP client's work and every control reads "Claude", including a client that is not Claude (L4). The cohort whose byline read "GPT" from browser settings reads "Claude" once local models are on. Local-model records written before the identity stamp existed (development builds) read "Claude"; a user can name a local model "Claude". The filter chip "AI" keeps Claude's coral pip while also covering local-model comments drawn in their own colour. The rule for an identity without a usable name applies in both flag states, but a dark build never writes `agentIdentity`, so nothing a dark user sees changes. Until "the send notice" lands, a card, margin, batch or context-menu send during a local period raises no notice. For part (b)'s issue, unchanged here: the review export says "Assistant" for every AI; the `.docx` comment export says "Claude" (dark); "Claude replied to your comment" has no identity check; chat export keeps `??`, so an empty stamped name exports as an empty author.
- **Owed before the flip.** "The chip and the default" (L2 and L6) and "the send notice" (a note sent to Claude while chat is local says it waits), each one PR *(amended 2026-11-18: "the chip and the default" is now three PRs, the server half (#2459) and the client half of the automatic default, both built, and the chip, still owed; see the amendment of that date on the automatic default)* *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the title chip)* *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the send notice)*. Part (b), L1's "unless told otherwise" with L4 and L5, is after 0.32: #2455.
- **Merged text this amends:** item 17 of the switching-on amendment's part (c) gains a pointer here; the 2026-11-11 amendment's owed-by-the-flip bullet and Ruling B-14 are not edited (see "Closed and standing" above).

**Amendment 2026-11-18 (the automatic default, still dark).** Label decision L6 of 2026-11-17 and Default decisions DD1 to DD4 of 2026-11-18, the reviewing session's rulings, and the drafter's rulings CD-2 to CD-10 (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in two PRs for #1123: the server half (#2459) and the client half (the PR that follows it). `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the rule runs only inside a Models-store write, which only the Models tab and the first-run window reach, and neither mounts while dark; the dark boot's one write (the browser-storage reconcile) is byte-identical, with no marker.

- **The decisions.** Each was picked from options the reviewing session wrote; the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words.
  - **Label decision L6** (2026-11-17), the chip's source. Picked: "First model becomes the default (Recommended)": "When you save your first local model it is marked as the default automatically, so the chip (and anything else that reads the default) works. You can change the default later." Not picked: "Chip names whoever answers"; "Leave it hidden".
  - **Default decision DD1** (2026-11-18). Asked which wins when someone has an old cloud entry or a switched-off model marked as the default and then saves their first local model (the 2026-11-11 bullet "Which saved model answers", that such a default "is never overridden by a spare local model", against L6). Picked: "Keep the old default (Recommended)": "Your 11-11 rule stands: Tandem never changes a default you set. The new local model is saved but does not answer chat until you mark it as the default yourself; Settings says so. Rare case (only people with a cloud entry from an old version, or who switched their default off)." Not picked: "The local model takes over" (the first-run window excepted: see below).
  - **Default decision DD2** (2026-11-18). Asked about one enabled local model left with no default after a delete or switch-off. Picked: "Mark it at the next change (Recommended)": "The next time you change anything in Models (the delete or switch-off itself counts), the one remaining enabled local model is marked as the default. Never touches a default you chose." Not picked: "Leave it unmarked".
  - **Default decision DD3** (2026-11-18). Asked about a model saved switched off and later turned on, with no other local model enabled. Picked: "Yes, turning it on counts (Recommended)": "Turning on your only local model marks it as the default, same as saving it enabled would have." Not picked: "No, only saving counts".
  - **Default decision DD4** (2026-11-18). He was told: today, if another program on this computer plants a local model in Tandem's settings, Tandem warns him when that model starts answering chat, and an unrelated edit of his never counts as him choosing it; with automatic defaults sent as part of his own change, the server would read "marked as default" as his choice: the warning would not appear when a deletion or switch-off makes another model answer, and renaming any model would record a planted lone model as chosen by him. Picked: "Server tells automatic from chosen (Recommended)": "The change says which default was set automatically, and the server ignores that part when deciding whether you chose a model: all your picks stay as they are and every warning stays. Costs a small server change on a route that also exists in today's builds (it must behave identically there), plus tests." Not picked: "Only mark what you just saved or turned on"; "Accept the weaker bound"; "No automatic default at all".
- **The rule.** After a models write (an add, an edit, an On/Off toggle or a delete, whatever it changed; setting the Default is not one), when the saved models have no default at all (none, or one naming no entry) and exactly one enabled local model, that model is marked as the Default in the same write, and the write says the default was set automatically (`autoDefaultId`). A default that names an entry, local, cloud or switched off, is never changed by Tandem. Two or more enabled local models with none marked stay unmarked.
- **What it amends.** One clause of the 2026-11-11 bullet "Which saved model answers": "a sole enabled local model is used without being marked Default" becomes "it is marked as the Default at the next models change". The rest of that bullet stands, including "two or more enabled local models with none marked get the "none of them was marked Default" notice" and "a default that is set but disabled or cloud is never overridden by a spare local model". The resolver (`resolveLocalModel`) is unchanged, so a sole enabled local model still answers unmarked in the states the rule has not yet reached (a registry older than this build, until its next models write).
- **The reviewing session's rulings** (open to Bryan's change). Its earlier ruling that a cloud or switched-off default "does not count" was its error and is withdrawn: DD1 decides it. **Ruling 1, after the write only:** the rule looks only at the state the write leaves. An earlier draft also marked the one enabled local model from before a write that took the enabled local models from one unmarked to two; it was dropped, because that was the only case where the client would differ from master on the server, keeping an unmarked, possibly planted, model answering and saving it as the default. So adding a second enabled local model beside an unmarked first marks nothing (master's behaviour and notice); since the first model saved is marked from now on, that state arises only from a registry older than this build or from another writer (the Models tab offers no way to clear a default by hand). The first-run window is not covered by this: its model becomes the Default (below). Also: Settings' line is static text above the list; it names the switched-off case when an entry is both cloud and switched off; it gives the longer instruction unless chat is known to be set to the local model; it gives no instruction when the Default radio is disabled or unavailable; and "anything in Models" means any models write, not the chat-answerer radios or the commenting checkbox.
- **What the two PRs built.** The server half (#2459): `ModelsPostBody.autoDefaultId`; the shared rule `autoDefaultFor` (`src/shared/models/auto-default.ts`); `ModelsWriteMeta.autoDefaultId`; and `autoDefaultHonoured` in the local-model collaborator, which honours the marker only for an own-window write, only when it names the posted default and the rule reproduces it, and only when the write did not replace a live default, and then only keeps the marking from counting as the user choosing the entry (DD4). The client half: the rule inside the `apply` of every models write in the Models store (`src/client/hooks/useModels.svelte.ts`), run again on the server's fresh state after a 409, never in `projectModelsFile` and never in `setDefault`; the marker reset at each run and on rollback; the first-run window's model becomes the Default (the bullet below); and Settings → Models says, above the list, when a cloud or switched-off default keeps the saved local models from answering (DD1).
- **The first-run window's model becomes the Default (Bryan, 2026-11-18, review round 1 of #2462).** Put to him with the window's behaviour under the rule above (its model not made the Default when another default, or another enabled local model, was already saved), he picked, from options written by the reviewing session, the one labelled "The window's model becomes Default (Recommended)" (the label is quoted; what follows is the reviewing session's summary, not Bryan's words): choosing a model in the first-run window counts as him choosing the Default, replacing any earlier one, as it did before this change; elsewhere the rule that Tandem never changes a default automatically still holds. Built: after the window's add commits, if the store's default is not the model it just saved, the window calls `setDefault` with it, a chosen default whose POST carries no `autoDefaultId`; when the add's own write marked it (an empty registry, the usual case) there is no second write. RW-5 is kept: a failed set-default after a committed add closes the window as a success and clears the store's error; a close while that set-default is pending completes the window once and clears the error if it fails. *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the title chip)* A close during the add itself skips the window's set-default, but a default the store marked inside the add's own write stands (CD-2); so the "not made the default" half of the first-run window brief's R2.4 holds only where the rule did not mark it.
- **The drafter's rulings (unconfirmed).** **CD-2:** a first-run window closed during its add no longer stops a default the store marked inside that add's own write (the window's own set-default is skipped). **CD-3:** an edit that turns a model on or off is covered exactly as the On/Off box is, because the rule is in the store's `updateModel`. **CD-4:** the Default radio stays unconditional and never sends the marker. **CD-5:** the rule runs on each `apply` run, which after a 409 is the server's fresh state. **CD-6:** #2456's Lows are built, its PR-body Low dropped. **CD-7:** after a failed save the first-run window puts focus on Save, whichever control submitted. **CD-8:** switching the default off leaves it the default (DD1: Tandem "never changes a default you set"). **CD-9:** Settings' sentences: "Your Default model, <name>, is switched off, so your local models won't answer chat." or "… is a cloud model, …", then " To use one, mark it as Default." with chat known local, or " To use one, mark it as Default and set chat to your local model." otherwise. **CD-10:** an empty name is left out of that line with its commas. (CD-1 is withdrawn; it is now Ruling 1.)
- **Accepted.** Two or more enabled local models with none marked: nothing marks one, and "no default chosen" shows as before. An older registry with one unmarked enabled local model stays unmarked until its next models write. With chat set to the local model and a cloud or switched-off default, the "Who answers your chat" `none` line shows beside the new line. The chat-answerer radios and the commenting checkbox do not trigger the rule. While lit and until the chip PR lands, the title bar's chip shows an automatically marked model even with chat set to Claude. *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the title chip)*
- **Next.** The title bar's chip (Label decision L2: shown only while chat is set to the local model) is its own PR, still owed before the flip. *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the title chip)* *(see the 2026-11-18 amendment on the send notice)*

**Amendment 2026-11-18 (the title chip, still dark).** Label decision L2 of 2026-11-17, the reviewing session's readings and rulings, and the drafter's rulings CT-1 to CT-4 (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in the PR for #1123 that follows #2462. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: App passes the title bar no chip label while the flag is off, the chat setting the chip reads is itself null while dark, the Models store fetches nothing while dark and the first-run window never mounts.

- **The decision.** Label decision L2 (2026-11-17), picked from options the reviewing session wrote (the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words): "Only while chat uses it (Recommended)": "Shown only while chat is set to the local model, so the title bar tells you who is answering. Hidden while chat is with Claude." Not picked: "Hide it"; "Leave as it is". L2 is the explicit exception, decided the same day about this chip, to P10(1)'s "a local model's name appears only on what it wrote": the chip labels no work. It names the user's own Default and recommends nothing (P2).
- **What this PR builds.** With the flag off the chip is hidden: App passes the title bar no label. With it on, the whole decision is `resolveDefaultModelChip` (`src/client/utils/model-chip.ts`), flag-free and pure, over the saved Default, the saved models, whether the list has loaded, the chat setting (App's `localChatSetting`, the status pill's own input) and whether the licence refuses AI. The first rule that matches wins: while the licence refuses AI (the pill reads "AI off") it is hidden; while chat is not set to the local model (a null chat setting) it is hidden; while chat is set to the local model but no local model can be used (`"unusable"`) it is hidden; until the list has loaded it is hidden; with no Default, or a Default naming no entry, it is hidden; with a cloud Default or a switched-off Default it is hidden (DD1); otherwise, with an enabled local model as the Default, it shows that model's name trimmed, or "Local model" when the name is empty. Solo is not an input ("In Solo mode the chosen answerer still answers chat"). Its visible text is the name alone; its accessible name is "Chat is set to your local model: <name>. Open Settings → Models." and its hover text "Chat is set to your local model: <name>". The click still opens Settings → Models. App's derivation is pinned by `tests/client/app-model-chip-wiring.test.ts`.
- **The reviewing session's readings of L2** (open to Bryan's change). With chat set to the local model, the chip still hides when no local model can be used (it would name a model that cannot answer), and when no Default names an entry (there is no Default to name; L6 marks a lone model at the next models write).
- **The reviewing session's rulings** (open to Bryan's change). The signal is the chat setting being `"local"`, never the local period or the held flag alone; the hidden rows for a null chat setting, an unloaded list, a cloud and a switched-off Default, and the shown row; the chip's strings above (Ruling B-18: they say what is set, never who "answers"); and the first-run window's failed set-default (the bullet below).
- **The first-run window's failed set-default.** RW-5 is replaced: a failed set-default after a committed add still closes the window as a success and clears the store's error, and then, when the saved model is still in the list and still not the Default, one notice says so: "Your local model was saved, but Tandem could not make it your Default model. You can mark it as Default in Settings → Models." (a warning, with no button). A close while that set-default is pending completes the window once, and a later failure raises the same notice. A close during the add itself raises none (CD-2 stands). Not built: keeping the window open with a retry.
- **The drafter's rulings (unconfirmed).** **CT-1:** the chip is hidden while the licence refuses AI, as the pill's rule 0. **CT-2:** a blank name reads "Local model", the fallback the byline uses for a nameless local model (AL-7); the state is unreachable through shipped UI, and the accessible name keeps the visible text. **CT-3:** the list having loaded replaces the old loading gate, so the chip no longer blinks out during a later reload, when the list keeps its last values. **CT-4:** the chip's appearing or going is not announced: the switch is the user's own act in Settings, and the chip repeats what the pill's name says where the pill renders; where the pill renders nothing (Solo with nothing running, booting, ready with nothing attached) the chip is the only sign.
- **Accepted.** The chip follows this window's optimistic Models store: it can appear before a write lands, vanish on a rollback and lag another window's save. A stopped local server reads `usable`, so the chip shows and the run fails with its own toast; with the chat setting `"local"` from a fresh held flag, a Default with no address or a rejected one also shows the chip; a second window's chat read is stale until a trigger (no polling); a refused chat read with a fresh held flag shows the chip while Settings says it can't show what chat is set to. A known local read whose state is `threw` reads as set to the local model, so the chip shows. The chip shows the name trimmed but not length-capped, while the byline caps it. After a failed boot load, a later successful write leaves the list not loaded, so the chip stays hidden until a load succeeds. An older registry's lone unmarked enabled local model answers with no chip until its next models write, while the pill says "Chat: your local model". The first-run window's notice comes after the window has closed and carries no button.
- **Owed.** The E2E chip assertions in `tests/e2e/settings-models.spec.ts` (a cloud Default with the chip expected) are wrong once lit and belong to item 13.
- **Merged text this amends:** the automatic-default amendment's DD1 bullet gains "(the first-run window excepted: see below)"; the RW-5 ruling in the 2026-11-17 first-run window note, the "RW-5 is kept" sentence and the chip's Accepted sentence of the automatic-default amendment, its "Next." bullet and the agent-labels amendment's owed bullet each gain a pointer here.

**Amendment 2026-11-18 (the send notice, still dark).** The owner's picks SN-P1 to SN-P3 of 2026-11-18, the reviewing session's rulings and the drafter's Rulings SN-4 and SN-6 (unconfirmed until the review session confirms them on the PR), built in one PR for #1123. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: App fills the card-send seam only under the flag, and the decision's local-period test (`inLocalPeriod`) is false at its first line while dark.

- **The picks.** Each was picked from options the reviewing session wrote; the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words.
  - **SN-P1, what waits.** Asked whether comments written, and notes sent to Claude, in the local period should be given to Claude after the switch back (told that today's code gives them, that his 2026-11-17 start pick (ADR-054) assumed so, and that A2's decision 3 says what was typed meanwhile is never handed over). Picked: "Yes, they wait for Claude (Recommended)"; the option's description, in the reviewing session's summary (not the option's text, and not Bryan's words): comments and sent notes reach Claude after the switch back; chat from the period never does. So A2's decision 3 reads as chat only.
  - **SN-P2, what Tandem says.** Shown the sentence below and told it says nothing about later and can read as a refusal. Picked: "Say it waits for Claude (Recommended)": "New sentence for both the comment box and note cards, along the lines of: 'Saved. Chat is set to your local model, so Claude will get this comment after you switch chat back to Claude.' ... I will word it so it stays true if you resolve the comment or turn on Solo first." Not picked: keep today's sentence; today's sentence plus a clause.
  - **SN-P3, a lasting sign.** Picked: "No, the notice is enough (Recommended)" (its description is not recorded in the builder brief on #1123, comment 6988660772, so none is quoted). Nothing more for 0.32; #2461 tracks it.
- **The replaced sentence.** Part B's comment sentence (part B's R8.2, never recorded here before), the composer's in the local period: "Comment saved. Chat is set to your local model, which is not sent comments, and Tandem is not sending this comment to Claude now." It is replaced by two strings in `src/client/status/addressed-ai-notice.ts`: **W**, info, `LOCAL_COMMENT_INFO_TEXT`, "Saved. Chat is set to your local model, so this comment waits for Claude." (the composer's `local-comment` toast and a card send's); and **W-past**, warning, `LOCAL_COMMENT_KEPT_TEXT`, "Saved while chat was set to your local model, so this comment was kept for Claude." (the composer's `no-agent` variant in the local period; its CTA and idPrefix `ai-not-ready` unchanged). W-past is past tense because a warning persists in the tray, and says "kept", not "held", because "held" means Solo elsewhere in Tandem.
- **Why the strings depart from the picked option's example** ("Saved. Chat is set to your local model, so Claude will get this comment after you switch chat back to Claude."). Delivery depends on states a send-time toast cannot know (the residuals below), and Ruling B-7 requires the toast to be true in every state, so the strings say only that the comment waits, never when or how it is delivered. Resolving or deleting the comment later ends the wait; that is a later act, not a false statement at send time.
- **What this PR builds.** A card send (a note promoted to a comment through `sendNoteToClaude` or `promoteNotesToComments`, from its card, the margin, the batch bar or the desktop context menu) calls a plain seam (`src/client/panels/card-send-raise.ts`) once, after its write, carrying nothing about the annotation; a throwing raise is caught and logged and the write stands. App fills the seam, under its own `if (BYO_MODELS_ENABLED)`, with `createCardSendNotice` (`src/client/hooks/useChatAnswerer.svelte.ts`), which reads the mode, the polled `serverUnreachable`, the store's read and the held flag at each raise, and in the local period pushes the composer's `local-comment` info toast with W (same dedupKey, so the two coalesce). It fetches, probes and dispatches nothing. `src/server/models/local-period.ts`'s header points here for decision 3.
- **The reviewing session's rulings.** The send notice as its own PR; Rulings B-7, B-16 and B-18 bind it; W and W-past as above; the precedence, first match wins: Solo, nothing; server unreachable, nothing (W would say "Saved ... waits" for a write a server restart can drop, and an offline toast would rest on a polled flag the card cannot refresh, so a card never shows `offline`); the local period (`inLocalPeriod`: a known read wins, held counts only when the read is not known), W; otherwise nothing. No separate flag check in the decision. No `no-agent` parity: every non-`ai-off` chip rests on the polled `sessionLive`, which can be stale without a probe, and the card makes none. The getter factory and App's pinned fill.
- **The drafter's rulings (unconfirmed).** **SN-4:** the card's toast uses the composer's id and dedupKey shape, `ai-local-comment-comment`. **SN-6:** a raise error is caught and logged, and the send stands.
- **Residuals to "waits"** (Ruling B-18's pattern: gaps recorded here, not in the UI). Claude is given the comment at its next poll once chat is back on Claude, Solo is off, the document is open and a Claude is running. The gaps: chat switched back without the user, where nothing is started nor woken (A2's decision 8); Claude not set up, where there is nothing to start; Claude Off or, today, stopped by the user, where it is not started (ADR-054's 2026-11-17 pick changes the latter when 3b-2b ships); on-click mode with no Claude running, where it is not started until the user's Start or next chat, and a restart before that Start leaves the comment to the next boot or chat; a running Claude is woken at the user's switch back whatever the mode; and the user's own Claude Code, which Tandem never starts, nor wakes after a switch back the user did not make. A stale read in this window shows W after another window's switch back (Accepted).
- **Accepted.** The composer can show `no-agent`, `no-push`, `ai-off` or `offline` where the card shows W or nothing; the card refuses at click with the licence off. With the server unreachable the composer says "saved locally ... wasn't delivered" and the card says nothing, as before; for up to one poll interval after the server is back the card stays silent in the local period (a missed notice, never a false one); and for up to about 16 s after the server goes, before two dead polls mark it unreachable, the card and the composer both show W, and if the server returns as a new process the promotion is dropped and the card is a note again. The composer's warning shows only with no AI connected; the old copy said what happens next, W-past leaves that to its button. The info entry lives 5 minutes in the tray, where "is set" and "waits" can go stale. A stale read either way: after another window's switch back that nothing makes this window re-read, it still shows W (the composer and the pill share this); a stale "claude" read is silent. Silent while the server's local period holds when the read is not known and nothing is held, and on a refused or foreign page (Ruling B-20). Early fetch: a tool-using Claude can read the comment in the period with `tandem_getAnnotations` (A2's residual); "waits" is about what Tandem hands. The batch path is unreachable while `.docx` is dark; the seam is never cleared on App teardown, as the chat-answerer toast sink is not. A user reply in Claude's thread during the period also waits, silently. A batch promotion of several notes still says "this comment". W-past shares its tray row (dedupKey `ai-not-ready-comment`) with the composer's warning for a comment saved outside the period with no AI connected, so one row can carry the newest message with a count that includes the other state's comments. A switch to the local model while the composer's probe is in flight gives W-past for a comment saved a moment before the switch; "kept for Claude" still holds.
- **What it closes.** The agent-labels amendment's Accepted sentence "Until "the send notice" lands, a card, margin, batch or context-menu send during a local period raises no notice." is closed lit and stands dark; it is left as written. The "send notice" half of that amendment's owed bullet is built. A lasting sign is #2461.

**Amendment 2026-11-18 (item 18, the reply refusals, still dark).** Item 18 of the switching-on amendment's part (c) is closed: the two reply refusals the local model's loop shares with Claude's `tandem_annotationReply` no longer name Claude. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged. The wording is the reviewing session's and is Bryan's to change.

- **The options.** One neutral sentence for every caller, Claude included (built), or a separate sentence for a local model (not built). This amendment builds the first because the sentences are produced by one shared function, `describeReplyWriteRefusal`, and neither is on screen.
- **The sentences.** `invalid-note`: "Claude can only reply to comments that are shared with it" becomes "You can only reply to comments that are shared with you". `not-owned` stem: "Claude can only reply on annotations it authored; " becomes "You cannot reply on this annotation; ", followed by each caller's remedy, which is unchanged (Claude: "answer a user's comment with tandem_reply or a fresh tandem_comment"; a local model: "start a fresh thread on the user's text with comment_on_quote instead"). "It authored" was wrong for both callers, since a local model's comment is stamped `author: "claude"` and either may reply on the other's. The `not-owned` stem speaks of the one record the guard refused, not of a class of annotations, so it needs no change if replies on a user's comments are later allowed (#2360).
- **Dark builds change for Claude.** Over MCP, Claude receives the new sentences in every build. The codes (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `NOT_OWNED`), which the shipped skill keys on, are unchanged, as are the ADR-027 guards and their order.
- **Merged text this amends:** item 18's own line and the 2026-11-11 owed-by-the-flip bullet gain a pointer here; their old text is left as history.

**Amendment 2026-11-18 ("Use it for chat", still dark).** Bryan's picks P5 and W2 of 2026-11-17, the reviewing session's readings, and the coordinating session's rulings CR-1 to CR-5, built in one PR for #1123 from the builder brief on #1123 ([comment 6992004629](https://github.com/bloknayrb/tandem/issues/1123#issuecomment-6992004629)). `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees or sends changes: App sets the toast sink only under the flag, the models-write listener that pushes the confirmation is never subscribed while dark, and the press and `setChatAnswerer` return at their first lines.

- **The picks.** Each was picked from options; the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words.
  - **P5** (also recorded in the amendment on switching local models on): "Offer one click (Recommended)": "The confirmation has a "Use it for chat" button. Chat stays with Claude unless they press it. Small change."
  - **W2**, picked from options on 2026-11-17, recorded by the reviewing session; the builder brief above is the tracked place holding its text: "Stays until pressed or closed (Recommended)": "It holds a button you may want, so it waits for you: press "Use it for chat" or close it. Chat stays with Claude unless you press."
- **The reviewing session's readings.** P5 narrows Decision B1 for this one button: the switch is still changed in Settings → Models, and the confirmation's button is the one other place. A press is a selection under Ruling B-1 ("a switch to the local model is only ever sent by the user's selection").
- **What this PR builds** (`src/client/hooks/useChatAnswerer.svelte.ts`). Decision B4's confirmation, pushed only when its read settles known Claude, in any local-model state including `"threw"` (CR-1), and dropped when the store already knows local by then, now carries the action `{ label: "Use it for chat", stay: true }`; `stay` is the toast flag of #2468, so the toast waits until pressed or closed (W2). A press does nothing during a pending switch (CR-3); otherwise it re-reads (CR-5), then, first match: a switch started meanwhile by another selection, nothing (that is the user's later choice); from the store, known local, nothing; still not known, X; otherwise it sends `setChatAnswerer("local")`, which now returns its POST's result (`not-sent`, `answered` with `effective`, `saved` and `superseded`, or `unconfirmed`), and reports it (CR-2): S for local saved, U for local not saved, nothing for a superseded Claude (Settings shows no line then either), nothing for `not-sent`; for any other result, first match: the store known local after the switch's re-read, S (it claims only that chat was set); the POST's own answer naming Claude in effect (a refused or failed write), R; anything else (a timeout, a network failure, a 403, 404 or other response that does not state the answerer, a dropped or failed re-read), N; after any `answered` or `unconfirmed` result it clears Settings' outcome line, since its own notice carries the result. The confirmation is withdrawn, toast and tray row, whenever the store's answerer becomes local: an adopted known-local read, or a switch whose POST answered local (CR-4). The strings, all `type: "launcher"`, info for S and warning for the rest (past tense, as a warning persists in the tray): **S** "Chat was set to your local model."; **U** "Chat was set to your local model, but Tandem could not save that choice, so chat may be set back to Claude when Tandem restarts."; **R** "Chat could not be set to your local model. It was left set to Claude."; **N** "Tandem could not confirm that chat was set to your local model. Check Who answers your chat in Settings → Models."; **X** "Tandem did not change who answers your chat, because it could not read the current setting. Check Who answers your chat in Settings → Models." Settings' own outcome lines are unchanged. The confirmation is marked shown only after it was pushed, so a push that throws does not spend the once-per-install offer.
- **Corrected by the reviewing session in review round 1 (its own brief's error).** The brief had N for every `unconfirmed` result and R for every refused or failed write, whatever the re-read found; so a timed-out POST that landed would leave "could not confirm" persisting in the tray beside a chip and pill saying chat is set to the local model. The rule above (the store after the re-read decides) replaces it; `SwitchResult` and Settings' own lines are unchanged. In the same round the reviewing session reworded N and X (the brief's "You can set it under ..." told the user to do what Settings may not allow on a refused, unreadable or unavailable page, and X's "Chat was not set ..." could read as a claim about the current setting), and added the press's quiet return when another switch began during its read. **Corrected again in review round 2 (also the reviewing session's own error).** Round 1 had R whenever the store knew Claude after the re-read; but the store can be stale (the `unconfirmed` path never writes the answerer, and a re-read is dropped when a new server process is detected, which is also when a POST fails or times out), so "It was left set to Claude" could be shown for a POST that landed. R now rests only on the server's own answer to this press; a store that merely still says Claude gives N.
- **The coordinating session's rulings** (built as written; the alternatives are Bryan's). **CR-1:** the button always, even when the model cannot answer now (Settings and the pill say why after a press; a `"threw"` check is not "can't answer", Ruling B-17); option: none then, which narrows P5. **CR-2:** the press reports its POST's result as above; a superseded Claude shows nothing; option: say so. **CR-3:** a press during a pending Settings switch sends nothing and spends the offer; option: wait, then decide (could override a fresh Claude choice). **CR-4:** the confirmation withdraws once chat is set to the local model, narrowing "stays until pressed or closed"; option: leave it. **CR-5:** the press re-reads first and X when the store is still not known; option: decide from the last read.
- **Accepted.** With a dialog open the toast is reachable by mouse only (Settings' radios are the equivalent); after a press focus lands on the body. The click always removes the toast, so a press ending in a pending switch, X, N, R, a superseded Claude or `not-sent` spends the once-only offer. A press finding chat already local is silent. A withdrawal for a local that later reverts (a write-failed local, lost at restart; a superseded local followed by another window's Claude) spends the offer. A second window's confirmation goes stale with no trigger there, and now stays. The staying toast takes one of the five visible slots, and can outlive its 5-minute tray row. A model that cannot answer at the press still gets chat set to it; the pill and Settings then say so. U, R, N and X persist in the tray. Settings' own alert line may be written and cleared within one flush when Settings → Models is open during a press (a possible double announcement; left to the accessibility work with the focus loss on press). U persists in the tray and still says "may be set back ... when Tandem restarts" after a restart. The staying confirmation can be evicted only when every other visible toast also stays. After a write-failed answer naming Claude, if another window then sets chat to the local model and this window's re-read fails, R's second sentence ("It was left set to Claude.") is stale. Also left to Bryan: "Use it for chat" when the Default is a cloud or switched-off entry sets chat to the local model although the new model will not answer (Settings says why); B4's sentence still points to Settings; the button is offered while the licence refuses AI (the gate is dark, and the Settings radio has no licence term either); a reload loses the offer for good.
- **What it closes.** Item 24 of the owed list in the amendment on switching local models on is closed lit, with no narrowing; it stands dark. Pointers, with no claim of their own: Decision B1 and Decision B4 and Ruling B-10 (part B's amendment), P5 and item 24 (the amendment on switching local models on).

**Amendment 2026-11-18 (the development allowance closed in release builds; built in #2476 for #1123 and #2410).** Bryan's pick P9 and the review session's reading V15 (2026-11-17 amendment on switching local models on), and the drafter's Rulings DA-1 to DA-4 (unconfirmed until the review session or Bryan confirms or changes them on the PR).
- **The pick and the reading.** P9 (picked from options; the option's text, not Bryan's words): "Re-confirm + close dev door (Recommended)": "Accept it live, and make release builds ignore the developer-only setting that lets one extra local address change these settings. Small change; no effect on normal use." V15 (the review session's reading): CI's E2E backend is the built server a release ships and relies on the allowance, so the E2E harness's run keeps it, told apart by a fact the change that builds it chooses and tests.
- **As built.** P9's "release builds ignore the developer-only setting" is built as: a release build (a tsup bundle carrying the `__TANDEM_VERSION__` define) ignores `TANDEM_MODELS_DEV_ORIGIN` unless the process was started with the argument `--tandem-e2e-harness`, which only the E2E harness's launcher (`scripts/e2e-server.mjs`) adds and no shipped launcher passes. A server run from source (no define: tsx, vitest) is unchanged. The fact lives in a dependency-free leaf, `src/server/models/dev-allowance-build.ts`; `resolveModelsDevOrigin` takes its answer as a required argument, and `server.ts` passes `devAllowanceBuild(process.argv)`.
- **What it stops, and what it does not.** It stops an accidentally inherited variable (an exported shell, a leftover developer setting, a profile entry) from opening the allowance in a build Tandem ships. It does not stop whoever controls the server's environment or command line: such an actor can set `NODE_OPTIONS`, whose preload reaches argv and the server's code. That is #2410's existing residual, "a process running as the user"; no new acceptance is made.
- **The fact chosen (DA-1), and why.** Argv, for the reason `isTauriSidecar` reads argv: a web page cannot set it, a child process does not inherit it, and both shipped launchers pass a fixed argument list (the desktop shell exactly the server entry point and `--tauri-sidecar`; the npm `tandem` exactly the server entry point), now pinned to pass nothing else. Rejected: another environment variable, `CI` or the app-data directory's name (each inherited); the define alone (CI's E2E backend is built); "not installed" or "inside a checkout" (against item 28).
- **The strings (the drafter's).** DA-2, the line a release build logs, built from the variable's name and never from its value, which it neither validates nor echoes: "[Tandem] TANDEM_MODELS_DEV_ORIGIN is set but ignored: a release build of Tandem accepts local-model settings from Tandem's own window only. The variable works with a server run from source (npm run dev:server or npm run dev:standalone)." DA-3, the Models tab's development sentence: "These settings can only be changed in Tandem's own window. This page is served from <origin>. In a development setup, start Tandem's server from source with TANDEM_MODELS_DEV_ORIGIN=<origin>, then reload this page."
- **What changes, for whom.** Without the variable nothing changes for anyone: no line is logged and nothing is admitted that was not before. With it set, the desktop sidecar, an npm install, `node dist/server/index.js` and `cargo tauri dev`'s own sidecar now answer a page on the allowance origin as they answer any other loopback port (the settings POSTs refuse it 403 before reading the body), and log the ignored line; CI's E2E backend (built, started through the launcher) and every run from source are unchanged; a release server whose environment carries a `NODE_OPTIONS` preload that pushes the argument is still admitted (the residual above). **DA-4: it acts while local models ship dark too**, with no flag gate, because `POST /api/models` answers in every shipped build.
- **Item 28 closed.** It rests on the chain of pins (CI's E2E command runs the bundle through the launcher, the launcher appends the argument, the leaf's `e2e-harness` rows, the evaluated tsup config defining `__TANDEM_VERSION__` for `dist/server`, and a `check` step that fails when the built bundle still holds `typeof __TANDEM_VERSION__`) and on the built-artifact probe, run on this PR: `node dist/server/index.js` with the variable set refused `POST /api/models` from that origin with 403 and logged the ignored line; with `--tandem-e2e-harness` it admitted the same request. **Item 31** (the pre-flip security review) **is still owed.** V16 stands: `npm run dev:tauri`'s own sidecar runs the built bundle and so ignores the variable; CONTRIBUTING gives the route through a server run from source.

**Amendment 2026-11-19 (clearer failure messages, still dark).** P6 as picked, the reviewing session's rulings and readings in the builder brief on #1123 (comment 6995265242), built in the PR for #1123 that closes item 25. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged; flag off the collaborator never subscribes, so no toast or chat notice below is reachable.

- **The pick.** P6, picked from options (the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words): "Clearer messages (Recommended)": "Each failure names the likely cause and the fix (start the server; check the model name; this model may not support tools, try another; or set chat back to Claude). Shown when the first message fails. Small change."
- **How failures are told apart** (part C's scope, question 2; no answer was recorded here, built as its recommended option by the reviewing session's ruling): by the HTTP status and by where the error arose, never by the words of an error. The chat client (`ollama-client.ts`) tags the thrown value itself, in a private `WeakMap` read through `requestFailure`, and throws that same value: no wrapper and no new message, so every thrown message, the harness's matching and what is sent are unchanged. A rejected fetch or a rejected body read is `unreachable` (an `AbortError` is never tagged); a non-2xx is `http` with its status; a non-JSON 200 or a stream with no valid frame is `unreadable`; the byte cap is `too-large`. A throw from Tandem's own code outside the fetch call and the body reads (a JSON 200 of an unexpected shape included), a caller's callback or the invalid-endpoint check is untagged and reads `unknown`. The loop copies the tag into `LoopMetrics.failure` in its catch's error arm.
- **The decision** (`classifyFailure`, first match wins): no tag → `unknown`; `unreachable`, `unreadable`, `too-large` as tagged; HTTP 401 or 403 → `refused-access` (Tandem sends no key to a local server, so one that requires a key refuses every request); 404 → `not-found`; 400 → `bad-request`; any other status → `server-error`.
- **The messages** (`RUN_FAILURE_TEXT`, the run toast posted once when a run ends `error`; the reviewing session's wording, Bryan's to change):
  - `unreachable`: "Tandem's request to your local model's server did not complete. Check that the server is running, then edit the model in Settings → Models to check its Endpoint."
  - `not-found`: "Your local model's server could not find the model or address Tandem asked for. Check that the model is downloaded, then check its Model ID and Endpoint in Settings → Models."
  - `bad-request`: "Your local model's server refused Tandem's request. Tandem needs a model that supports tool calling; if this one does not, use another, or set chat to Claude in Settings → Models and send your message again. Any comments your local model already added are still there."
  - `refused-access`: "Your local model's server refused access to Tandem. Tandem sends no API key to local servers, so if the server requires one, turn that requirement off in the server."
  - `server-error`: "Your local model's server reported an error; its own log may say why. Try again in a moment, or set chat to Claude in Settings → Models and send your message again. Any comments your local model already added are still there."
  - `unreadable`: "Your local model's server sent a reply Tandem could not read. Its own log may say why; also check that the model's Endpoint in Settings → Models is the server's address."
  - `too-large`: "Your local model's reply grew too large, so Tandem stopped it. Send your message again, or try another model. Any comments your local model already added are still there."
  - `unknown`: "Your local model's reply stopped because of an error. Send your message again, or set chat to Claude in Settings → Models and send it again. Any comments your local model already added are still there."
- **They replace** `classifyFailure`'s six: "The local model returned an unreadable response.", "The local model endpoint is misconfigured.", "The local model response was too large and was stopped.", "The local model server returned an error.", "The local model request was interrupted." and "The local model could not reach the server or complete the request.", each chosen by matching the thrown message.
- **The no-local-model chat notice is reworded**, because "Add or enable a local model" does nothing when the Default is switched off or is a cloud entry (a set Default is never overridden, 2026-11-11), and "set … back to Claude there" did not say the message must be sent again (Decision B6: a message sent while chat is set to the local model is never handed to Claude). It was "No local model could answer this message. Add or enable a local model in Settings → Models, or set who answers your chat back to Claude there." and is now "No local model could answer this message. In Settings → Models, add or turn on a local model and mark it as Default, or set who answers your chat back to Claude and send your message again." It holds in each state the resolver answers `none` for: nothing saved, no enabled local model, the Default switched off, a cloud Default. When it posts is unchanged (D-C1). Rows already in a chat keep their stored text.
- **Rulings.** B-7, B-18, C-5, C-8, D-C1 and D-C2 bind it unchanged; `RUBRIC_VERSION` is unchanged (notice text is printed by the harness, never matched). **C-2 ("No change to the loop") is amended**: the loop gains one optional metrics field, written in its catch; no turn, no `onTurnEnd` and no `RunTurnOpts` changes. The new field is printed by `probe/local-model-spike/dogfood-m1.ts`, which is harmless; it reaches no harness record, hash or key list. One change from the ruled text: `unknown` began "The local model's reply", which B-18 does not allow; it reads "Your local model's reply".
- **Item 26d** (the lone closing tag, V12) moves from owed to done-or-accepted in part (c) of the 2026-11-17 amendment on switching local models on, as Bryan's pick of 2026-11-18 (#2471). As the reviewing session's reading, this takes 26d out of what is owed before the flip. It is carried in this amendment and is not this PR's feature.
- **Accepted.** 405, 408, 413, 422, 429 and every 5xx share one message; a missing model or missing tool support reported with another status (a 500, say) reads `server-error`; `bad-request` steers every 400 toward tool support, although a 400 can mean something else (a request too long for the model's context, for example). An error the server sends inside a 200 stream counts as a valid frame, so the run ends `clean` and the user sees only "Your local model produced no reply." A server that ignores the model name raises no failure for a wrong one. A refused 3xx reads "did not complete". What LM Studio, llama.cpp and Ollama return for a missing model, a model without tool support or required authentication was not measured; each message is true of its status class and every "why" is conditional. `unreachable`, `not-found`, `unreadable`, `refused-access` and `too-large` do not offer "set chat to Claude" (only `bad-request`, `server-error` and `unknown` do). "Any comments your local model already added are still there" can go stale in the tray once the user deletes them. A resolver throw posts the no-local-model notice, whose fix is then wrong (a bug, logged). The Models tab's own line for the no-local-model state has the same "nothing saved" gap and is not changed here.

**Amendment 2026-11-19 (cloud providers hidden in the add-model window; an old cloud entry shown as unavailable, still dark).** Bryan's picks P4 and W3 of 2026-11-17, and the reviewing session's reading of item 23 and its rulings CP-1 to CP-7, built in one PR for #1123 from the builder brief on #1123 ([comment 6996126248](https://github.com/bloknayrb/tandem/issues/1123#issuecomment-6996126248)). `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the add-model window mounts only from the Models tab, which `SettingsModal.svelte` filters out while dark (pinned by `tests/client/local-providers-dark.test.ts`). No server, store or App change.

- **The picks.** Each was picked from options; the label and description quoted are the option's text, not Bryan's words.
  - **P4** (recorded in the amendment on switching local models on): "Hide until they work (Recommended)": "Only local choices are shown. No public promise in the product; cloud keys can be announced whenever you decide."
  - **W3**, picked from options on 2026-11-17, recorded by the reviewing session; issue #1123 comment 6965850014 is the tracked place that holds the option's text: "Shown as unavailable (Recommended)": "The entry stays in the list; opening it shows its provider as unavailable with "This provider can't be used in Tandem." They can delete it." One question was left open there: an old cloud entry that is the current Default (disable it, or offer to clear the default). Built: kept as it is (Decision DD1: a cloud Default is never overridden); offering to clear it is Bryan's option.
- **An open question, built as recommended.** Should an old cloud entry's Default and On/Off controls work, and can its window save? Built: the controls are unavailable and say why; the window has no Save. Open to Bryan's change.
- **The reviewing session's reading of item 23.** "No OpenAI, Gemini or Anthropic row is shown in the add-model window" means no such option can be chosen there; the unavailable window's provider name is governed by W3. On that reading item 23 is closed for the add-model window (its first-run half closed 2026-11-17), lit; it stands dark.
- **What this PR builds.** A cloud entry is one whose provider fails `isLocalProvider` (`anthropic`, `openai`, `gemini`); there is no new predicate.
  - *The add-model window.* The provider select lists the four local providers only, with no disabled option and no suffix. The old cloud note and the two cloud placeholders are gone.
  - *An old cloud entry's window.* Opening an existing cloud entry (on or off, Default or not, with or without a stored key) shows a window named "Model unavailable" and described by "This provider can't be used in Tandem.". It holds the entry's name (its model ID when it has none), a Provider line reading "<name> (API key)", that sentence, the × and one "Close" button, which takes focus on open. It has no form, field or Save. Closing it returns focus to the row's "View" button. The local edit and Add windows are unchanged and move no focus on mount (item 27).
  - *Kept, unreachable.* The window's API-key block and the cloud arms of its Save stay in source, because their test ids are in the test-id contract and cloud keys may return.
  - *The Models tab.* A cloud entry is listed under "Anthropic (API key)", "OpenAI (API key)" or "Gemini (API key)". Its row adds "This provider can't be used in Tandem." under the model ID. Its Default and On/Off keep their checked state but are unavailable: `aria-disabled`, a refused click and the unavailable style, each described by that sentence and then by the own-window message. Its open button reads "View", and Delete works as before. A local entry's row is unchanged.
  - *The strings.* **U1** "This provider can't be used in Tandem." (W3's option text, verbatim); **U3** "View"; **U4** "Model unavailable"; **U5** "Close"; **U6** the three "(API key)" headings, which are also the window's Provider line.
- **The reviewing session's rulings** (built as written; the alternatives are Bryan's).
  - **CP-1:** the row's Default and On/Off are unavailable, and opening is kept (W3 says "opening it shows"). Option: leave them live.
  - **CP-2:** the window has no Save. Option: allow Save, to switch the entry to a local provider.
  - **CP-3:** the sentence is on every cloud row as well as in the window. Option: in the window only.
  - **CP-4:** the "(API key)" headings, so the sentence under "Anthropic", near "Who answers your chat: Claude", does not read as Claude being unusable. They also show on an entry with no stored key. Option: the plain names, with the cloud groups after the local ones.
  - **CP-5:** "View" and "Model unavailable". Option: "Edit" and "Edit model".
  - **CP-6:** a cloud entry's controls keep their checked state, and the Default radio keeps its title "Use as the default model". Option: hide On/Off on cloud rows.
  - **CP-7:** the key block is kept, with no store or server change.
- **Accepted.**
  - The × and "Close" share the name "Close".
  - A cloud entry's key stays in the keychain until the entry is deleted.
  - *Settled before this landed:* the brief accepted that, until the PR for ADR-039's owed item 25 (clearer failure messages) merged, the `no-local-model` notice's first instruction ("Add or enable a local model") did not help with a cloud Default beside an enabled local model. That PR merged first and reworded the notice ("In Settings → Models, add or turn on a local model and mark it as Default, …"; the 2026-11-19 amendment on clearer failure messages), which holds in that state; nothing of it remains accepted here.
  - A switched-off cloud Default: Settings' line says the Default "is switched off" while the row's On/Off now refuses. CP-6's option would also settle this.
  - A non-UI caller can still save, enable or mark a cloud entry: a script with Tandem's own origin, the development allowance, a hand-edited file, or the older version's reconcile. The tab shows such an entry as unavailable, and the resolver answers `none`.
- **Left to Bryan** (built as above; each option is his).
  - Delete on a cloud entry drops its stored key with no warning. Option: say so in the confirm step.
  - In the Default radio group an arrow key commits where it lands. Moving off a checked cloud Default therefore makes a local model the Default, and the cloud entry cannot be marked again from the UI. Option: take the cloud radios out of the group.
  - With a cloud Default, "Use it for chat" (the 2026-11-18 amendment "Use it for chat") sets chat to the local model although the new model will not answer.
- **What it closes, and pointers** (no claim of their own).
  - Closes item 23 of the owed list in the amendment on switching local models on, for the add-model window. P4 is in the same amendment.
  - Ruling H-1 (the 2026-11-17 test-hardening amendment) is retired: there is no cloud option left to drive the modal back to.
  - The LM Studio amendment's "the edit modal's cloud note" is removed.

**Amendment 2026-11-19 (the add-model window's fields, still dark).** Bryan's picks P10(5), P10(6), P10(4) and P2, the review session's readings V2, V9 and V18 (all in the amendment on switching local models on), Ruling L-h, and the review session's rulings, built in one PR for #1123 from the builder brief on #1123 ([comment 7013549304](https://github.com/bloknayrb/tandem/issues/1123#issuecomment-7013549304)). `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the add-model window mounts only from the Models tab, which `SettingsModal.svelte` filters out while dark, and the first-run window never mounts while dark (the first pinned by `tests/client/local-providers-dark.test.ts`, the second by `tests/client/model-first-run.test.ts`). The address rule the server uses is moved, not changed. No server behaviour, store or route change.

- **The picks.** Each was picked from options; the text quoted is the option's, not Bryan's words.
  - **P10** "Confirm all six (Recommended)", items as put to him: **(5)** "the add-model window fills in each provider's usual address and says why Save is greyed out"; **(6)** "the add-model window keeps its explanatory small print (about 650 characters)"; **(4)** "nothing is read aloud by a screen reader when the provider is changed in the add-model window".
  - **P2** "No recommendation (Recommended)": "Remove "≥14B recommended". Say: use any local model; results depend on the model, and smaller models answer less reliably. The measurement results stay published as a record, not as advice."
- **The review session's readings** (open to Bryan's change): V2 (Ollama and LM Studio filled in, llama.cpp and Other not), V9 (an address not on this computer is refused at Save with the reason shown, a client-side check) and V18 (Display name optional, with the first-run window's fallback). Ruling L-h gives Other's fallback, "Local server". Splitting item 26a into an add-model half and a first-run half is the review session's reading, not a pick.
- **The review session's rulings** (built as written; the alternatives are Bryan's): the Endpoint's reason is chosen by what is wrong with it, so the sentence never lists a condition the address already meets; the name rule below; and U4's wording.
- **What is built.**
  - *The address.* Add starts at the selected provider's usual address (Ollama's `http://127.0.0.1:11434` on open; LM Studio's `http://127.0.0.1:1234`; none for llama.cpp or Other). On a provider change, on Add only, the field is replaced by the new provider's usual address only while it holds the previous provider's exactly (no trimming), whoever typed it; otherwise it is kept. On Edit it is never replaced. One function, `endpointAfterProviderChange` in `src/shared/models/provider-ui.ts`, decides this for the add-model window and the first-run window (whose behaviour is unchanged).
  - *The name.* The field is labelled "Display name (optional)". On Add it starts empty, its placeholder is the selected provider's fallback (`nameFallback`: the provider's label, "Local server" for Other), and a name blank after trimming saves as the fallback of the provider selected at Save. On Edit the field holds the stored name, has no placeholder, and its text is saved after trimming, `""` included. A provider change never touches the name.
  - *Save's reason.* Save is never natively disabled; while it cannot save it is `aria-disabled`, dimmed by colour, and one line under it says why, linked as its description. First match wins: the page refused (no line; Save is described by the refusal, as before); the Model ID blank, U1; the Endpoint blank, U2; an address starting `http://` (trimmed, any case) that is not a complete URL, U4; any other refusal of the address rule, U3; otherwise no line and Save works. The line is not a live region (P10(4)).
  - *The strings.* **U1** "Enter a Model ID to save." **U2** "Enter an Endpoint to save." **U3** "The Endpoint must start with http:// and name this computer as 127.0.0.1, localhost or [::1], with no user name or password." **U4** "The Endpoint is not a complete address. Check it, including the port number (for example http://127.0.0.1:11434)." The placeholders are existing names.
  - *The address rule's new home.* `validateEndpoint` and its types moved unchanged from `src/server/local-model/config.ts`, which re-exports them, to `src/shared/models/local-endpoint.ts`. The server relies only on its own calls, at resolve and again at fetch; the window's call decides only whether its own Save is live, and the server never sees it.
  - *Unchanged:* the window's two small-print lines and the Endpoint hint (P10(6)), Enabled, "Advanced parameters" (item 26b), the error block, and focus (item 27).
- **Should the add-model window say that smaller models answer less reliably?** Not added: the docs already say it (P2). A test pins its absence from the window, to be removed if Bryan wants the sentence there.
- **Accepted** (open to Bryan's change).
  - An entry already saved at a network address cannot be saved from the window until its address is fixed; its row's On/Off and Default still work.
  - On Edit, a cleared name saves `""` and shows as a blank-named entry does today. On Edit the name never follows a provider change, so an entry named "Ollama (local)" by the fallback keeps that name if its provider is later switched. Two models of one provider added with blank names share a name.
  - Save is a tab stop while greyed out, as in the first-run window.
- **What it closes, and pointers** (no claim of their own).
  - **Item 22** of the owed list in the amendment on switching local models on is closed: both windows fill in Ollama and LM Studio and not llama.cpp or Other, Save says why it cannot save, and the add-model window's Display name is optional with the first-run window's fallback, with tests.
  - **Item 26a:** the add-model window's half is done. The first-run window's half stays owed before the flip: it is the first window a new user meets, and it still saves a network address silently. Items 26b, 26c and 27 stay owed, unchanged.

**Amendment 2026-11-19 (agent colours as text, still dark).** Item 20 of the owed list in the amendment on switching local models on, built in one PR for #1123 from the builder brief on #1123 ([comment 7018460733](https://github.com/bloknayrb/tandem/issues/1123#issuecomment-7018460733), PR B). `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the two tokens are read only through `src/client/utils/agent-color.ts` (a test now pins that no other `src/client` file names them), and only for a record carrying `agentIdentity`, which a dark build never writes.

- **The reading.** Item 20 names `--tandem-agent-local-ollama` against its light-theme background. Reading it over `--tandem-agent-local-llamacpp` too (and so over LM Studio and Other, which share that token, Ruling L-5), over the warm theme (which inherits `:root`'s agent tokens) and the dark theme, and over every place either token colours text, is the reviewers' reading, not this ADR's.
- **Where the tokens are painted** (every `agentColor` / `agentTintColor` caller in `src/client`):

  | Consumer | Kind | Ground |
  |---|---|---|
  | Chat author label, `ChatPanel.svelte` | text | the identified bubble, `color-mix(in srgb, <agent> 10%, --tandem-surface)` |
  | Reply byline, `CommentThread.svelte` `.ct-author` | text | the annotation card, tinted by its root author (`getCardTint`): `--tandem-author-claude-bg`, `-user-bg` or `-import-bg`, or `--tandem-accent-bg` while it is the review target |
  | Card-header dot and note ring, `AnnotationCardHeader.svelte`; the reply's dot | non-text | the same card tints |
  | Peek-strip dot, `PeekStrip.svelte` | non-text | the rail, `--tandem-surface-muted` |
  | Margin leader, `marginLeaderGeometry.ts`; decoration underline, `extensions/annotation.ts` | non-text | the page, `--tandem-page-paper` (and `--tandem-page-bg` around it) |

  `--tandem-surface` and `--tandem-bg` are measured as text grounds too, as a margin for a later consumer on a plain panel.
- **What changed.** In `index.html`'s `:root` only: `--tandem-agent-local-ollama` from `oklch(0.58 0.11 200)` to `oklch(0.49 0.10 200)`, and `--tandem-agent-local-llamacpp` from `oklch(0.58 0.15 330)` to `oklch(0.52 0.15 330)`. Hues kept; Ollama's chroma 0.11 to 0.10. The dark literals are unchanged, because every dark pair already passed. High contrast does not override the tokens, and forced colours still map both to `CanvasText`.
- **Measured** (Ottosson OKLCH to sRGB, clipped; WCAG 2.x), worst text pair per theme, before → after. Ollama: light 3.44 (accent-bg) → 5.00, warm 3.27 (author-user-bg) → 4.70 (bubble). llama.cpp: light 3.98 (accent-bg) → 5.14 (accent-bg), warm 3.78 (author-user-bg) → 4.83 (bubble). Dark, unchanged: Ollama 5.00, llama.cpp 4.87 (both on the bubble). Every non-text pair is at least 4.66 after. `tests/design-system-impl/agent-token-contrast.test.ts` asserts every pair, the reply byline's included, in light, warm and dark (it was red on 27 text pairs, both tokens in light and warm, before the change); `tests/e2e/token-contrast.spec.ts` measures the plain-variable text pairs by canvas. The test reads the bubble's mix (the percentage and the colour under it, from `ChatPanel.svelte`'s `.chat-bubble.claude.identified` rule) and the card grounds (each `getCardTint` return and `AnnotationCard.svelte`'s review-target override) from their source rather than copying them, so changing the bubble to 15% turns it red (warm Ollama 4.37). It also sweeps the accent hue the Settings slider sets (`--tandem-accent-h`, 0 to 360), which `--tandem-accent-bg` follows, and asserts every pair at its worst hue: light's worst is at hue 17 on the review-target card, Ollama 4.94 and llama.cpp 5.08 (5.00 and 5.14 at the default 275); warm and dark's worst stay on the bubble, which the hue does not move.
- **Faded cards: measured, and accepted by the review session's ruling.** A resolved or dismissed card is drawn at 60% opacity (`AnnotationCard.svelte`, `opacity: isPending ? 1 : 0.6`), which composites the byline and the card's ground over the panel behind it. Measured over `--tandem-surface` and `--tandem-bg`, on every card ground: light, Ollama 2.43 to 2.57 and llama.cpp 2.44 to 2.58 (2.04 to 2.26 before this change); warm, 2.39 to 2.55 and 2.39 to 2.55 (2.00 to 2.23 before); dark, unchanged, 2.83 to 3.14 and 2.78 to 3.09. **The review session's ruling:** accepted, because the fade is the card's inactive state and applies to every byline on such a card, not to the agent colour; item 20 closes for active text.
- **Not measured.** Ollama's new literal, like its old one, sits just outside sRGB; the test clips, a browser maps the gamut, and the canvas-measured pairs agree with the test's numbers to within 0.02.
- **What it closes.** **Item 20** is closed. Nothing else in the owed list changes.

**Amendment 2026-11-19 (focus into and out of the add-model window, one save per press, still dark).** The review session's Rulings R-b, R-c and R-d, built in the first of two PRs for #1123 from the builder brief on #1123 ([comment 7018460733](https://github.com/bloknayrb/tandem/issues/1123#issuecomment-7018460733)); the second closes item 27. `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED` is unchanged, and nothing a dark user sees changes: the add-model window mounts only from the Models tab, which `SettingsModal.svelte` filters out while dark (pinned by `tests/client/local-providers-dark.test.ts`). No server, store or route change.

- **The review session's rulings** (built as written; Bryan can change them). **R-b:** while a save is in flight, the add-model window's Save reads "Saving…", as the first-run window's does. **R-c:** Delete moves focus to Cancel, not Confirm, so a held or doubled Enter cannot delete. **R-d:** after a confirmed delete, focus goes to the next row's Delete, else the previous row's, else whichever Add button exists.
- **What is built.**
  - *Focus in.* On open, Add and Edit put focus on the Provider select (the unavailable view keeps Close), once, so the dialog is read and Escape works at once. A provider change never moves focus.
  - *Focus out.* On every close (Cancel, ×, Escape, a click outside) and every committed save, focus goes back to the Edit or View button of the entry the window was opened for, looked up again by its test id (its row may have moved to another provider's group), else to whichever Add button there is, in the same task, before Settings' next-frame recovery. A save that does not commit leaves the window and focus as they are. A save that settles after its window closed changes nothing, except that when its rollback removed the focused button, focus is put back in the list the same way.
  - *One save per press.* Save is latched from the press until the save settles; a second press or a held Enter sends nothing. Meanwhile Save reads "Saving…" and stays `aria-disabled`, never natively disabled.
  - *Delete.* Delete focuses its row's Cancel (R-c); Cancel focuses Delete. Confirm hands focus on as R-d says, before the call when another row stays; for the only row, to the empty state's Add button once the row has gone. A delete that is rolled back puts focus on the restored row's Delete (its Cancel while the page is refused). **The review session's ruling, amending the brief's A5 text:** after a delete settles or rolls back, the tab moves focus only when focus is truly lost (on `<body>` or a detached element) or still on the element this flow last focused; anywhere else, the Models nav button included, it is left, and never moved while the add-model window is open. "Rollback wins" now means: when the tab does move focus after a rollback, the restored row is the target even if an Add button exists. A Delete pressed on another row meanwhile keeps its own pair. A Confirm whose delete is still in flight does nothing on a second press, so a doubled Enter sends one delete.
- **Carried items.** 4 (the edit window moved no focus in and restored none) and 6 (a double click on Save sent two saves) are built.
- **Accepted** (open to Bryan's change).
  - Deleting the only row while an earlier write is still queued: the row goes while Confirm has focus, Settings' recovery moves focus to the Models nav button, and it stays there when the delete settles. Likewise after a rollback that Settings had already recovered from.
  - Edit, a provider change, Save while another write is queued, then Cancel: focus returns to the row's Edit button in its old group, the queued move then removes that button, and focus ends on the Models nav button.
  - Any rollback of a delete that does move focus moves it twice (to the next row or the Add button, then to the restored row).
  - A conflict that makes the store reload the list can remove the row focus was handed to, and Settings' recovery then takes it to the Models nav button.
  - Cancel on a confirmed delete still waiting behind another write shows Delete again, and the delete still goes through, as before.
  - Closing the window does not cancel a pending save (as before).
- **Item 27** stays owed: the row controls' names, the first-run picker's subtitle and forced colours come in the second PR, which closes it. Owed to that PR too: carried item 4's third clause, Escape after a click on the dialog's own padding (focus is then on `<body>`, outside the dialog's `onkeydown`).

**Cross-references:** #1123 (engineering tracker, M0–M4), ADR-038 §3, ADR-040 §1/§2/§3, ADR-031 (origins), ADR-026/027 (the `author` model #1123 M3 extends), roadmap Wave 5M / v0.17.0.

---

## ADR-040: Audience and Monetization (Individuals; Same-Canvas Moat; Free Beta to One-Time License)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-12) — Supersedes the institutional-market and undecided-revenue framing in docs/positioning.md. All sections (§1–§6) are now fully accepted following legal counsel draft of the BUSL re-scope. **Amended 2026-08-18 and 2026-08-19 (#1346)** — the §3 gate changes shape from per-tool content-write to a surface gate (unlicensed = plain markdown editor, no AI at all, reads included); the 14-day trial is unchanged. Read both amendments below before reading §3 as current. **Amended 2026-09-08 (#1909)**: §5's evaluation period is 14 days for v1.0+, matching `LICENSE` and the trial. **Note 2026-10-08 (license review)**: see the 2026-10-01 note at the end of §5; the Change Date in `LICENSE` is capped by a fixed date that §5 leaves out. **Amended 2026-10-09 (Bryan, licensor decisions)**: `LICENSE` Parameters rewritten and the 2026-10-08 note superseded; see the 2026-10-09 amendments at the end of §3 (all future updates included, no update window or renewal), §5 and §6. **Amended 2026-10-13 (#1521)**: the surface gate is implemented, still dark. The third amendment, after the second, supersedes or corrects decisions 2, 3, 6, 7, 8 and 9 (and notes that decision 5's copy was rewritten early), and records that Decision F (symmetric read-only) is superseded. **Amended 2026-10-20 (key ceremony)**: the original signing key is retired as lost, every license must carry `keyId`, and licenses carry no `name`; see the amendment at the end of this ADR. **Amended 2026-10-28 (#2231, #1943)**: an installed licence that fails its check keeps the AI features on for a 14-day grace period, superseding decision 4's "no grace period", and a licence can be removed locally (`tandem deactivate`, Settings → License); see the last amendment.

*Correction 2026-10-22 (Bryan):* no legal counsel was engaged, so the status line's attribution of the BUSL re-scope to a counsel draft is wrong. The re-scope text was drafted in this repository and approved by Bryan, the licensor. The status line is kept as written; [licensing-terms.md](licensing-terms.md) already says no counsel is being consulted.

**Context:** Tandem shipped without a recorded audience or revenue decision. `docs/positioning.md` frames the market as institutions and (§economics) says paying cases "require either a hosted offering or a support contract… This needs a decision." `README.md` said "Tandem is free to use." `docs/roadmap.md` tracks "#394 Monetization" as "tracked outside engineering roadmap."
