The decisions themselves (`claimForWrite` — `checkWrite` from PR 2a — `decideTake`, `takeDocument`) are in `sessions/ownership.ts`; the refusal copy is `sessions/ownership-refusal.ts`; the mode of every tool is `mcp/tool-modes.ts`, pinned against the registered tools.
- **The operation queue** (`sessions/doc-op-queue.ts`, design §5.1). Flag-on, every MCP write to a document runs at its turn in that document's queue; the ownership check and the claim of an unowned document are the first thing inside it *(amended by PR 2a, below: the check is first; the claim takes effect at the body's outcome)*, and the body is given the **pinned document id**, so a tab change, a close or a Y.Doc swap while a call waited cannot move its write off the document that was checked. An idle document starts its operation in the caller's own tick. The chain survives a close and reopen. **The wait is bounded, the operation is not** (Bryan, 2026-11-11: "Time limit, then 'try again'"). A call that finds the document busy waits at most **20 seconds** (`DOC_OP_WAIT_MS`) for its turn and is then refused `DOCUMENT_BUSY`: it never ran, claimed nothing and changed nothing; it stops counting as queued, and is skipped when its turn comes, so the calls behind it keep their order. The bound covers every entrant: the write tools, `tandem_open` (one wait for the whole call, however often its target moves; a hop that finds its queue idle always runs), the take of a document a call just created (2 seconds, then the document is left untaken and the call still succeeds), and a lapse, which at the limit **runs anyway, out of turn**, so a dead owner's document is released at most 20 seconds after its grace ends, with one corner: if the operation still running is that dead record's own `tandem_open` and it later finishes, its take is decided afresh and can hand the document back to the dead record, which then holds it for one more grace (the take re-arms it) before lapsing again. That one change can land beside a running operation (its clock starts when it is queued, so possibly one that only just started); other sessions' writes stay serialised behind it and every decision re-reads the entry, so no two operations run at once and no entry is clobbered. It is not harmless in one case, which is accepted within the design's decision D4 (ownership coordinates sessions; it is not a security boundary): when the stuck head is the dead record's own operation, the lapse moves the document while that operation is mid-body, and the operation then completes as a write by a record that no longer holds the document — a save, a force reload, or a reply that records an answerer for a record that may already be deleted. A side effect to carry forward: a lapse to Tandem's Claude that lands while another record's open is in flight is followed by that open's take, which records the loss against Tandem's Claude, not the lapsed record; and PR 3b's `startOrWake` can then be called while another operation is mid-write. **Why 20 seconds:** Tandem's own stdio bridge gives up on a request after 30 seconds (`TANDEM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`) and answers the client itself without telling the server, so the wait limit must be answered, and delivered, before that; a test pins the limit at no more than two thirds of the bridge's default. **A cancelled call is skipped:** the MCP SDK's per-request abort signal reaches the queue through the tool caller (flag-on only), and a call whose signal is aborted when its turn comes does not run. The SDK aborts it on `notifications/cancelled` for the request (what an SDK client sends when its own timeout fires or the user interrupts; a request with id `0` cannot be cancelled, an SDK limit) and when the transport closes. **Fairness:** a refused call that retries joins the back of the queue, so behind a session that keeps the document busy with slow operations it is allowed to run, another session's write can be refused repeatedly and is told the document may be stuck when it is only busy. `details.waitedMs` is the limit that applied to the wait (for `tandem_open`, what was left of the call's one deadline), not a measurement. **What is not covered, each a limit of 1c:** (1) an operation that has started is never cancelled, so if the head never settles every later MCP write and `tandem_open` of that document is refused after 20 seconds, until the server restarts, and each refused call's body and arguments stay referenced until then (the user's own surface is unaffected, and ownership itself still moves by lapse); (2) a client that gives up while its call is *running* gets no protection: that write lands, and a retry is a second write; (3) a client that gives up without signalling while its call is *waiting* can have it run up to 20 seconds after it was queued. Tandem's stdio bridge is such a client wherever it answers a request itself after forwarding it, among them: its request timer when `TANDEM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` is set below the wait limit, a session swap after a lost stream (it answers in-flight requests at once and the old session is not closed), an upstream send that fails after the server already queued the request, and the answers it synthesizes for pending requests on a failed re-initialize, a licence refusal or shutdown. Closing (3) means the bridge sending `notifications/cancelled` wherever it answers for the server; the bridge ships live, so that is not in this dark PR and is a gate on the flip (the plan's flip row). Not every ownership change is queued. Four run where they are: a lapse whose wait for its turn ran out (above), and three that are synchronous by design — the tab-close point, the user-open default (`applyUserOpenDefault`, which PR 3b must queue) and the backstop that clears an entry whose record is gone (*corrected in PR 2a: this sentence listed three and left out the lapse; and from PR 2a the backstop clears only inside a queued decision, never from a reader*).
- **Refusals that never claim** (design §5.1) are marked where they are built (`noClaim`) and a claim the operation made is reversed if one is returned *(amended by PR 2a, below: no claim is made until the outcome, so there is nothing to reverse)*, rather than every argument check being hoisted ahead of the queue: the checks that already came first stay first (and are answered before the queue), the rest keep today's order. So a lone session's error precedence is unchanged flag-on (`tests/server/mcp-write-precedence.test.ts`, which also passes on the commit before 1c), and a refused call leaves no claim. A refusal by the annotation lifecycle wrote nothing and is marked too, so the four id-keyed tools claim only when they succeed. Refusals decided by live content, the disk or the save lock keep the claim. The line is drawn by what decides the refusal, not by its code, which is narrower than design §5.1's list (it files `INVALID_RANGE` under live content): an `INVALID_RANGE` for an inverted, fractional, negative or empty range is decided by the offsets alone and leaves no claim, one for an offset past the end keeps it. One argument-decided refusal still keeps the claim because it shares its code with a disk lookup: `tandem_restoreBackup` with a malformed `backup` name (`FILE_NOT_FOUND`). The claim and its reversal both go through the ownership-change composite (`setDocOwner`, causes `claim` and `claim-reversed`; the reversal also puts back, directly, a loss record the claim cleared), which is silent only while its steps 3 to 5 are stubs: the plan records, as a precondition of PR 2, that a reversible claim must not emit a notice, a badge change or a wake. *(PR 2a met that precondition: there is no reversal and no `claim-reversed` cause any more.)*
- **The id-keyed tools** resolve the document by annotation id first (#2187), then check it. A document that holds **no record at all** for the id has nothing to write and answers its own `NOT_FOUND` unchecked; any record that is present, whatever its type or audience, makes the call a write on that document.
- **Loss records** (design §5.3) are one per document (`lossByDoc`), not a field on the record: a loss stands only until the document changes owner again, so there is never more than one. It therefore survives the loser's record being deleted and re-created for the same identity, which is stricter than the design's wording.
- **Answered-once for comment threads** (design §5.5, R1.5). The item is the thread's latest user reply **that Claude can read** — the parent must project to the channel and pass the Solo hold, replies go through `channelVisibleReplies`, and a reply still carrying `heldInSolo` is skipped in every mode — so neither the item nor an `answered` refusal can disclose a private or held reply. `answered` is said before `lost`, `held` and `claimed` (§5.3: the operation-specific rows come first), to owners and non-owners alike, so a session is never told to take a document only to be refused the answer. The answerers are an in-memory map, one entry per item answered this server run, forgotten when the annotation is removed through MCP and on restart, kept across a close and reopen. An answerer whose record is gone still blocks its item until the user writes again. The lifecycle is not edited: the check and the record are in the tool wrapper.
- **`tandem_open`** predicts its target with the open path's own code (`resolveOpenPath`, `findOpenTarget`, both now exported from `documents/open.ts` and used by `openFromDisk` itself), decides the take before any reload, activation or authorship stamp, and passes `expectTargetId` so the core refuses (`OPEN_TARGET_CHANGED`) rather than act on a different document than the one checked. Those are the only edits to a shared core, and neither is an ownership check: `/api/open` and startup never pass the option.
- **ADR-011 is narrowed from here** (design §5.2): a read without `documentId` reads the active document; a write without one targets the active document and is refused if another session holds it — it never retargets — with the refusal saying that it targeted the active tab and listing the documents the caller holds.
- **What a refusal discloses.** Flag-on, any local MCP client learns a held document's holder label and display kind from a refusal, and from `answered` the label of the session that answered a thread. The first is design §11.3's accepted exposure, whose register entry lands with PR 5; the second is not in §11.3's wording and is for that entry too. No refusal, log line or thrown message carries an owner id (`ownership-source-pins.test.ts`; `ownership-enforcement.test.ts` checks every envelope).
- **Where 1c departs from the design's wording.** (1) §5.2 says a `focusParagraph` on a document the caller does not hold is ignored; 1c ignores it only when **another record** holds the document, so a focus on an unowned document works as it always has and claims nothing (pinned in `ownership-enforcement.test.ts`). (2) §5.3 gives `details.holder` as the document's holder; on `answered` it is the session that answered the item, which is the one the message names. (3) Loss records are per document, above. (4) `answered` is said before `held` and `claimed`, above. (5) A document created by `tandem_scratchpad` or `tandem_convertToMarkdown` goes to the caller by the take rule: `tandem_convertToMarkdown` can therefore take an already-open Markdown document another session holds as `claimed` or `default` when that document's file was missing from disk and the conversion wrote it; one held as `opened` or `assigned` stays with its holder and the conversion still succeeds. (6) A `tandem_open` of a path the open path refuses is run with a target no document can match, so it can only refuse; it never opens, reloads or takes.
- **Not yet enforced.** `tandem_reply` (and `/api/channel-reply`) are PR 2's and PR 4's: flag-on after 1c alone, a session refused a write can still answer in chat, so #2358's double answer is closed for writes and threads only. Presence arrives in PR 3b, so until then no document is held `default`.

**Amended 2026-11-11 (#2358 PR 2a) — a write's claim takes effect at its outcome; PR 2 is three PRs.** Still dark. PR 2a is the first of three PRs that replace the plan's PR 2 ("Inbox and replies"): **2a** (this one) lands what the inbox work rests on, **2b** the document half *(since split again: see the 2026-11-12 amendment below)* (not the live setup-entry PR that the plan and this record elsewhere call 2b; design §6: inbox scope, scoped acknowledgement, the handover, the handled filter, change notices, the `ownership` field, per-document delivery rounds, the inbox pin amendment), **2c** chat (design §7: per-message owners, reply and post acceptance, `settles` and `superseded`, the chat handover, labels, pickup). The split is because 1c came to several times the design's estimate and PR 2's file list was incomplete. `tandem_checkInbox` and `tandem_reply` are untouched by 2a.

- **This amends the 1c text above, and design §5.1, on purpose** (the builder brief for 2a says so and wins over both for this one point): "the ownership check and the claim of an unowned document are the first thing inside" a queued operation becomes **the check is first; the claim takes effect at the body's outcome.** 1c claimed at the check and un-claimed when the body refused on its arguments or threw. Both went through the ownership-change composite (`setDocOwner`), so once PR 2b gives its step 1 (the handover) and step 3 (change notices) their bodies, every refused call on an unowned document would have handed over and notified twice; and while the body awaited, readers outside the queue already saw the claim. With an inbox poll among those readers that loses a user's comment: the writer polls mid-write and is handed the document's items, another session's poll excludes the document and its mark passes them, the claim is reversed, and the items are below everyone's mark on a document nobody holds. This is the plan's precondition (a), met by the second of its two options. The argument checks were **not** hoisted ahead of the queue: that would change error precedence (`tests/server/mcp-write-precedence.test.ts`, unchanged) and would not cover a throwing body or a lifecycle refusal.
- **Mechanism** (`sessions/ownership.ts`, `mcp/owned-op.ts`). `checkWrite` (was `claimForWrite`) decides and writes nothing, except that it still clears an entry whose record is gone. A write that may claim registers a pending-claim token that **nothing reads to decide who holds or may claim a document** (only the settle of that same write looks its own token up). A body that throws, or returns a refusal marked never-claims (a tool's own or the annotation lifecycle's), drops the token: `setDocOwner` is never called, so no step of the composite runs, for a claim or for a reversal, and no loss record, grace timer or `sinceSeq` is touched. `reverseClaim` and the `claim-reversed` cause are gone. A body that keeps its claim (success, or a refusal decided by live content, the disk or the save lock) applies it **inside the queued operation, before it returns**, so the next operation in the queue finds it in effect; a continuation outside would land after the next entrant's check.
- **What is true at the outcome decides.** The caller becomes owner only if, at that moment: the document was not closed during the write and is open (the tab-close point cancels the token, so a document **closed and reopened** during the write also gets no entry: reopening starts from the default rule, design §6.3; this is stricter than "is it open now"); it has no entry (an unqueued change that gave it an owner during the write stands, and the writer is not made owner); the caller's record exists (one that **lapsed but still exists** gets the entry and its grace is armed again; one that was **deleted** gets none, since an entry for a record that does not exist could never lapse; one deleted and registered again under the same identity gets it); the caller holds no blocking loss for the document; and a Tandem's Claude caller is still present. `since` and `sinceSeq` are read then.
- **Applying the claim cannot fail the call.** The write has already happened; an error there would be retried by the client and written twice. A claim that cannot be applied is logged (rate-limited, in a line that names no record, beside whatever `setDocOwner` itself logged) and the write's own result is returned, the document staying unowned. The same holds for everything else that runs after the body.
- **Answered-once does not depend on the claim.** A successful thread reply records its answerer whether or not the claim took effect (the document closed, the record is gone, the claim failed). So an item can have an answerer whose record no longer exists; another session is then refused `answered` with no holder named, as 1c already allowed.
- **`sinceSeq` has a real source.** `src/server/index.ts` wires the inbox delivery registry's counter before either transport branch (pinned from source, since an unwired seam answers 0 and passes every unit test). For a claim made by a write it is the counter when the claim takes effect; `setDocOwner` takes an optional value that overrides the source, for PR 2b's claim by a poll (the counter before that poll's pass). It is read before anything is changed, and only when an entry is being written, so removing an entry never depends on it.
- **A reader never changes ownership.** The stale-entry backstop is an ownership change and now runs only from the two decisions made inside a document's queue. `tandem_switchDocument`'s check, the status focus check and the new scope read treat such an entry as unowned and leave it. No production path is known to create one.
- **`handoverSeq` outlives a close** (design §6.3 step 2): the tab-close point no longer deletes it. Nothing writes it before PR 2b.
- **The scope read PR 2b will call** (`inboxScopeOf`, and `inboxScope` in `owned-op.ts`, which takes the tool caller so the inbox code never reads an owner id): "holds", "may-claim" or "neither" for a record and an open document, pure, by design §6.2's conditions for documents — a blocking loss, presence, and **only while the document's queue is idle**, which is what protects the window above: a document mid-write reads as unowned, and without the condition a poll would claim it. Nothing in production calls it yet. **Decided** (the review session, on PR #2411, 2026-11-11): a caller with no owner record is answered "neither" (§6.2 is silent). Such a caller may still write to an unowned document (1c's rule) whose items it is never shown, and in 2b the generic flag-on suites that call as a recordless test caller would get empty inboxes, which is 2b's to handle.
- **`PATH_REJECTED` left `RENAME_NEVER_CLAIMS`.** `assertPathSafe` walks the disk to decide it, and a refusal the disk decides keeps the claim (the 1c bullet above); the set's own comment said its members were decided before the disk was touched. What reaches it: a link at the destination name, a document whose stored directory is a link (the open path stores the real path, so only where that resolution fell back or the directory was replaced after open), and a real path that leaves the allowed root; each such refusal now claims.
- **Decided on PR #2411** (the review session, 2026-11-11, where the brief or the design left room): a document closed and reopened during the write gets no entry; a record deleted mid-write and registered again under the same identity gets the claim; a writer that lapsed but still exists claims and gets a fresh grace (the brief required it); a caller with no owner record is "neither" to the scope read. Three reviewer proposals were not adopted and go to the 2b brief: a fourth scope answer for "unowned but mid-write", a bound on how long a stuck operation keeps an unowned document out of every poll's scope, and counting a mid-write record as holding state. Also for that brief: `sinceSeq` carries no registry epoch (`deliveryRegistry.reset()` zeroes the counter; apart from the registry's own constructor only `resetInbox` in `awareness.ts` calls it, and nothing in `src` calls `resetInbox`), and the settle reads the raw entry where the scope read goes through `readEntry`.
- **What changes for a session, flag on — two things in ordinary use** (the outcome cases above and the consequences below are the rest: they need a close, a lapse or an unqueued change to land during the write). (1) During a write on an unowned document, readers outside the queue see it as **unowned** until the write's outcome: another session's `tandem_switchDocument` to it is not refused and its status focus is not ignored (that focus stays on the document after the writer becomes owner, until the writer sets its own), read-mode `tandem_status`'s `holding` does not list it for the writer, and the id-keyed tools' held candidates (and a refusal's `details.heldDocumentIds`) leave it out. (2) A rename refused `PATH_REJECTED` on an unowned document leaves the caller holding it. Flag off, nothing here is reachable.
- **Consequences of an invisible claim, accepted for 2a** (the review session, on PR #2411, 2026-11-11; the 2b brief rules on each, since 2b is where they first have an effect, and on what `sinceSeq` at the outcome means for items registered during the write). A record in the middle of its first write holds nothing as far as the lapse rules can see: if it is detached and its grace ends, or the detached-record cap lapses it early (records holding nothing go first), and no transport carries it, it is deleted mid-write and the document ends unowned rather than passing through the default rule. An operation on an unowned document that never settles leaves the document unowned and its queue busy until restart; under the scope read nobody may claim it by polling meanwhile (PR 2b decides whether that needs a bound). A change that is queued for "every unowned document" (PR 3b's presence sweep) will see a document mid-write as unowned and must decide again at its turn.

**Amended 2026-11-12 (#2358, the document handover) — when ownership leaves a record, what it was shown is owed again.** Still dark. This is the first of the pieces the "2b" of the 2a amendment above became (the plan's rows now name them by content): **the handover** (this PR), **the scope PR** (inbox scope, scoped acknowledgement, claims by a poll, the `ownership` field and change notices, per-document delivery rounds, the inbox pin amendment, and the notice rule), and **the handled filter for comment threads**, which goes with #2360's PR that lets Claude reply on a user's comment thread. Chat is unchanged ("2c").

- **This amends merged text on purpose, in three places** (the builder brief for this PR says so and wins over them for these points). (1) The plan's row "2, documents" is three rows. The **notice rule** (design §6.3 step 2's use of `handoverSeq`, §6.4's last bullet) moved to the scope PR: until acknowledgement is scoped, "acknowledged" does not mean "the owner had it", so settling on it would lose an owner's notice that some other session's poll had acknowledged. The **handled filter** for comment threads (§6.4's first two bullets) moved to #2360's PR: until then no server path can produce the reply that filter keys on, and its "later reply" rule needs rework first (it would lose a user reply written during Solo). (2) **Design §6.3's "Mechanism" paragraph is not binding**, including its closing sentence about a tab close; its "Rule" paragraph is, as narrowed here. The mechanism described code that does not exist as written. (3) §6.3 re-owes items "not handled"; **this PR re-owes comments and replies whether or not they were answered**, because the handled filter is not here. The cost is a duplicate hand-out of an answered item, never a loss.
- **The rule.** Ownership "leaves a record" when a document's entry named that record before a change and does not after it: a take by another record (`tandem_open`'s, and the take of a just-created document when another record got an entry on it first), a lapse, the clearing of an entry whose record is gone, any other `setDocOwner` change from one record to another or to unowned, and a tab close while a record holds the document (`POST /api/close` and `tandem_close` alike). Not a claim of an unowned document, not the user-open default landing on one, not a change that leaves the owner the same. At that moment every user comment and user thread reply on that document that any poll had been handed is owed again to **every** reader: a cursor reader whatever its mark, a fresh cursor, a cursor-less poll. Items on other documents are untouched.
- **It changes only what counts as seen.** The handover hands nothing. Every hand-out, before and after, goes through the inbox's collectors and every gate they apply, so the handover applies **no gate of its own**: an item that is private, held by Solo or resolved at the handover and qualifies later is not lost because it was skipped, and forgetting an item the collectors will never hand out reveals nothing. It makes no Y.Doc write, changes no content, annotation or chat row, and emits nothing on any push path; `startOrWake` stays the no-op it is.
- **Mechanism** (`sessions/ownership.ts`, `mcp/awareness.ts`, `mcp/inbox-cursor.ts`). The ownership module calls a private `handOver(documentId)` from exactly two places, each behind its function's first-line flag return: `setDocOwner`, as soon as the entry is written or removed, and the tab-close point, as soon as the entry is removed. Both are synchronous with the ownership change, so no poll sees one without the other, and a change that fails before its entry hands nothing over. `handOver` calls the inbox through a seam `src/server/index.ts` wires at boot (`setOwnershipInboxHandover`, pinned from source: an unwired seam re-owes nothing and passes every unit test), then writes the document's `handoverSeq`. The inbox's half (`handOverDocumentInbox`) reads no flag and no caller. In order: it clears the document's two quiet-skip records, so no reader skips the document on a record made before the handover (design R4.5; the poll path gains no check); it retires the document's entries from the delivery registry in one pass (`DeliveryRegistry.retireWhere`, new: per-entry `retire` is quadratic over a whole scope); and it deletes the document's keys from the two cursor-less ledgers, by key prefix, because those ledgers can hold a key whose registry entry was already retired. That last step walks both ledgers whole (they hold every document's keys and have no per-document index), so a handover costs one read of the document's annotations, one visit per registry entry of the document, and one pass over the cursor-less ledgers. The cursor codec is unchanged.
- **Notices keep their entries.** Accept and dismiss notices about Claude's own annotations keep their registry entries and cursor-less keys at a handover; *what is handed after it follows the notice rule (the notice-rule PR's amendment, below), which settles the notices a holder was handed: the sentence "handed after a handover exactly as before it" that stood here ended with that PR.* A registry or ledger key is a notice's only if it is the key of a **current Claude-authored annotation at its current status and no other current record on the document has that key**, whatever that record's type, audience or author, a malformed one included; every other key on the document is forgotten. Ids are client-written, so a user comment whose id ends `#accepted` has the very key of a notice, and the colliding record need not be a comment at the handover to be one later (a note, a highlight, a private comment). *This widens the builder brief's rule, which gave the key up only to a current user comment; the review of this PR amended it (PR #2414, 2026-11-12), on the brief's own line "when in doubt, re-owe".* The cost is one duplicate notice in a case that needs a crafted id, and a private record with such an id can make a notice repeat once (it could already, as a private comment, under the narrower rule). A colliding record that is deleted at the handover cannot be seen and is not covered. A key left behind by a superseded status or a deleted Claude annotation is forgotten, which differs from before only by a duplicate notice if that status recurs. Telling the two apart needs the document's annotations, which are readable on every path, the tab close included (the document is still registered at the close point). When in doubt a key is forgotten: one duplicate.
- **`handoverSeq`** becomes the delivery registry's counter at the handover, also where the document becomes unowned (`setDocOwner`'s own sequence value is 0 there). Only a handover writes it; *the notice rule reads it (the notice-rule PR's amendment, below; until that PR nothing did).* It survives an unowned spell and a close and reopen, and is never pruned: one entry per document that ever had a handover, for the server run.
- **It cannot fail the change it rides on.** `handOver` never throws; a failure is logged in one fixed, rate-limited line that carries nothing from the error. What each failure leaves: an annotation read that fails (or a document that is not open: no production path is known) forgets every key, notices included, with its own fixed line, so notices may repeat; a failure in the registry pass changes nothing there (the pass decides before it removes), with the quiet records already cleared; a failure between the registry and the ledgers leaves cursor readers re-owed and cursor-less readers as before the handover. In those last two cases, and when the sequence source throws, `handoverSeq` does not move (after a failed annotation read the handover carries on and the mark moves as usual). **Two of these are a missed re-owe for one structure, not a duplicate:** a failure between the registry and the cursor-less ledgers leaves the cursor-less readers not re-owed, and a sequence source that throws leaves the mark unmoved after the items were re-owed (which for the notice rule that will read it is the duplicate direction, but is still a write that did not happen). Both are judged unreachable: every step after the annotation read is an operation on an in-memory Map or Set with no user data in its path, and the production sequence source reads a number off the registry. The handover's annotation read can also print two lines every poll already prints, now on a close or a lapse where nothing was read before: the "Skipping malformed annotation entry" warning, which names a client-written id, and the legacy-migration line; neither carries content. In every case the ownership entry ends as it would have. A handover whose registry or ledger step failed has not met the rule above for that document; nothing retries it.
- **Left open on purpose**, each in a row of `tests/server/ownership-flip-blockers.ts` (below; (c) is in the handled filter's row): (a) every poll still reads every document and acknowledgement is not scoped, so after a handover a poll by the previous owner, by any other session, or any cursor-less poll, is handed the re-owed items and its acknowledgement hides them from a **fresh** cursor again (a reader with a numeric mark below them still gets them); (b) for the same reason a session can be handed and acknowledge items on an unowned document that another session then claims with no handover, which is no worse than before; (c) an answered comment is re-owed like any other; (d) re-owed items open no delivery round, so `/health` can read idle while items are owed.
- **What changes for a session, flag on.** A session that closes and reopens a tab it holds, or whose record lapses and returns (a laptop asleep past the grace; a Claude Code restart, whose new session id is a new record while the old one lapses two minutes later; the detached-record cap; a session whose stream dropped while it keeps making requests, which is detached, so each request re-arms the grace, an idle spell past it lapses it, its next write claims the document again with no handover, and the next idle lapse hands over again: a re-hand on every write-then-idle cycle, though how often a dropped stream with live requests happens is unverified), is handed again every pending user comment on that document, including ones it answered; every resolved user comment that was ever edited (retiring an entry makes the edit look unaccounted for, the documented restart residual of `hasUnaccountedEdit`, now reached by every handover); and every user reply on a Claude-facing thread, whatever the parent's status. Not resolved comments that were never edited, and no notices beyond what it would have been handed anyway. Without ownership a close and reopen re-hands nothing. This is not narrowed by status: an entry kept for a resolved comment would read as seen to a fresh next owner after an Undo, which is a loss. Three consequences: a re-handed item is indistinguishable on the wire from a first hand-out, and one a session already answered can be answered by that same session again (answered-once refuses another record, not the answerer); a comment edited since it was seen arrives after a handover **without `edited: true`**, because the handover forgets it was ever handed; and any local process can cause handovers (two MCP identities trading takes, `POST /api/close` and a reopen, enough new records to trip the detached-record cap) and so duplicate hand-outs to every session, and with gap (a) its own poll can then hide the re-owed items from a fresh cursor; both are within D4 (coordination, not security), and the second closes with gap (a). The re-hand of thread replies covers threads resolved long ago, so it grows with a document's history until the handled filter lands. A restart that returns under the same owner id inside the grace is no handover and re-hands nothing. All of it is for Bryan's answer before the flip.
- **The flip blockers are one list.** `tests/server/ownership-flip-blockers.ts` holds every gap the flip must not land without, each with what closes it: (a), (b) and (d) above, the notice rule, the handled filter with the stripping of client-written Claude replies (design §6.4: a browser client could otherwise hide a comment from every inbox), `tandem_checkInbox`'s description (one sentence that stops being true with the scope PR, and one that is already not the whole truth flag-on), the lone-session re-hand (closed by Bryan's answer), the three gates 1c's review recorded in the plan's flip row, and #2360's switch. `tests/server/ownership-flag.test.ts` fails when the flag's literal is `true` while the list is not empty; the check is redundant with the literal's own `false` pin until the flip PR edits that pin. The plan's flip row points at the list rather than restating it. The setup-entry change the flip also waited for shipped in v0.31.0 and is recorded there as closed.
- **Not here.** Any "handled" or "answered" filter; any reader of `handoverSeq`; inbox scope, scoped acknowledgement and claims by a poll; the `ownership` response field and change notices; per-document delivery rounds; the cursor pin amendment; anything on the chat paths; any change to a tool's description or schema.

**Amended 2026-11-12 (#2358, the scope PR) — a poll reads only what its caller holds or may claim.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR amends merged text on purpose, in four places, and wins over them for these points: (1) **the scope PR is two PRs**: this one (scope for documents, scoped acknowledgement, a claim made by a poll, the `holding` and `notYours` parts of the `ownership` field, the inbox pin amendment and the rulings below), and the work that was then called the change-notice PR; the plan's row is rewritten as two rows. *(The notice-rule PR's brief split that work again: the notice rule is its own PR, change notices and `changed` the next, per-document delivery rounds the chat PR's; see its amendment below.)* (2) **Chat is the chat PR's**: the chat pass, chat acknowledgement and chat stamps behave as on master for every caller, and ~~a chat message never claims a document, whatever `documentId` it carries~~ *(superseded by the chat owners amendment below, Ruling 3: chat on an open document follows the document, and a poll that hands it, or an accepted reply to it, claims the document)*. (3) **A stuck write releases its document after 20 seconds** (Bryan, 2026-11-12, "Release it after 20 seconds"), amending the 2a amendment's "only while the document's queue is idle" (below). (4) **Design §6.2's and §6.8's mechanisms do not bind** (the handler reading the caller's record, the handler importing the scope predicate, the quiet-skip pattern as the way to mark an excluded document read); their outcomes do.

- **The rule** (design §6.2, for documents). Flag on, a poll by record X reads the open documents X holds and the open unowned documents X may claim now: no blocking loss, the `tandem` record only while Tandem's Claude is present, and the document's queue idle or busy past the bound below. No other document is read: no item on it is collected, refreshed or registered, its store is not opened for the pass, it costs no collection work, and nothing is logged as dropped. On a default poll an excluded document is read as empty: X's mark for it moves to the counter and it is never listed, so the cursor does not grow with other sessions' documents (R4.6: 300 documents another record holds and has polled add nothing to X's cursor or to its collection work beyond enumerating open documents). A named poll of an excluded document returns no items, lists it in `notYours`, and leaves every position where it was. A caller with no record reads no document; chat is unchanged for it.
- **Mechanism.** `mcp/awareness.ts` imports one name from `sessions/ownership.ts`, `inboxOwnership`, and passes the tool caller it is given to `inboxOwnership.begin(caller, openDocumentIds, now)` without reading it; it names no owner id and never reads the flag (`tests/server/inbox-cursor.test.ts`, `ownership-source-pins.test.ts`). `begin` answers the unscoped poll while ownership is off (read everything, acknowledge as on master, no field) and, flag on, a snapshot of `inboxScopeOf` over the open documents taken once, with the poll's one clock reading, which serves the acknowledgement, the pass and the commit. `responseField()` is read once, at the tool's registration: a server advertises the `ownership` field only if it was registered flag-on, the handler keeps that answer for its own backstop schema and for whether it emits the field, and the field is optional. So no response ever carries a key its advertised schema forbids (the SDK client validates against it with `additionalProperties: false`); the cost is that a test must turn the flag on before it builds its server. `inbox-cursor.ts` imports nothing from the ownership module and two records whose scope is the same get byte-identical cursors.
- **Scoped acknowledgement** (R1.4). From the cursor it passes back, a poll acknowledges entries only on open documents in its scope as it stood when the poll began, and on documents that are not open. A cursor-less poll acknowledges only what it was handed, since it reads only its scope. One session's acknowledgement never hides another session's document's items from a fresh reader.
- **A claim by a poll** (R1.5, R1.6). An unowned document the caller may claim that yields it at least one item, after every gate (Solo, private, notes, `admit`, and so on; a document whose only items are withheld yields nothing), is claimed in the handler's commit: after the response validated and after the ledger and chat commits, in the same synchronous run, with no `await` from the scope decision. It is a new cause, `poll-claim`, and an unqueued ownership change (`doc-op-queue.ts`'s header now lists five). At the commit it is applied only if the snapshot answered "may-claim" for that document, it is still open, still unowned (an entry whose record is gone reads as unowned), the caller's record exists (*unless the caller is Tandem's Claude, which needs none: the ownership change creates its record; corrected by the notice-rule PR*) and the scope rule still answers "may-claim" at the poll's clock reading. Those checks cannot fail while the run stays synchronous except through re-entrancy from an earlier claim in the same commit: a claim re-judges its record, which can arm a grace, which past `MAX_DETACHED_RECORDS` lapses records early (`enforceDetachedCap`), possibly the claimant itself; a lapse moves only documents their records held, which were never candidates, and its handover retires entries only. So `holding` is read after every claim (provisional before validation, replaced after, not validated again). `sinceSeq` is the registry counter before this poll's pass over the document, so every item the pass registered counts as handed (design §8.5). On an unowned document the only live entries below that counter are notices a handover kept, which §8.5 never counts, and what a handover whose inbox half failed left behind. *A document whose only items are settled notices yields nothing and is not claimed; one with an unsettled notice is (the notice-rule PR's amendment, R1.5, replacing the sentence that stood here).*
- **Nothing is left behind** (R1.7). When the response fails validation, a document's pass throws after registering, anything throws between the pass and the end of the commit, or a claim is refused at commit, the registry entries this poll created on that document (the scope's entries above its counter before the pass: only a pass registers in a document scope) are retired, and for a refused claim the cursor-less ledger keys this poll committed there are put back to what they held before (rather than deleted, so an earlier `editedAt` is kept), and an acknowledgement that poll made of an entry older than its pass (a cursor-less poll acknowledges every item it returns, a kept notice included) is taken back. A claim whose ownership change ran but left the caller without the document (a re-entrant lapse) retires only its entries: its handover already re-owed the rest. Entries made by earlier polls, chat entries and hand-out tokens are untouched. Flag off none of this runs.
- **A stale entry** (R1.1). A document with an entry whose record is gone (no production path makes one) is unowned and claimable to the scope read, as 2a left it; a poll that claims it runs a handover inside the commit because the entry existed, which re-owes what this very poll handed: a duplicate, never a loss, **for a reader that has been reading the document**. One bound on that sentence, judged unreachable and not closed here: a reader whose mark was moved by exclusion while another record held the document can pass that record's unacknowledged items, and if the record then vanishes without a handover (a stale entry) or a handover's inbox half fails, nothing re-owes them to that reader, and with nothing yielded no claim runs its handover. The same mark passes a notice the holder was handed and never acknowledged, which a handover keeps; that reader then acknowledges it and is never handed it. *The notice-rule PR settles the notice part (a notice the holder was handed reaches nobody, as intended) and accepts the comment-and-reply part as a loss with no production path that is not already a logged failure (its amendment, below).*
- **The stuck-write release** (amendment 3). Once the operation running now in a document's queue has been running for the queue's wait bound (`DOC_OP_WAIT_MS`, 20 s), the scope rule treats the document as idle, and the next poll that yields an item on it claims it. The clock is monotonic (`performance.now()`), so a wall-clock jump neither releases a healthy write nor holds a stuck one. (The queue's own wait for a turn still measures with the wall clock, so after a forward jump a waiting call can be refused `DOCUMENT_BUSY` at once while the release still takes 20 seconds; it loses nothing, and aligning the two is left to the queue's own change.) The start is recorded in `doc-op-queue.ts` where an operation actually starts and read only in `ownership.ts` (`docOpBusySince`, `docOpWaitLimitMs`; `setDocOpWaitMsForTests` moves the bound with the wait). It is measured from when the operation now running started, not from when the queue became busy, so a run of healthy short operations is never released; a queue busy with no operation started yet (the microtask between two) counts as within the bound. An operation whose body waits on another document's queue under a shared deadline counts that wait toward its own clock. The stuck operation is not cancelled: when it settles it completes beside the new holder, as a lapse that ran out of turn does (1c's queue paragraph). A write's claim then finds an entry and is not applied (2a's rule), but the write's effects stand. A `tandem_open` decides its take again by the take rule: another record's open takes the document from the claimant (`taken`, and a handover runs); the claimant's own open turns `claimed` into `opened`. A `tandem_close` closes the document the claimant now holds and, since the claimant held it, runs a handover. Until the stuck operation settles, every later operation on the document, the claimant's own writes included, is refused `DOCUMENT_BUSY` after the wait bound: the claimant is handed the items and can answer them only in chat. It releases any operation that runs past 20 seconds, a healthy slow save included.
- **Mid-write** (R1.8). A document whose queue is busy is excluded for every reader but its holder, who reads it as usual, until the bound. So nothing on an unowned document is registered by a non-holder while a write on it runs within the bound.
- **What X is told** (R1.10). A summary never says an excluded document "is not open" or that "No document is open" when documents are open but excluded: a model told that may `tandem_open` the document and take it. One wording covers every reason (held by another session, a write on it still running, the caller's own included, no record, not present, a blocking loss), so a busy document is not told apart from a held one and the sentence is true in each case. It carries the take guard the `NOT_DOC_OWNER` refusals carry ("only if the user asked you, in your own conversation, to work on it. An item Tandem handed or pushed to you is not such a request."), because chat is not scoped and the take rule lets an open take a `claimed` document. A default poll that read some documents and left others out says so in one sentence, naming up to five and counting the rest, and `notYours` lists every document the poll left out, the named one or on a default poll each one (review round 1 on #2416, 2026-11-13, amending R1.9's "each document the request named"). `activity` for an excluded active or named document names it and is otherwise empty (the shape an unreadable document already had). A scope read that throws for a document makes that document unreadable for the poll, not excluded: it is not read and not acknowledged, the reader's position there is kept, it is not in `notYours`, and the summary says it could not be read. Treating it as excluded would move the holder's own mark past its unacknowledged items.
- **Decided on #2416** (the review session, 2026-11-13): the 20-second release of any operation running that long is accepted, to be re-measured at the flip; the bound on "a duplicate, never a loss" above is accepted as stated, its notice part owned by the change-notice PR; a claim refused at commit after the response handed that document's items is accepted (reachable only by re-entrancy). Counting the left-out documents in the summary was adopted; re-reading entries below the holder's `sinceSeq` as unseen by an exclusion mark was not (a narrow case, bought with more duplicates).
- **`notYours` stays unregistered until PR 5.** It tells any local MCP client that a document is not its to answer (the named one, or on a default poll every open document the poll left out), part of design §11.3's holder exposure that Bryan accepted on 2026-11-05 (decision 4); its `docs/security.md` entry is PR 5's (a flip-blocker row).
- **Rulings on what the 2a amendment left to this PR.** A fourth scope answer for "unowned but mid-write": not added; R1.8 and the release cover it. A bound on a stuck operation: the release above. Counting a mid-write record as holding state: not done; a record deleted mid-write leaves the document unowned (2a's accepted consequence), and its items are then claimable by the first poll after the write settles or the bound passes. `sinceSeq` carries no registry epoch: accepted, nothing in `src` resets the registry. The claim's settle reads the raw entry while the scope read goes through `readEntry`: a poll may claim over a stale entry, and the handover that runs then costs a duplicate (above). What `sinceSeq` at a write's outcome means for items registered during the write: no non-holder registers anything on an unowned document mid-write within the bound; after it, a poll's claim comes first and the write's claim is not applied.
- **Closed and opened.** Closes the handover amendment's gap (a) (its test is flipped) and gap (b). New flip-blocker rows: `notYours` unregistered; the global delivery round, which a scoped poll closes for documents it did not read; a lapse to unowned wakes no session until PR 3b; chat unscoped until the chat PR; a document a poll claimed whose items its holder never answers; and the description and skill sentences that are false flag-on. The notice-rule and gap (d) rows now name the change-notice PR.
- **Not here.** `changed` and change notices; the notice rule and any reader of `handoverSeq`; per-document delivery rounds and `/health`; anything on the chat paths or `tandem_reply`; the handled filter; "waiting" itself; labels on `tandem_getAnnotations` or `tandem_listDocuments`; presence and wakes; any change to `skills/tandem/SKILL.md` or to the flag-off description.

**Amended 2026-11-13 (#2358, the notice-rule PR) — a notice a holder was handed goes to nobody once the document leaves it.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR amends merged text on purpose, and wins over it for these points: (1) the plan's "2, change notices" row is three pieces, named by content: **the notice rule** (this PR, with the items carried from the scope PR's review), **change notices** and `changed` (the next PR), and **per-document delivery rounds**, which go to the chat PR beside its per-message rounds because the open local-model series (#1123) suspends and discards the global round and is built first; the two flip-blocker rows about rounds now name the chat PR. (2) The handover amendment's "notices are handed after a handover exactly as before it" and "nothing reads `handoverSeq`" end here (rewritten above). (3) The scope amendment's "a document whose only item is an accept or dismiss notice is claimed" is replaced by R1.5 below. (4) Design §6.2's "the handover retires every such entry" is wrong for notices: the rule below replaces it for them. (5) Design §6.3's "Mechanism" paragraph and §6.4's `carryHeld` clause stay non-binding; the outcomes below bind. `DOCUMENT_REQUIRED` is not here (it goes with chat). *(It was then dropped: see the chat owners amendment below, Ruling 10.)*

- **The rule.** A notice is an accept or dismiss notice about a Claude-authored annotation, keyed as the handover amendment defines it. It is *handed to a holder* when a `tandem_checkInbox` response that validated and whose ledgers committed returns it to the record that held its document when the poll began, or to the poll whose claim of the document was applied (a push never counts). It is *settled* once the document's ownership has since left that holder (a take, a lapse, the clearing of a stale entry, a tab close while the record held it). **A settled notice is handed to no reader**: not the next holder, a later one or the old holder, with a fresh cursor, an old one or none, on every later poll, across a close and reopen (R1.1). **A notice never handed to a holder** — pushed only (design §15: a duplicate notice, never a second answer), held back while the holder held the document (an `admit` refusal, or in indeterminate mode a client-written `heldInSolo` stamp; in Solo itself no notice is held, since `hideFromAI` holds only user-authored records), or created while the document was unowned — is handed to the document's next holder on its next poll that reads it, whatever other sessions' polls did in between (R1.2). While a record holds a document without a break, its own retry with an old cursor (#2294) is handed again every notice it was handed and has not acknowledged, as on master (R1.3), including what the poll that claimed the document handed it (R1.4). An unowned document whose only items are settled notices yields nothing and is not claimed; one with an unsettled notice is (R1.5). A session whose documents never leave it is handed every notice exactly as on master.
- **A decision changed after the leave** (R1.3a). When a committed poll of the document's current holder (the scope snapshot said it holds the document, or its claim was applied) reads the document while the notice's record stands at another status, or at another version, the settled mark for the old state ends there: that poll is handed the new status if it is a notice, and a later return to the old status is handed too. A status that moves away and comes back with no such poll in between is the same notice (same key, same `editedAt`) and stays settled: nothing on the pull path can tell them apart, so no observer is built, and this is accepted. A handover while the status is moved away forgets the old key, as the handover amendment says. A record that is deleted stays settled, so an Undo of the delete restores a settled notice (until the next handover, which forgets a key with no current record). After that, one reader's view follows master's rules.
- **Mechanism** (`sessions/ownership.ts`, `mcp/awareness.ts`; `inbox-cursor.ts` is unchanged and reads no flag). A poll's scope (`inboxOwnership.begin`) gains `settledThrough(documentId)`: the document's `handoverSeq`, read live, or 0; the unscoped answer while ownership is off is always 0. It is the one reader of `handoverSeq`, inside the ownership module, and `awareness.ts` still imports only `inboxOwnership`. Under scope only a document's holder, or a poll whose claim of it is judged at the commit, reads it and so registers an entry on it; a claim refused or `changed`, a response that fails validation and a pass that throws retire what they registered. A handover retires every entry on the document except current notices and then writes the mark. So a live annotation entry at or below the mark is a notice handed to a holder the document has left. (This depends on the handler running synchronously from the scope decision to the commit: an `await` before the pass would let a non-holder register entries that later settle unseen. A document id the snapshot does not know reads as `"closed"` and is read unscoped; no open document can be missing from the snapshot while the run is synchronous.) On each pass over a document with a mark, after its annotations are read: an entry at or below the mark whose key is a current notice key (the handover's rule, colliding records included) at the record's current `editedAt` is **settled**, and the pass's ledger — cursor or cursor-less — reports it as seen, so it is not handed, not refreshed and not counted as yielding. `carryHeld` neither holds nor retires any annotation entry at or below the mark, whatever its record does, so the entry the rule depends on is never lost to a pass (two consecutive polls are pinned by every test). Entries there whose record now stands at another status or version are recorded, and retired — with their cursor-less key, where it still records that version or an older one — at the very end of a poll that committed and read the document as its holder. The handover also registers, below the mark, a kept cursor-less notice key that has no live entry, so a notice a cursor-less holder was handed settles for cursor readers too. **Bound:** the per-pass walk visits the entries at or below the mark and stops at the first above it; a handover leaves only current notice entries there, ended ones are retired by the first holder poll that sees them, and deleted records' entries and entries under a colliding key stay until the next handover forgets them (one per resolved Claude annotation kept at a handover and since deleted; a delete restored after that handover is handed again, once). The handover's own cost grows by one `assign` per kept cursor-less notice key that has no entry, inside the walk over the cursor-less ledger it already makes (R1.9).
- **The `changed` claim outcome** (R1.6). A claim applied and then moved on by re-entrancy leaves its claimant handed the notice but not its holder, so the notice is not settled and is owed to the next holder. The re-entrant handover writes the mark above the claimant's entries, but the claim's undo then retires them; and the undo now also puts back the cursor-less ledger keys this poll wrote **where they still hold exactly what it wrote** (the notice keys the handover kept), leaving every key the handover deleted on purpose deleted. Before this PR the undo kept every such key (`keepLedgers`), so a cursor-less next holder was never handed the notice.
- **Exceptions, each a possible duplicate and never a loss.** A restart forgets `handoverSeq` and the registry (nothing is persisted), so a settled notice may be handed once more (design §15). A handover whose annotation read fails forgets every key, notices included, and one whose inbox half throws does not move the mark, as the handover PR left them: the notice may be handed again, once. A key the handover forgets under the colliding-key rule is handed once. A claim not applied at commit (`refused`), or applied and moved on by re-entrancy (`changed`), leaves the notice owed to the next holder, whether it polls with a cursor or without. A reopen that changes a notice annotation's `editedAt` would make it a new version, handed again: the envelope re-merge (`annotations/sync.ts`, `mergeMap`/`pickWinner`) takes whole records, the file's copy or the Y.Map's, and synthesises no `editedAt`, so a reopen changes a notice's version only when the two stored copies disagree, and then the winner is a different stored version, handed as such. **Three more duplicates, accepted** (the change-notice PR's brief, R9.1; accepted by the reviewing session under Bryan's standing instruction that a call with no real downside is decided and documented rather than brought to him, and listed for him on that PR under "Needs Bryan"), each one duplicate, never a loss: (1) a notice whose key collides with another record (a client-written id) only after the handover is not settled while the collision lasts, so a holder may be handed it once more; (2) a notice only a cursor reader of the holder was handed, whose registry entry a pass of an older cursor of the same holder retired before the handover (its status moved away and back, or its record was deleted and restored, in between), has no entry to settle, so the next holder is handed it once; (3) a notice the holder was handed and acknowledged, whose Claude record the user deletes before the handover: the handover forgets the key (no current record), an Undo restores the record, and the new holder is handed it once. The flip-blocker row that held (1) and (2) is deleted.
- **Accepted losses.** A notice in a response lost in transit (#2294) is settled if the document then leaves the holder, and goes to nobody (design §15). A holder that was handed a notice and then went away for good leaves it settled. **A claim over a stale entry** runs a handover inside the commit, which treats everything handed before it as settled, the claiming poll's own notices included: the claimant's retry with an old cursor is not handed them again, and a notice the stale record was handed and the claiming poll handed again is told once and not repeated; no production path makes a stale entry (the 2a amendment), so no mechanism is built for it. **Comments and replies passed by an exclusion-moved mark** (the scope amendment's stale-entry bullet): when the holder's record then vanishes with no handover (a stale entry), or a handover's inbox half throws, nothing re-owes them; neither has a production path that is not already a logged failure.
- **The scope PR's cases, closed.** (1) A close of an unowned document, then a reopen: no handover runs, and none is needed — a notice handed to a holder was settled when the document became unowned, and one created since was never handed (tested). (2) An exclusion mark passing a kept notice: a notice the holder was handed now reaches nobody, as intended; a never-handed notice cannot be passed, because under scope only the document's holder **registers entries that outlive the poll** on it, so an exclusion mark has no entry to pass; the one exception, the cursor-less ledger on the `changed` path, is closed above (the notice-loss probe is kept as a regression test, and it passes on master too). (3) A poll's claim over a stale entry: accepted above. (4) A claim refused at commit after a cursor-less poll's acknowledgement: the kept notice of that case is now settled, so a later poll is handed nothing, yields nothing and attempts no claim; `ackedExisting` and `unacknowledge` stay, for the handler's catch-all after commit and for an unsettled older entry. (5) A mark moved by exclusion dropping held seqs: the held seqs it can drop belong to notices already settled, by the argument of case 2. The notice-rule flip-blocker row is deleted.
- **Unchanged.** What counts as a notice key; comments and replies, re-owed by a handover exactly as the handover PR left them; `handOver` runs from exactly `setDocOwner` and `onDocumentClosed`, and only a handover writes `handoverSeq`; `retireWhere` keeps its two callers (the end step and `carryHeld` retire per entry). Flag off, `tandem_checkInbox` is master's byte for byte, and nothing here runs: the mark reads 0, so there is no walk, no ledger wrapper, no `carryHeld` skip and no end step, and the `changed` undo is reachable only while scoped.
- **Also carried from the scope PR's review.** The four exclusion texts no longer say that nothing on an excluded document was returned: they say no comments, thread replies or accept/dismiss responses on it were, and that chat is shown for every document, so a chat message or a push may still be about it *(reworded by the chat owners amendment below, R9: chat is scoped, so it is a chat message this session answers that may still be about it, and so may a push)*; the take guard follows that clause. The take guard in `notYours`'s description and the scope-read failure's log line are pinned by tests. The doc drift the review found is corrected.
- **Not here.** Change notices and `changed`; delivery rounds and `/health`; anything on the chat paths or `tandem_reply`; `DOCUMENT_REQUIRED`; the handled filter; "waiting", presence and wakes; labels; any change to `skills/tandem/SKILL.md` or to the flag-off description.

**Amended 2026-11-13 (#2358 PR 3a) — the handle and the bind, still dark and inert.** Route L ships: probes 9 (2026-11-11, Claude Code 2.1.322, a direct-HTTP user entry) and 13 (2026-11-13, 2.1.323, stdio user-scope and local-scope entries, with `--settings` and with `--strict-mcp-config`) measured that the launcher's own `--mcp-config` entry named `tandem` replaces the user's. With the flag on, each spawn of Tandem's Claude gets a fresh owner handle (`th_` plus 32 base64url characters) and two run files under app-data `launcher/`: the run MCP config (one `tandem` server running the stdio bridge with this server's Node, its `env` holding the handle and this server's base URL twice, as `TANDEM_URL` and the launcher-only `TANDEM_OWNER_HANDLE_URL`, and no auth token) and the run settings file (every enabled `tandem@` plugin key set to `false`). The child is spawned with `--mcp-config` and `--settings` before its session flag; the bridge presents the handle as `X-Tandem-Owner-Handle` only to the server that named it; the server records, per transport, whether its `initialize` bound to the current spawn. The rulings of the PR 3a brief, recorded:
- **A. 3a changes no record.** A transport, bound or not, resolves to exactly the record it does on master; no retirement, demotion, private or unbound-child records, no re-pointing at teardown. In 3a Tandem's Claude holds nothing, so those moves protect nothing, and they made a resumed child lose its documents and split a child from them on a 404 re-initialize.
- **B.** What 3a adds on the server is one fact per transport (bound to the current spawn, or not), kept as the spawn's serial on the registry entry, never the handle; nothing reads it but tests. 3b is its first reader.
- **C.** The Settings list of bound transports is 3b's. **E (scope).** The handle and its run files, the two arguments, the secret env keys, the bridge presenting the handle, the bind fact, the per-spawn state and its teardown, the redaction, docs. **F.** The bind window, `lastRunBound` and the failed-to-bind judgement are 3b's; 3a's share of design R2.5 is that a child that never presents its handle resolves as on master and its `initialize` succeeds.
- **G and D3.** `--settings` disables every key starting `tandem@` set to `true` in the user settings file (in the child's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set, else the home default; `tandem doctor` reads the home default only), and in the project and local settings files of the child's working directory (read as hostile: no link, capped size, key count and time; a failure drops that file's keys), plus `tandem@tandem-editor` always. **D4.** `--settings` only with `--mcp-config`. **D11 and R1.5.** No usable bridge (a missing bridge script, a bound address that is not `127.0.0.1` or `::1` (D14, read from the address actually bound), any fail-closed step) means neither flag, one log line naming the step, and no file left.
- **H.** The handle is a bearer secret: never in a log line, error, route response, the bridge's stderr or a test snapshot; `docs/security.md` files it as posture, not as an accepted finding.
- **I (dark).** Flag off, the spawn's arguments, environment and files are master's, except that `TANDEM_OWNER_HANDLE` and `TANDEM_OWNER_HANDLE_URL` join `TANDEM_SECRET_ENV_KEYS` unconditionally. Three changes are live on purpose: that strip, the bridge's reading of the variable (which nothing sets while dark), and the log-sink redaction.
- **J.** Unmeasured things are assumptions, listed on the PR for a real-launcher smoke on Bryan's Windows PC before 3b, and held by a flip-blocker row. **K.** The spawn path is added to, not restructured (the local-model series also changes `supervisor.ts`).
- **L and D6.** The log sink redacts every `th_` followed by 32 base64url characters, unconditionally and with no guard on either side, and at the end of a clamped field any `th_` followed by 16 or more. A dark line with no `th_` is byte-identical; one with `th_` and a long run may be rewritten (accepted). Sentry's own `scrub` carries the same pattern (`src/shared/scrub-text.ts` is unchanged).
- **M.** The run-file write is the first await between planning and spawn. `planAndSpawn` re-checks after it what it re-checks after `buildPlan`, plus `child` and a shutdown begun during the write; the relaunch path (`stopAndSpawn`) re-checks the licence (recorded, then `LicenseRefusedError`) and a shutdown begun during the write (a new `RespawnResult` `why`, `shutting-down`, answered 503) — new behaviour on that path. Either way the run files are deleted and no child is spawned.
- **N.** The bridge script is the path `STDIO_BRIDGE_DIST` resolves, not the durable copy: the run file lives for one spawn.
- **D7.** A not-bound `initialize` is logged only when it carried a handle header, at most once per spawn and, with no current spawn, once a minute. **D9.** Design §11.3's two logging probes are PR 4's (they concern the identity selectors). **D13.** The boot sweep's age gate is one hour, and it runs only while this process holds the store lock.
- **Deletion points.** The run MCP config at the spawn's first bind (probe 11), at teardown and by the boot sweep; the run settings file at teardown and by the boot sweep.
- **The supervisor reads the flag itself** (`ownershipEnabled()`, per spawn), so the flag's importer pin gains `src/server/launcher/supervisor.ts`; the supervisor and the MCP server meet in `src/server/sessions/owner-handle-bind.ts`, imported by both, and the supervisor learns the bound address from `index.ts` (`getBoundMcpAddress`).
- **Rulings on the PR's "Needs Bryan" list (Bryan, 2026-11-13, review round 1).** A hostile project settings file setting `env.TANDEM_URL` and friends: accepted as a stated residual for 3a; whether Claude Code lets a settings `env` block beat an `--mcp-config` entry's `env` is unmeasured, and a flip-blocker row holds it until the real-launcher smoke measures that merge order before 3b gives the bound fact a reader. Pinning the URL variables through the run settings file's own `env` block: not adopted. The relaunch path's licence re-check running after `stopInternal`: accepted (with the licence refused there should be no running Claude, and the refusal is recorded so activation resumes it).
- **Review round 1's other decisions.** Every request Tandem's own programs make to `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` that carries the token, the handle or a licence never uses an environment proxy (#2422, `src/shared/loopback-fetch.ts`: it goes through undici's own `fetch` with a private `Agent`; other MCP clients that hold the token, such as a direct-HTTP Claude Code entry, are outside it and unmeasured; `tandem doctor`'s two probes carry no token and use a private `http.Agent` so a proxied probe cannot misreport a healthy server), so the bridge no longer needs a rule for when the proxy could carry the handle. Review rounds 1 and 2 first withheld the handle whenever the proxy could be on (`envProxyMayCarry`, with a plain rule rather than a model of `NO_PROXY`, Bryan's ruling); #2422 replaced that guard, which also covers the bearer token and every other header. A file whose ACL check refuses forgets its directory's verification, so the next spawn re-locks it, and `stop()`'s wait for the deletions is bounded at one second. The run config also blanks `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_SERVER_URL`. On Windows the `launcher/` directory's owner check, lock and re-read run once per process per directory identity, the files' ACL per spawn. `stop()` awaits the run files' deletion. No spawn becomes current while an earlier spawn's state is alive (`startOverlaps`, asserted by the spawn suites); `confirmBind`'s identity check is defence in depth behind that.

**Amended 2026-11-13 (#2358, the change-notice PR) — a session is told when what it holds changes, and why.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR wins over merged text it contradicts, and amends on purpose: design §6.6 ("`changed` drains the record's change notices"), §4.3 step 3 ("queue a change notice for both records, drained by their next poll") and §8.6's last bullet (rewritten in the design, marked there); `recordHoldsState`'s and `lapse`'s docblocks (change notices are per-record state that `recordHoldsState` does not count and a lapse does not move); this ADR's notice-rule amendment, whose flip-blocker sentence about two duplicates is replaced by the three accepted duplicates stated there; and the plan's "2, change notices" row (rewritten by content). The notice-rule amendment's "Decided on #2416" history stays. The rulings are the brief's; each is recorded here.

- **What `changed` is for (Ruling 2; Ruling 13; the brief's "Not in this PR").** `tandem_checkInbox`'s `ownership` gains `changed: [{ documentId, change: "gained" | "lost", cause }]`, exactly those three keys (Ruling 1), always present, `[]` when there is nothing to report (reviewing session). **`holding` is the truth; `changed` is an explanation and may be incomplete.** It tells facts and gives no instruction: no "do not", "carry on", "after this point", nothing about what to do with a lost document. A static text cannot say what to do about a document whose state keeps changing, and each instruction tried over three review rounds contradicted the code or a merged sibling text; the refusals and the exclusion texts own that guidance. Considered and not adopted (Ruling 13): reporting only changes the session did not cause itself (its own claims and opens are reported), counting a loss in `hasNew`, and keeping a deleted record's notices for a session that returns under the same owner id.
- **Which changes queue a notice (Rulings 7, 8; reviewing session).** Step 3 of the composite queues a `gained` for the record that holds the document afterwards and a `lost` for the one that held it before, each only if that record exists then; none when the holder stays the same record (X's own `tandem_open` of a `claimed` document); none for a claim not applied (refused on its arguments, thrown, closed or not open at the outcome, an entry already there, a gone record, a blocking loss, a not-present Tandem's Claude; a poll's claim refused at commit); none under an owner id with no record, so a record registered later under it inherits nothing (Ruling 6). Step 3 has its own catch: once the entry is written the change stands, a throw in queueing is logged (fixed text, rate-limited, no owner id, no document text) and steps 4 and 5 still run. **A tab close** (Ruling 8) does not run the composite: `onDocumentClosed` queues the holder's `lost` after its handover, in its own catch, so a throw there can never skip the handover; a close of an unowned document queues nothing, and a reopen applies the default rule and queues nothing for the record that held it before. `tandem_scratchpad` and `tandem_convertToMarkdown` take through `takeFreshDocument` and are reported as `tandem_open` is (reviewing session).
- **Causes (Ruling 9; Ruling 10, final form).** Six words, each a past-tense fact in plain English that reads by itself, none equal to an internal cause string: `your write claimed it`, `a poll of yours claimed it`, `you opened or created it` (`tandem_open`, `tandem_scratchpad`, `tandem_convertToMarkdown`: one cause for all three), `another session took it`, `its tab was closed` (true whoever closed it: the user, another local process through `POST /api/close`, or the session's own `tandem_close`), `Tandem stopped counting this session as attached` (the grace or the detached-record cap; it names no destination, since in 3b a lapse can hand the document to Tandem's Claude, and never says "released"). A cause states only what happened: never the document's state now, never an instruction, never who another session is ("another session" is allowed), never that the user chose. No cause no production path produces today is shipped (Ruling 9): PR 3b's take-back and every Tandem's-Claude gain or loss, the chat PR's post claim, PR 5's Release and Assign each have a flip-blocker row and must follow Ruling 10; until then such a change is told only through `holding`. No notice is ever queued for the `tandem` record before 3b. The descriptions live in the output schema (one `cause` description listing each word with its fact); the flag-on tool description stays byte-identical to the flag-off one.
- **The property and the cap (Ruling 3; reviewing session).** For a record that existed without a break since its previous poll that returned, with nothing dropped by the cap or lost by a throw in queueing in between, and whose previous response was not lost after `return`, applying this poll's `changed` to the previous poll's `holding`, in order, gives this poll's `holding`; changes that cancel out are both reported, in order. The final `holding` and the drained `changed` are read at one point of the commit, after the claims, with no ownership change between. Each queue keeps the newest 100 notices; past that the oldest go, and given-back notices keep their order and count toward it.
- **Draining (Ruling 4; reviewing session).** A notice is reported by the first poll of its record that returns, with or without a cursor, and by no later poll. **One record's queue is shared by everything polling as it** (several transports, or agents sharing one), so whichever polls first gets the explanation. A poll whose response fails the handler's validation drains nothing; a poll that throws after its drain point gives back what it drained, before anything queued since. A poll naming one `documentId` drains the whole queue, so its `changed` may name a document neither `holding` nor `notYours` mentions. A caller with no record reports `[]` and drains nothing. Nothing about the queue is in the cursor.
- **A poll's own claims (Ruling 5).** They appear in its own response, in `changed` and `holding`: the handler validates with a provisional `changed` (what the queue held when the response was built), replaces it after the claims and does not validate again; every entry fits the advertised schema, because the SDK validates after `return`. A claim refused at commit is in neither field; a claim applied and then moved on by a re-entrant lapse shows `gained`, then `lost`.
- **Where the state lives (Ruling 6).** In server memory, on the owner record (`changeNotices`), lost at restart. Not counted by `recordHoldsState`: a record whose only state is queued notices is deleted, and ranked under the detached cap, as one holding nothing is. It goes with the record, so a record created again under the same owner id starts empty.
- **The summary (Ruling 11, final form).** When this poll's `changed` has a `lost` entry for a document absent from the final `holding` whose last loss here is a take or a close, the summary gains one sentence of facts, appended after everything else (after the claims, after any exclusion text and `noDocumentInboxNote`): `You stopped holding a.md (another session took it), b.md (its tab was closed).`, five names at most, then `and N more`. No instruction follows it. A lapse is not named (a session whose stream stays down is lapsed every grace), nor a document lost and held again. A close's notice carries the file name the document had, stored with the notice and freed with it, never put into a `changed` entry, and used only when that close is the document's last loss here; otherwise a document that is not open is named by its id. `documentLabels` runs once per poll, before the summary, over its usual set plus the take and close candidates, and a stored closed name joins the collision count. `hasNew` keeps its meaning.
- **Accepted losses, stated.** `changed` may be incomplete (R1.3): after a restart; after a response lost after `return` (a throw in `withWakeAdvisory`, rejection by the server SDK or by the client, loss in transit), which loses its explanation, never the state (R4.4); past the cap; when queueing threw; and when the session's record was deleted and created again. A lapsed record whose transport is gone is deleted once its documents move, so its lapse notice reaches nobody: the lapse cause reaches only a record that survives its lapse (R6.3).
