A document lost by a take or a lapse and closed before the poll is named by its id (R8.3). **The changed-path undo** (the notice-rule PR's review, R9.5): on a claim's `changed` outcome the undo retires every registry entry above the poll's `counterBefore`, including what the re-entrant handover just registered for a kept cursor-less notice key with no live entry; such a notice is then not settled for cursor readers, so a cursor reader as the next holder is handed it once (a cursor-less one is not). One duplicate, never a loss; no behaviour was changed for it.
- **Also carried from the notice-rule PR's review (Ruling 12).** Three more duplicates accepted (stated in the notice-rule amendment above); the end step's `claimed.has(id)` arm, `undoPassOn`'s per-key guard and three "not handed" cases each now have a test that fails without what it pins; the `handoverSeq` pin catches any use of the map; `settleNotices`'s docblock covers a delete made before the handover; the exclusion texts and `notYours`'s description are pinned exactly.
- **Unchanged.** Flag off, `tandem_checkInbox` is master's byte for byte: no queue is created (records never exist), `setDocOwner` and `onDocumentClosed` return on their first line, the poll scope's unscoped answer has nothing to drain, no sentence is appended and `tools/list` carries none of the new words. `closeDocumentById` now passes the path it already holds; the file name is computed inside `onDocumentClosed`, flag on only. `handOver` runs from exactly `setDocOwner` and `onDocumentClosed`; `awareness.ts` imports only `inboxOwnership` from the ownership module; `inbox-cursor.ts` and the delivery registry are untouched. Not here: wakes and frames, step 4 (publishing), delivery rounds, chat, `DOCUMENT_REQUIRED`, the handled filter, labels, the skill and the flag-off description.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (#2358, the chat owners PR) — each chat message has one owner, and only it may answer.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR wins over merged text it contradicts, and amends on purpose: the plan's row "2, chat" (now three rows), design §5.1's reply row and §7.1's queue sentence, the plan's preconditions (b) and (c), `DOCUMENT_REQUIRED` in design §5.1 and §5.2 and the plan's R4.1, the scope amendment's item (2) and the code comment "Chat never claims.", design §6.5's pickup-clock sentence, design §7.2's first bullet as it reads with §6.5, the "chat, rounds and the greeting" renames, the texts that said chat is not scoped, and the comments that named this PR as still to come. The handover amendment's "Chat is unchanged" stays as that PR's history.

- **Bryan's decision of 2026-11-11, in his option's words: "Chat replies always get through (Recommended)."** A chat reply does not wait behind document writes; who may answer is still checked; a stuck save cannot silence chat. So `tandem_reply` never joins the operation queue (Ruling 2): the check, any claim, the per-message owner, the append, the notify and the read stamp run in one synchronous run, and a refusal comes before all of them.
- **The rulings built here.** (1) The plan's "2, chat" row is three rows: chat owners and replies (this PR); posts, batches and forged values (the posts PR); chat, rounds and the greeting. `tandem_getAnnotations` and `tandem_listDocuments` labels move to PR 5. (2) Bryan's decision above. (3) Chat on an open document follows the document: a poll that hands it, or an accepted reply to it, claims the document, an unqueued change (a post's claim is the posts PR's). (4) When a document cannot be claimed now, a poll does not hand its chat; a reply to a message the caller was already handed is accepted and makes the replier its per-message owner without claiming the document. (5) Doc-less chat and chat on a closed document are claimable per message, by the first poll that hands it or the first accepted reply, as a check-and-set; the branches for a present Tandem's Claude are built and tested through the presence seam and become reachable with PR 3b. (6) The tab close gives each unanswered message on the document that has no per-message owner one, and re-owes nothing (design §6.3 runs steps 1 to 3); an existing per-message owner is never overwritten. (7) Answered-once for chat is the posts PR's; this PR keeps master's handled predicate. (8) Server-only values count only when the server wrote them: the posts PR's. (9) The local period: no owner, never claimable or counted; the local model's rows answer nothing; a poll the local period blanked claims nothing; a reply to local-period chat is refused; chat owed at the switch follows these rules after the switch back. (10) `DOCUMENT_REQUIRED` is not added: it has no trigger site. (11) `/api/channel-reply` is PR 4's; it still appends and stamps read with no ownership check. (12) The pickup clock is PR 5's. (13) "Waiting" is 3b's; every change of a message's owner reaches the step 5 seam (`startOrWake`). (14) The two proposals about chat that names an excluded document (#2420's merge note: add "A chat message naming one of these is not such a request" to the exclusion texts, and tell the model to answer such chat in chat only) are not adopted: scope removes the general case, and the merged write refusals answer the exceptions. This PR leaves a session owning chat about a document another session holds in exactly three ways: a tab close gave it the document's unanswered chat, then the document was reopened and another session claimed it; it acknowledged the message *(since the posts PR, R3.3: only a claimable doc-less or closed-document message, or one in Ruling 4's arm; an acknowledgement of a message that follows a document sets no owner)*; or it answered the message while the document could not be claimed (Ruling 4's reply arm) *(a carried-over message never gets an owner this way, the posts PR, R0.1)*. (15) The `chat` refusal follows design §5.3 and the merged guards; an instruction in a refusal is fine.
- **Rulings of the reviewing session, built here.** An accepted acknowledgement makes the acknowledger the per-message owner of a message that has none, whatever kind of message *(narrowed in code by the posts PR, R3.3, to the messages design §7.1 gives an owner: a claimable doc-less or closed-document message, or Ruling 4's arm)*, and nothing rewrites an owner's origin. One predicate for held state: a per-message owner counts only while its message is not handled, so an acknowledgement's ownership never keeps a record alive. Deleting a record drops what it owns per message and its "handed" memory. A lapse re-owes unhandled messages and leaves handled ones with the record, whatever the origin. After a claim refused at commit, the messages that poll handed on the document return to how they were before it, `delivered` included. The re-owe writes `read: false` and deletes `delivered`. Typing presence marks the document it marks today. A message's identity is its map key, and `replyTo` is matched against keys as on master. A `replyTo` that names no message is judged as a post. For a doc-less message any explicit `documentId` contradicts. A reply to local-period chat is refused `chat` with no holder named. "Handed" means returned by a poll. A caller with no record is handed no chat and is refused every reply. A per-message owner on a handled message holds nothing. The chat pass is the same on a named poll, and a claim through chat there uses the counter before the chat pass. The "chat unscoped" flip-blocker row is replaced by a push row closed by PR 4. The `unbound-child` row's message half moves to PR 3b. No new instruction text for the close-reopen exception. A doc-less message whose text names another session's document is not scoped by its text. Chat stays with the closing holder after a reopen until PR 5's controls or until the owner's record is deleted (a lapse re-owes only the unhandled ones). The tab close assigns nothing to a holder that has lapsed.
- **What is built.** Per-message owners live in server memory only (`ownerByMessage` in `sessions/ownership.ts`, with their origin: poll, reply, acknowledgement, or the close of a named document), never on a row or in the session file, lost at restart. The inbox's one door (`inboxOwnership`) gains a chat scope per message and a reply gate (`replyGate`), so `awareness.ts` still imports one name from the ownership module and reads no flag. The ownership module reaches chat only through `setOwnershipChatSide` (handled keys, messages on a document, a row's facts, the re-owe), and the chat side tells it of deleted rows through `chat-row-deletions.ts`; both are wired in `index.ts` and pinned from source. The new cause word is "a chat reply of yours claimed it"; the internal cause is `reply-claim`. The `chat` refusal names the holder by display kind and label only ("The terminal session owner-ab12 answers this message. Do not reply to it."), or, with no holder to name, says why without saying another session answers. **A close whose holder has lapsed** (its move deferred behind a busy queue) assigns nothing, as Ruling (reviewing session) says, and re-owes the document's unanswered chat that has no per-message owner: the owner moves away from a record there (R6.1), and the deferred move that would have re-owed it never runs once the close deleted the entry. Without it a message the lapsed holder had read was owed to nobody (found by the PR's review). **From the reviewing session's round 1:** a poll whose claim held keeps that document's cursor-less ledger if a later step throws, as on master (the chat commit now runs after the claim loop, so the loop clears the undo log for every claim whose change ran); a lapse whose chat re-owe throws keeps the record's per-message owners and re-arms its grace, so the next expiry retries *(replaced by the posts PR, R0.7: a retry timer of its own, never a grace)*; a failed poll takes back only the "handed" memory it added; a reply refused on a document the caller lost (PR 5's blocking `released`, unreachable before then) says so rather than that an operation is running.
- **The interim until the posts PR, stated.** *(Each item below ends with the posts PR; see its amendment.)* Chat is scoped and owned, and a reply that names a message is accepted only from its owner. Still as on master, each a flip-blocker row closed by the posts PR: posts are accepted from any caller and stamped with the active document; the batch rule is master's, so one session's final reply can settle a message another session owns and has received, and if that owner then lapses the message is lost (master can do the same, less often); an acknowledged message is handled under master's predicate, and its acknowledger owns it (a live acknowledger that never answers keeps it); answered-once is not enforced for chat; a client can still write a Claude row with `replyTo` and hide a message.
- **Accepted, each stated.** A claim refused at commit after the response carried the message: two sessions were handed it. A claim over a stale entry re-owes and re-hands what the claiming poll just registered: a duplicate (on a cursor poll the poll's own entries are retired by the re-owe; the chat commit now runs after the claims, so a cursor-less claimant's stamp lands after the re-owe and the duplicate is a cursor poll's only). **An acknowledger's record deleted before its final reply** *(these losses end with the posts PR: an acknowledgement no longer handles a message, so the lapse re-owes it and its next owner is handed it)* (its transport went and its grace ran out, or the detached cap lapsed it early, or it had already lapsed, or Tandem restarts): its per-message owners go, and a doc-less or closed-document message with Tandem's Claude absent has no owner again, so the returning session's reply is accepted as a claim; the same message with Tandem's Claude present is Tandem's Claude's, never handed (it is handled), and the returning reply is refused naming Tandem's Claude; a message on an open document nobody holds is claimed by the returning reply, but while that document's queue is busy within the bound the "handed" memory went with the record and the reply is refused `chat` with no holder; a message on a document another record holds is that record's, the returning reply is refused naming the holder, and the holder is never handed it (it is handled), so nobody answers it until the posts PR; a session with no `X-Claude-Session-Id` (record `http:<Mcp-Session-Id>`) gets a new record on any re-initialize, and while the old record lives its final reply is refused naming its own old label (probe-contingent: PR 3b's real-launcher smoke probes which identity a real Claude Code HTTP session resolves to). A live acknowledger that never answers keeps the message. After a restart, an unanswered message that was read is handed to nobody, as on master. *(The interim re-owes such a row at the first change of its document's holder, since the sweep is empty after a restart; the posts PR's carried-over messages, R0.1, close that.)* **An acknowledgement whose apply throws** (#2430's item 16): its reply row stands and the message has no owner *(added by the posts PR, R0.7)*. Per-message owners are lost at restart. Chat stays with the closing holder after a reopen, until PR 5's controls or until the owner's record is deleted. A row whose `id` differs from its key is never matched by a reply, as on master; a row whose `id` equals another row's key is client tampering. Rename and save-as keep the document id, so no message changes from open to closed without a close.
- **Unchanged.** Flag off, `tandem_checkInbox` and `tandem_reply` are master's byte for byte (the reply gate answers `legacy`, the poll scope's chat answer is "owns" for every message), no re-owe writes, nothing is recorded, and the flag-off `tools/list` schema equals master's frozen dump.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (#2358, the posts PR) — posts, answered-once for chat, `settles`, and a client's chat writes undone.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR (two comments on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts, the chat owners PR's own text and code included, and amends on purpose: the plan's "posts, batches and forged values" row (rewritten); the chat owners amendment's interim (each item ends here, below); its narrowing of design §7.1 (an acknowledgement's owner, R3.3, below, marked in place above); its handled predicate (master's `answeredChatIds`, sweep included), replaced flag-on and rewritten in the docblocks that state it (`OwnershipChatSide.handledKeys`, `recordHoldsState`); its corrections listed under R0 below; the refusal copy's `body` where a merged form is false for a post; and the comments that named this PR as still to come (`ownership-refusal.ts`'s header, `CHANGE_CAUSES`' docblock, the ownership module's header). No further contradicting merged sentence was found.

- **Bryan's decision of 2026-11-11, in his option's words: "Chat replies always get through (Recommended)."** As it applies to posts (Ruling 2): a post, like a reply, never joins the document operation queue. Its check, any claim, the append and the notify run in one synchronous run with no `await` between the check and the write, and a refusal comes before all of them, typing presence included.
- **The numbered rulings built here.** (1) The plan's chat row is three rows; this one is "posts, batches and forged values", rewritten in the plan. (2) Bryan's decision above, for posts. (3) A post naming an open, unowned document the caller may claim now claims it: an unqueued ownership change with its own internal cause, `post-claim`, listed in `doc-op-queue.ts`'s header. (6) The tab close skips carried-over messages. (7) Answered once for chat: the global received-sweep goes flag-on; `settles` is written by the server and settles exactly what master's batch rule settles, within the replier's scope; acknowledgements and posts settle nothing; the answerer is the first record whose final reply is accepted; the cursor-less path gains the check; the answerer is keyed by the message's chat key in its own map, apart from the thread answerers. (8) Server-only values count only when the server wrote them: a Hocuspocus client connection may only add a user row with its own fields and the fixed values, and any other change it makes to the chat map is undone. (9) The local model's rows set no answerer and settle nothing. (11) `/api/channel-reply` is PR 4's; its final reply settles only its target. (12) This PR writes `superseded` and the pickup row leaves superseded rows out of its acknowledgements and its replies. (15) Every refusal a post can get is true for a post; an instruction in a refusal is fine. Rulings 4, 5, 10, 13 and 14 were built by the chat owners PR and are unchanged.
- **Rulings of the reviewing session, built here.** Carried-over messages are the rows restored at boot that were `read` and unhandled then, fixed for the run, never re-owed, handed again or given an owner, while a final reply or an acknowledgement naming one is accepted and sets no owner (a final reply sets its answerer if none). An acknowledgement that makes a record the owner of an unhandled message re-judges that record, as a poll does. A `replyTo` that names no message is judged as a post. A caller with an owner id and no record is refused every post with a form mirroring `REPLY_NO_RECORD`, checked before anything about documents. `lost` with a holder is a post's refusal too. "In the replier's scope" means owned by the replier at that moment. `settles` settles master's batch set exactly (sequence AND timestamp at or below the target's); step 2 adds only live entries, and only to the confirming record's own rows. R3.8 changes nothing flag-off. A post about an unowned document whose queue is stuck is accepted without a claim. A blocking loss wins over a busy queue and is refused `lost`. A post naming a document that is not open is judged doc-less and keeps the id as its stamp. The property of R4.1 and its three categories (server-only: `settles`, `superseded`, `inProgress`, `agentIdentity`, `chatNotice`, `delivered`, `greeting`, `localRun`, `replyTo`, and every field of a Claude row; fixed at add: `author: "user"`, `read: false`; the client's own: `id`, `text`, `timestamp`, `documentId`, `anchor`), pinned against `ChatMessage`'s keys. An undone client delete leaves ownership state as it was. What a client may still choose when it adds its own row is recorded below, not undone. A message's identity is its map key.
- **Ruling of the drafter, unconfirmed, built here.** An acknowledgement of a message that already has an answerer, from a record that is not its answerer, is refused `answered`.
- **What is built.**
  - *The handled rule* (flag-on): `mcp/chat-handled.ts`'s `settledChatIds`, pure over the rows: a real Claude final reply (no `inProgress`, `superseded`, `agentIdentity` or `chatNotice`) names a message in `replyTo` or lists it in `settles`. One rule for the cursor and cursor-less inbox (the cursor-less path gains the check), the scoped acknowledgement, every ownership decision (held state, the close, the re-owe) and #2419's owed-at-switch set; `awareness.ts` learns which rule from the poll's scope, or from `inboxOwnership.chatRule()`, and still reads no flag. Flag off, master's `answeredChatIds` with its sweep.
  - *Acknowledgements count for nothing.* The re-owe marks every live acknowledgement of a re-owed message `superseded` in the same `withInternal` write. An acknowledgement sets a per-message owner only on a claimable doc-less or closed-document message, or in Ruling 4's arm (origin "acknowledgement", never rewritten); an acknowledgement of a message on an open unowned document claims the document and sets no owner.
  - *Carried-over messages* (`carriedOver` in `sessions/ownership.ts`): taken once by `restoreCtrlSession` right after `restoreCtrlDoc`, through `captureCarriedOverChat`, which returns at the flag before it reads a row. Never handed (`chatScopeOf` answers "neither"), never re-owed (the handover's and the close's chat halves filter them), never given an owner (`setMessageOwner` refuses), never counted in `recordHoldsState` or by a lapse (judged from the set). Forgotten with the row (Clear chat).
  - *`settles`*: step 1 in the reply's `apply`, judged before the reply's own claim or owner write (so a claim never brings into scope a message only another session received), from registry entries already `received`, and written last, in its own guard, after the claim or owner, the answerer and the row's writer are recorded, so a failed `settles` write skips none of them; step 2 at the confirming poll, in `acknowledgeCursor`, right after `DeliveryRegistry.confirmHandOut`, which now returns copies (`base`, `seq`) of the hand-out's still-live entries it marked. The confirming record's own rows come from `replierByRow` (who wrote each accepted final reply row). Both steps write with `withMcp`. Because step 2 is written in the same step that spends the token, a poll that then fails validation or throws cannot lose it (R3.8); flag off the return value is ignored and the token behaves as on master.
  - *Answered once*: `chatAnswerer` (server memory, by chat key), set by an accepted final reply and by each message `settles` adds; checked after the owner. Refusal: `NOT_DOC_OWNER`, `reason: "answered"`, the merged copy ("{holder} already answered this. Do not answer it again."), `details.documentId` the message's or absent.
  - *Posts*: the reply gate's post arm (`postGate`): no owner id, then a not-present Tandem's Claude, then no record; then an explicit open document (a blocking loss first, then the holder, then a claim or, while its queue is busy, acceptance without one); then the active document if the caller holds it; otherwise doc-less, accepted from the doc-less chat owner. New reason `post`. New cause word "a chat post of yours claimed it" ("a tandem_reply of yours that named no chat message (no replyTo, or one that matched none) and passed this document's documentId claimed it"). A post's refusals come from `postRefusal`: `held` and `lost` with a holder keep the document forms, and every form adds "Nothing was sent. If the user asked you in your own conversation to say this, tell them there."; the `claimed`, `default`, no-holder `lost`, Tandem-caller `lost`, `not-present` and `post` forms are the post's own, none says "Do not answer this" or that another session holds a document nobody holds; a post judged so because its `replyTo` matched nothing, or because its document is not open, says so first.
  - *The undo* (`events/observers/chat-client-undo.ts`): the CTRL chat observer, when the transaction's origin is a Hocuspocus `Connection` (a string `socketId`, as `mode.ts` reads it) and `chatClientWritesUndone()` answers true (the seam `chat-client-writes.ts`, wired in `index.ts` to `undoClientChatWrites`, the ownership flag), puts back each changed or deleted row's earlier value (a plain object only), deletes an added row that is not a user row (a value whose prototype is not `Object.prototype` or `null` is not), and rewrites an added user row to its own fields and the fixed values, in one `withInternal` write from inside the observer (no `chat:message`; the push loop reads the repaired rows). For a delete it will undo, neither #2419's ledger nor the ownership side is told, so everything keyed by the row stays (R4.2), and the restored row keeps its class (an `add` of a classed key). A client-added non-user row it deletes goes through the delete arm as a server delete, but the observer never classes it at its add (`ctrl-chat.ts`, review round 1, item 12), so the delete finds no class and feeds nothing to #2419's deleted-class cap (corrected by the delivery-rounds PR, R0.3; this text said it fed the cap); the ownership side holds nothing for it. A failed undo is logged and its un-restored deletes are then told to both ledgers. **Found by this PR's security review, and built:** Yjs holds an update whose dependency is missing as pending and integrates it later under ANY origin (the next update, a clone for the session file, the Hocuspocus load merge), so a client could aim a struct or a delete set at the server's next write and have it land past the undo. Flag-on, an `afterTransaction` listener on the CTRL doc drops pending data after any `Connection`-origin transaction, and `restoreCtrlSession` drops any after the restore. This rests on a browser tab's updates depending only on what the server sent it or it sent first (CTRL is a star); that is reasoned, not measured. It amends the brief's R4.4 fact: a no-origin merge is not server-only content if pending data rides in it.
  - *The failed re-owe's retry* (R0.7): a lapsed record whose re-owe throws keeps its owners and gets a retry timer of its own (`reOweRetries`), never a grace, so nothing is armed through the detached cap and no record lapses early; three retries one grace apart, then one every four graces for as long as the re-owe fails (**an unbounded residual, accepted**: stopping would leave the messages owed to nobody). `recompute`'s grace rule is master's. **This departs from the brief's literal sentence** ("the retry is never armed from inside a lapse that the detached cap caused"): the retry timer IS armed there, but it is not a grace and the cap does not count it, so the outcome that sentence protects holds (no recursion, no second record lapsed early). Accepted by the reviewing session (review round 1, item 4).
- **Corrections to the chat owners PR (R0).** R0.1 carried-over messages (above); its interim re-owed such rows at the first change of their document's holder, which ends here. R0.2: its R3.3's "only a poll can re-arm a grace this way" becomes "a poll, or (from the posts PR) an acknowledgement". R0.3: its two flip-blocker rows on `/api/channel-reply` and on a never-answering per-message owner are reworded (an acknowledgement handles nothing). R0.4: its R6.1's "A forged client `read: true` is the posts PR's stripping" is true from here (`read` is fixed at add). R0.5: its R6.1 premise ("every remaining writer of `read` is either scoped ... or arrives with a row that handles the message (`/api/channel-reply` appends its Claude row with `replyTo` before it stamps `read`)") is false for a `/api/channel-reply` **acknowledgement**, which handles nothing yet still stamps `read`: on a message a re-owe made unread it hides the message from its owner's polls until the next re-owe; PR 4's permit closes it (row and Accepted). R0.6: its R3.6 ("An owner whose row is gone ... is dropped") does not cover a row deleted by a client connection and undone, which is not gone; its R9.1/R9.2 third way never applies to a carried-over message; its R6.3 (as #2430's builder changed it: a tab close whose holder has lapsed re-owes) skips carried-over messages. R0.7: the retry above; the licence test's doc-less half, the flag-off round-1 case, the `lost` sentence, the re-owe's origin, the throwing-poll fixture, item 15's count of `recordHoldsState`, the `chat.commit` and `commitChat` throw sites and the refusal copy's backtick import pin, each tested; and the acceptance "an acknowledgement whose apply throws" added to the chat owners amendment's list.
- **New state, each empty flag-off.** `carriedOver` (written only by `captureCarriedOverChat`, which returns at the flag), `chatAnswerer` (`setChatAnswerer` returns at the flag; reached only from the flag-on gate), `replierByRow` (written only in an accepted gate's `apply`, which flag-off never runs), `reOweRetries` and its timers (only from a lapse, flag-on). The undo keeps nothing. `ownershipSnapshotForTests` reports the first three, the timers are counted in `timers`, and `ownership-dark-enforcement.test.ts`'s `EMPTY_SNAPSHOT` holds them empty.
- **Accepted, each stated.** A carried-over message that was in truth unanswered stays unanswered until the user sends again; on the pickup row, while it is the latest message and under ten minutes old, an acknowledged one shows "working" and an unacknowledged one "Your AI has your message". Two sessions may both take up a carried-over message (both acknowledgements accepted, the first final reply wins), so the user may see two "working" acknowledgements. A `/api/channel-reply` acknowledgement on a message a re-owe made unread hides it from its owner's polls until the next re-owe (until PR 4). A future-dated or non-numeric client `timestamp` can push rows that carry `settles` out of the 200-row prune (a non-numeric one makes the prune's comparator NaN, so it may drop any row). A client-forged Claude row persisted in a flag-off run is honoured after the flip. A message whose reply's batch was not yet settled at a restart (step 2 had not run) is unread after it and handed again: a duplicate. A handover between a reply and step 2 re-owes the batch's earlier messages: a duplicate (design §15). A `/api/channel-reply` final reply settles only its target, so its batch's earlier messages are handed again (until PR 4). The 200-row prune may drop a reply row that carries `settles` while keeping what it settled, which then restores as carried-over if `read`. Two sessions may both post about a document whose queue is stuck. What a client may still choose when it adds its own row: `documentId` (which decides the owner), `timestamp`, `text`, `anchor`, and an `id` different from the row's key, even another row's key (a reply naming it matches the other row, as on master); a `superseded` forged flag-off shows on the pickup row (display only); a tampered session file can write any row. A client can still change a Claude row's `text` through the `chatStream` sidecar map, which `foldChatStream` folds in at save and restore (outside "the chat map"; it changes no handled or ownership state; offered to Bryan as an extension of R4). Chat answerers are lost at restart, so a settled message's owner may reply once more; a record created again under the same owner id as a deleted answerer is read as that answerer. Two records handed an identical seq list share one hand-out token; the first to confirm runs step 2 for its own rows only, so the other's batch is handed again (a duplicate). A step 2 that throws after the token is spent is lost (a duplicate). A failed step-1 `settles` write loses `settles` for the earlier received messages, which are handed again after the next re-owe or lapse (a duplicate; added by the delivery-rounds PR, R0.3). An undo whose write throws leaves whatever part of the client's change Yjs had applied, with no retry (logged; the un-restored deletes are told to both ledgers). A tampering tab's forged update reaches other tabs before the undo does (they briefly show it: a notification, the pickup row); its forged rows are visible to any other CTRL observer that runs before the chat observer inside the same transaction (none reads chat today); and one small delete set makes the server re-set every live chat row (the user's own editor, #2410's class). A `replyTo` on a post that names a key with no row still counts that key handled, so a later user row under that very key is never handed (master's sweep did the same; keys are server-generated ids).
- **Unchanged.** Flag off, `tandem_reply` and `tandem_checkInbox` are master's byte for byte (the gate answers `legacy`, `chatRule()` answers "sweep", the poll's scope is unscoped, `confirmHandOut`'s return is ignored), no row gains `settles` or `superseded`, client writes are not undone, no carried-over set is taken, and the CTRL session file is master's.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (#2358, the delivery-rounds PR) — delivery rounds keyed by document and by message.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts, and amends on purpose: the plan's row "chat, rounds and the greeting" (rewritten as "delivery rounds"; the greeting and handover gap (d) moved to the 3b row); design §3.3, §7.3 and §16's R2.2 PR column (the greeting lands in PR 3b); design §6.7's closing sentence, rewritten, with paragraphs added where it was silent (rounds with no key, a forward on a document that is not open, latency once rounds are keyed, abandonment); three flip-blocker rows; and the comments that described the round as one global round or named this PR as still to come (`ownership.ts`'s header, `delivery-state.ts`'s docblocks and `DeliveryJoinState`, `health.ts`'s docblock, the comment at the round's close in the `tandem_checkInbox` handler, `pendingDeliveryItem`'s docblock, `delivery-stall.ts`'s "What it deliberately cannot catch", the comment above `WakeStallBanner` in `App.svelte`), each now saying what holds flag on and what holds flag off. `docs/mcp-tools.md`'s round paragraph and `docs/security.md`'s `/api/channel-reply` paragraph gain the flag-on sentence. No further contradicting merged sentence was found.

- **The numbered rulings, as recorded here.** (1) The plan's chat row became three rows; this is the third, now "delivery rounds". (2) to (10) were built by the chat owners PR and the posts PR; nothing here changes them. (11) `/api/channel-reply`'s permit is PR 4's; an accepted acknowledgement or final reply through it closes a message's round here. (12) The pickup clock is PR 5's. (13) "Waiting" and `startOrWake` are 3b's: a re-owe or a reopen opens no round here. (14) Settled by the chat owners PR. (15) No refusal copy here.
- **Rulings of the reviewing session, built here** (each a sequencing call inside the dark series with no product choice in it). *The greeting moves to PR 3b*: there "the reply came from Tandem's Claude's bound run" is a fact about the caller's record, and per-call plumbing here would be made redundant by 3b's routing; its flip-blocker row is retargeted. *A handover's re-owe opens no delivery round here; gap (d) moves to PR 3b*: a round means something was sent and not picked up, and a re-owe sends nothing until 3b's wake; its row is retargeted and the source pin forbidding the handover's halves from touching a round stays (its denylist gains this PR's drop and close names). *When a round closes*: a document's round when a committed poll read the document and put nothing back on it at commit; a message's round when a committed poll handed it to a record allowed to answer it or found it refused by `admit`, or when an acknowledgement of it or a final reply to it is accepted through `tandem_reply` or `/api/channel-reply`; so a keyed round never closes on the poll of a session that did not read it, and a document's round closes when a poll reads it and puts nothing back, even when the item is gone. *A forward of an annotation item on a document that is not open opens a key-less round, and a close drops in the step that makes the document not open*, so no keyed round outlives its document. *After a drop* a drop records no latency, and `state` is `idle` until a later round closes. *Abandonment is master's rule applied to every round*: detach marks every open round abandoned, re-attach clears it and keeps each first clock, and a round closed while abandoned records no latency.
- **Rulings of the drafter, confirmed by the reviewing session, built here.** `latencyMs`, flag on, is the forward-to-close interval of the oldest round the most recent closing event closed, and null while any round is open (no client reads it). A key-less round is closed by any committed poll. A message round is keyed by the chat-map key, not the row's `id` field.
- **Choices of this PR's builder, where the brief was silent.** (a) The keyed state lives in `events/delivery-state.ts` beside master's one round, and every exported function branches once on `keyed()`: flag off each runs master's body unchanged. (b) The mode reaches `events/` through one seam, `setDeliveryRoundSeam({ keyed, isDocumentOpen })`, wired in `index.ts` beside `setOwnershipSeqSource` to the ownership side's new `keyedDeliveryRounds()` (which returns `ownershipEnabled()`, so `index.ts` does not join the flag's importers) and to the document registry; a source pin holds the wiring. (c) The chat key reaches the round in a `WeakMap<TandemEvent, string>` the CTRL chat observer fills just before `pushEvent` (`noteChatMessageRowKey`, a no-op unless keyed); `pushEvent` passes `deliveryRoundKeyOf(event)` beside the event to `recordWakeForward`; no payload or meta changes. (d) The poll's coverage is computed in the handler from the pass (`read`: a pass that ran and did not throw, or the quiet-document skip), the claim loop (`putBack`: a claim `"refused"` or `"changed"`), the chat commit (`handed`: returned minus `chatExcluded`) and both collectors (`droppedKeys`), and passed to the one `resolveDeliveryRound` call, which now runs last, after the notice loop; a test seam (`setInboxNoticeFaultForTests`) throws in the notice step. (e) A reply closes `m\0<replyTo>` right after its row is appended and before `gate.apply` (`tandem_reply`) or `notifyTandemReply` (`/api/channel-reply`); the close never throws. (f) The close's drop is in `closeDocumentById` right after `onDocumentClosed` and immediately before `closeDocument(safeId)`, with no await since the close's awaits (the diff review moved it before the untrack, so a throw from the registry's broadcast cannot strand the round on a document no poll reads; the handover between it and the awaits pushes nothing, by the source pin); Clear chat's is in `clearCtrlChatDurably` beside `forgetChatRows` and `noteChatRowsDeleted`. (g) `deliveryRoundKeyOf` falls back to the key-less round if deciding the key throws, so a push is never lost to it.
- **New state, each empty flag off.** `keyedRounds` (the open keyed rounds), `keyedLatencyMs`, `keyedClosed`, `keyedSuspended` and the `chatRowKeyOf` side table, all in `delivery-state.ts`; every write sits behind `keyed()`, which answers false unwired and flag off. Pinned by `delivery-state.test.ts`'s keyed twin ("the dark guard") and `ownership-delivery-rounds.test.ts`'s flag-off tests. Bound: one key-less round, one round per open document (a close drops it, and a forward on a document that is not open keys none), one per pushed chat row (a poll or a reply closes it, Clear chat drops it; the exceptions, client-written rows that no poll hands, are the flip-blocker row named below, and a row a client deleted whose undo failed keeps its round until a restart). No ownership-side state is added, so `EMPTY_SNAPSHOT` is unchanged.
- **Accepted, each stated.** A forward is recorded before any narrowing (design §15): a round can open for an event that no external consumer was allowed to receive, until PR 4 and Phase 3. A key-less round (a wake, or a round on a document that is not open) is closed by any session's poll; after a Solo release another session's poll closes the key-less release wake while a document's held items still wait on its holder. A `/api/channel-reply` reply has no caller, so any session's acknowledgement or final closes the round of a message another session owns, until PR 4's permit. An ask answered from a push with no poll, by an answer that does not name it, leaves its round open until the owner's next poll, so the banner can show beside a visible answer: a chat answer posted without `replyTo`, a comment answered with `tandem_annotationReply`, and (named by this PR's plan review) a batch whose acknowledgement or final names only the latest message, as the skill instructs, which leaves the earlier messages' rounds open. On master a second session's poll hid this. A client-written row whose `id` equals another row's key: a reply by that pushed id closes the other row's round (client-written rows only; bounded by #2432's undo). A document's round stays open when its forwarded item was deleted or resolved and its holder is wedged or idle, or nobody may claim the document, until a poll reads the document or the tab closes; no later PR is named to close it. `/health` reports one aggregate and cannot say which session's poll is overdue; `sincePollMs` is any session's poll, so `awaiting-poll` beside a fresh `sincePollMs` is expected. A handover's re-owe (from a take, a lapse or a record's deletion) opens no round until PR 3b, so `/health` can read idle while re-owed items are owed; a closed round is never reopened. A document's close drops its round, and a reopen opens none, while its items may still be owed, until PR 3b.
- **Rulings of the reviewing session on the PR's four items for Bryan (review round 1; sequencing and mechanism, none changes what Bryan asked for).** (1) A message round that no poll will ever hand and no reply will name stays open: until a restart for a user row a client deleted whose undo failed (the row is gone from the live map, so Clear chat, which drops the rounds of the rows it captures, never drops it); until Clear chat for a client-added user row under a key a Claude reply already handles, and for a carried-over key re-added after such a delete (each needs client-written rows). No fix here; it stays a flip-blocker row for Bryan. (2) A batch whose acknowledgement or final names only the latest message, as the skill instructs, leaves the earlier messages' rounds open: accepted as recorded above (on master a single session shows the same banner, so this is no regression). (3) The stall banner's wording and its lack of a dismiss flag on: the behaviour is what R2.6 asks for; the copy is Bryan's to review against keyed rounds before the flip, a flip-blocker row decided in PR 3b, whose start-or-wake changes when the banner shows. (4) Narrowing `/api/channel-reply`'s close to an unread user row: not built (a round open on a read row is essentially unreachable, and the cross-session case is PR 4's permit, already accepted).
- **Unchanged.** Flag off, `tandem_checkInbox`, `/health` and `pushEvent` are master's byte for byte: the one global round as `delivery-state.ts` has it, `/health`'s `delivery` payload, the forward and every event payload; the inbox's call that closes the round runs later (after the notice loop, which is empty while ownership is off) with an extra argument master's path ignores.
- **Corrections to the posts PR (R0).** R0.1: the post-refusal trace test now checks that the first seven refusals wrote no Y.Doc update before its reset (shown to fail with a `held` refusal made to write awareness, which the old test passed) and detaches every document it listened to. R0.2: the CTRL chat observer's two guards (an undone client delete; a client-added non-user row the undo removes) are each pinned at the deleted-class cap, and each test fails with its guard removed. R0.3: this ADR's undo text and the observer header now say an undone non-user add is never classed; the posts amendment's Accepted list gains a failed step-1 `settles` write; the `lost` and `not-present` post tests assert the code; the flag-off pending test clears `pendingDs`; the undo suite's `waitFor` keeps polling (stated on the PR).

**Amended 2026-11-17 (#2358 PR 3b-1) — Tandem's Claude's run state in the supervisor, computed and not consumed.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR (two comments on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts, and amends on purpose: the plan's 3b row and design §17's 3b row (now 3b-1 and 3b-2); design §3.3, §8.1's parenthetical, §8.2's "Sign-in and crash loops" and §8.7's "binds and gets past sign-in", and every other passage stating the old rule (each marked in the design, with a note under the §3.3 and §8.1 headings and an inline marker on each passage: §0's launcher row and §1's "Who is calling"; §3.3's bind-window paragraph, its sign-in failure bullet and its teardown paragraph; §4.2's rows "starts, binds and gets past sign-in", "becomes present without a start" and "Server restart"; §8.1's `lastRunBound` bullet and its two announcement bullets; §8.2's rule and "Sign-in and crash loops"; §8.7's failed-to-bind paragraph; §14's decision E and its take-back line; §15's sign-in and crash loops); the comments in `ownership.ts`, `doc-op-queue.ts` and `ownership-refusal.ts` that named "PR 3b" (now 3b-1 for the supervisor's half, 3b-2 for the consumer), and `resolveTransportRecord`'s comment (rule 0's routing is 3b-2's). Flag on, `supervisor.ts` now keeps a spawn record per spawn (numbered at `child = spawned`, reached from `onFirstBind` by 3a's serial), a 60-second bind window from a spawn's first turn, `lastRunBound`, and presence with its announcer, run-start and failed-bind events, exposed as `Supervisor.tandemPresence()`. Nothing subscribes outside tests: `setTandemPresenceSource` is still not called, so ownership behaves exactly as on master, flag on and off. Flag off, no record, timer, recompute, announcement or log line exists.

- **Rulings (owner)**, each picked from options put to Bryan on 2026-11-17. *D-own-1, "Present once connected"* (as shown to him: "No extra usage. If the first message then shows Claude is signed out, Tandem shows the sign-in notice and hands the documents on at that point, so one message can wait a few seconds longer. With a single session nothing differs."): a run starts at the bind, the latest spawn's first `confirmBind`; past sign-in is no longer part of becoming present; take-back (3b-2) runs at the bind; no extra turn is written to find out. This replaces decision E's reading "bound and able to answer". *D-own-2, "Accept for 0.32"*: the flag-on start cost on Windows (about 2.8 s at Tandem's start and 1.0 s per restart of Tandem's Claude, measured 2026-11-17, one sample each); #2440 batches the checks. *D-own-3, "Accept and record it (Recommended)"*: the launcher-folder cache residual, #2439, recorded in the register by a separate docs PR. *D-own-4, "Enough for 3b-1 (Recommended)"*: the smoke's one-key, one-file measurement (Tandem's `TANDEM_URL` reached the bridge over a project `.claude/settings.json` that set another) meets the 2026-11-13 condition for 3b-1; the uncovered cases stay a flip blocker. *D-own-5, "Accept and record it (Recommended)"* over "Fix it", then "Still accept (Recommended)": the three-principal limit of the broad-ACE check, #2441, recorded elsewhere. *D-own-6, "Accept the limits (Recommended)"* over "Require the heavier test": #2438's seam-pin limits; its flip-blocker row is deleted under the list's "a decision of Bryan's" clause, and the two pin comments now cite this decision. Binding here from earlier: decision 7 (presence and documents survive idle-stop, resume, crash-resume, relaunch).
- **Rulings (reviewing session).** The reading of D-own-1's "at that point": the `needs-login` trip makes Tandem's Claude not present at once, identified by its site, not by an error-code switch. *A*: a restart underway is exactly `restartTimer !== null || launching > 0`. *B and F3*: once the computed presence is false because of `lastError` with no restart underway, or a tripped breaker, it is true again only at the next run start; a restart underway does not override it. *C and F4*: Ruling K wins (add to the spawn path, do not restructure it); only presence's fields go through setters; a spawn record numbered at `child = spawned` and reached from `onFirstBind` by 3a's serial. *D*: a spawn with no run files gets the 60 s window. *E and F2*: the window starts at the first turn written to the spawn, and a #1169-shaped teardown (resumed, unconfirmed, non-zero exit) is never judged. *F and F5*: the first teardown cause wins, set just before the stop's first teardown step; a `fault` cause; judged at most once, at three sites (the exit handler with the code in hand; the callers of `releaseTurnDelivery` for a record not yet torn down; window expiry). *H*: `status()`'s `cli-unusable` clear is exempt, and the one exception to the one-writer pin. *I and F8*: a total announcer and recompute; the settle timer's lifetime; a suppression flag raised by `stop()` and lifted only by a `start()` that began after it, in a `finally`. The run-start event is emitted after the transport records its bind. *K and F6*: run-start and failed-bind events, presence announced before the run-start event. *O and F7*: a throwing `stopAndSpawn` records `spawn-failed` only when nothing else was recorded and no restart is pending; a child `error` is not a failed bind. *S1 and S2*: the keyed-round wiring became its own PR (#2438); the `env` guard was not built. *2026-11-17*: the window stays 60 s and a late bind still counts; an auth failure that is not "Not logged in" is Accepted for 0.32 with a flip-blocker row; the open smoke items are flip-blocker rows; start-or-wake moved whole to 3b-2; the register entries moved to a separate docs PR; the announcement timing keys on the R2.1 predicate that turns the value; the timing harness drives a faked clock.
- **Rulings (drafter's), confirmed or for confirmation.** 3b-1 and 3b-2 are separate PRs. The greeting classification stays with rule 0 in 3b-2. A bind after the window expired is a run start (confirmed by the reviewing session). A run start needs no teardown cause; a bind in a stop's or a fault kill's grace latches "ever bound" only.
- **Choices of this PR's builder, where the brief was silent.** (a) The spawn record lives in the `spawnOnce` closure and on `activeTurnDelivery` (so a stop path reaches the not-yet-torn-down current spawn's record), with a supervisor-level `latestRecord` and the serial map; 3a's `SpawnRun` and bind state are untouched. (b) The deferred bind point is a microtask queued in `onFirstBind`: `confirmBind` calls it synchronously and `noteOwnerHandleBind` then sets `entry.boundSpawn` in the same synchronous run, so the microtask runs after the transport records its bind (pinned from source, and behaviourally through the real server and bridge). `lastRunBound` is set there, not in `onFirstBind`, because R1.5 makes it true only at a run start and the run start's conditions are read at the deferred point. (c) The lift of the shutdown suppression recomputes at once (it announces the current value if it differs, not through the settle). (d) While suppressed, the failed-bind and run-start log lines are still written; only the events and announcements are not emitted. (e) R2.6 is a non-async wrapper named `stopAndSpawn` around the renamed body `stopAndSpawnSteps`, returning the body's own promise flag off. (f) `tandemPresence` is an optional member of the `Supervisor` interface, so the interface's test fakes need not implement it; the real supervisor always has it. (g) **For 3b-2:** failed-bind subscribers are called synchronously at the judging site, inside the exit handler or just before a stop releases the spawn's turn delivery, so a subscriber that wakes or writes a turn (§8.7's failed-bind wake) must defer that work, or it reaches the dying spawn (the seam's doc comment says so; review round 1, item 5). `diagnosticsForTests` sits on the production seam and is pinned unread by any `src` file but its definition.
- **Accepted, each stated.** 3b-1 computes presence that nothing consumes; until 3b-2 Tandem's Claude holds nothing flag on. Present at the bind: a signed-out child is present from its bind until its first turn's refusal, and a boot-resumed child with nothing owed that is signed out stays present until a message reaches it; whether a signed-out child's refusal comes before or after its bind is not measured, and if after, Check again while signed out announces present, then not present at once. An auth failure other than "Not logged in": such a child stays present though it cannot answer (known gap for 0.32; its flip-blocker row). The start cost with the flag on (D-own-2). The two ACL residuals (D-own-3, D-own-5). The bind can take a large share of the window (25,974 ms of 60 s on one fresh spawn, measured; depending on the user's other MCP servers, inferred): after a bound run, a spawn with a turn written to it that binds late shows one not-present at the window's expiry and one present at the bind; a plain relaunch writes no turn and starts no window; at boot a late bind shows only present. A fault-caused, resumed, unconfirmed spawn that exits non-zero is judged failed if a Start or Off tears it down before its exit, and not judged if its exit comes first. A fault-killed child whose exit never arrives, with no Start, Off or stop after it, is never judged. After a bound run, a crash-resumed spawn with nothing owed that cannot bind keeps presence true until a turn is written to it and its window expires. A resumed spawn with no run files and nothing owed at boot is never judged. Shutdown: nothing is emitted once `stop()` begins, so presence can read true after shutdown; a relaunch after `stop()` with no `start()` stays suppressed. Presence can read true while the licence refuses a running child (3b-2's consumers refuse through `aiRefusal()`). A locked start holds the operation lock through `prepareRun`, about 0.8 to 2.8 s on Windows (inferred). `ownChildSessionIds` stays supervisor-wide. `status()`'s `cli-unusable` clear does not mark presence dirty.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Deleted and replaced: PR 3a's assumptions (its smoke ran; the unmeasured remainder is a new row); the `env`-merge row (D-own-4; the remainder is a new row whose closure a measurement showing a project value wins does not satisfy); "Nothing reads PR 3a's bind fact" (3b-1 gives it a reader), reworded by delete-and-add as "Nothing routes on PR 3a's bind fact", closed by 3b-2. Added: an auth failure that is not "Not logged in"; resumes the smoke did not exercise. Deleted under D-own-6: #2438's seam-pin limits row. One wording fixed: the set of suites that read no round state is over-approximated and possibly incomplete. Every other row naming PR 3b is left for 3b-2.

**Amended 2026-11-17 (#2358 PR 3b-2a) — the supervisor's start-or-wake and `requestWake`, built and not called.** Still dark. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts, and amends on purpose: (1) the plan's 3b-2 row and design §17's 3b-1/3b-2 row (3b-2 is now 3b-2a, this PR, then 3b-2b to 3b-2e; the plan's rows name them); (2) design §8.4's Off test ("queue a `startOrWake` … ahead of an Off, and assert no spawn") cannot pass as worded, because the lock is FIFO and a pass queued ahead of an Off runs first, so the tests put the Off ahead on the lock instead, and the "user comment ahead of an Off" half goes to 3b-2b with the widened arm; (3) design §8.4's re-queue and "so the status reads Starting" (drafter's rulings 2 and 3 below); (4) design §8.4, a coalesced from-Off pass: when a chat accepted from Off and a `startOrWake` share a pass, start-or-wake does not carry the start past another attached AI, and today's arm starts it from Off only on its own terms; (5) design §8.4's "otherwise started if present" has no counterpart here: the pass does not consult presence, so like today's arm it can start from a state such as `stop-failed` with a standby set (gating starts on presence is 3b-2b's). Flag on, `supervisor.ts` now has `requestWake()` and `startOrWake(cause)`, optional members of the `Supervisor` interface, and a waiting decision option (`workWaiting`) that `index.ts` does not pass; a source pin (`ownership-source-pins.test.ts`) holds that no `src` file but the supervisor names either entry point or the option, apart from the ownership module's own `startOrWake`. Flag off neither does anything, and the start pass is master's.

- **Decided by the owner, 2026-11-17 (picked from options).** Bryan picked each answer from options the reviewing session wrote; none changes what 3b-2a builds, and nothing in it is visible to a user. *1, "Nothing new (Recommended)"* (the option's description: "The existing "Starting" status only. Richer wording can come with the later status work (#2167)."): while Tandem's Claude starts for something the user sent it. *2, "Yes, unless Claude is Off (Recommended)"*: after a stretch where a local model answered chat, Tandem's own Claude starts for the items left for it while another Claude session is open, unless Off; 3b-2a still builds today's rule and pins neither answer, and a flip-blocker row records the decision for 3b-2b. *3, "Hide while starting, then reword (Recommended)"*: the "your message is queued" banner with two sessions open is hidden while Claude is started or woken for that message, and if it still shows afterwards it says the message is waiting without promising when Claude will look (built in 3b-2e). *4, "Yes, a comment starts it (Recommended)"* (the question as put was scoped "(Tandem mode, Claude alone, idle-stopped)"; the option's description: "a comment you write in Tandem mode is you asking Claude for something, so it counts as asked-for work; Solo mode still holds comments, and Off still means Off"; not picked: "chat and replies only" and "only after my first Start"): for 3b-2b, a new comment starts a stopped Tandem's Claude. *The reviewing session's reading, open to his change:* because he was offered "Only after my first Start" and did not pick it, a comment also starts Tandem's Claude before his first Start.
- **Drafter's rulings, unconfirmed until the reviewing session confirms them.** (1) The split, with 3b-2a first and the widened arm moved to 3b-2b. (2) Only owed-by-start-or-wake re-queues a pass; today's `finally` never re-queued, and today's arm owes its wake synchronously, so an event that joins a pass that then declines gets no start, as today (departs from design §8.3 and §8.4's wording). (3) A start-or-wake-only pass does not read as Starting while queued, because setup's Apply answers "running" whenever `restartPending()` is true and would then start nothing for a pass that declines; Starting shows once the start runs (`launching`); revisable in 3b-2b (departs from §8.4's "so the status reads Starting"). (4) The waiting guard lives in the pass, not only at 3b-2b's call sites. (5) No per-event "ignore another AI" bit: the only thing that ignores another AI is owed-by-start-or-wake itself; if 3b-2b's `startWorthyFor` answers `ignoreOtherAi` per event, 3b-2b reintroduces a per-event flag under R2.4's rule.
- **R0.1, recorded here rather than by editing the 3b-1 amendment:** that amendment's builder choice (g) names only judging sites (a) and (b) for synchronous failed-bind subscribers; the bind window's expiry is site (c), where the spawn is not torn down (teardown cancels the timer), so `activeTurnDelivery` is that live, unbound spawn. The seam's doc comment now names all three; the rule that a subscriber defers any write, start or wake is unchanged.
- **Choices of this PR's builder, where the brief was silent.** (a) Both entry points are optional members of the `Supervisor` interface, beside `tandemPresence`, not of the presence seam (that seam is read-only state; these are writes). (b) The pass knows who owes it by two flags read at its start: `messageStartQueued` (today's arm; still what `restartPending()` counts) and `startOrWakeOwed` (start-or-wake), with a separate `passQueued` saying a pass is queued or running; flag off `passQueued` always equals `messageStartQueued`. A chat that finds a start-or-wake pass queued raises `messageStartQueued` and joins it. Today's arm is decided first, with master's guard in master's order (so `otherAiAttached` is called at the same points); start-or-wake's share runs only when that did not start. (c) `cause` is a supervisor-side union, `"document" | "message" | "solo-release"`, the three kinds of returning work design §8.3 and §8.4 name (a document's owner, a message's owner, the Solo release, which is 3b-2b's); a value outside it is logged as `other`. (d) `requestWake`'s licence and local-period checks sit at the spawn's writer (`requestWakeOnSpawn`), so any later caller in the file inherits them (plan security review); the public entry checks the flag and a live child. (e) A pass with a live child writes no wake once shutdown has begun (plan concurrency review M1: `stop()` raises `shuttingDown` before the lock, and its stop would kill the child the wake was written to). (f) The new log lines carry an error's constructor name only, never its message (a waiting decision from another module may throw a message carrying an id the log sink does not redact). (g) A flag-on pass counter, `startPasses`, on the seam's test diagnostics. (h) The inventory parses `supervisor.ts` with the TypeScript compiler API and keys each site by its innermost named function plus, for a `sendTurn` call, its argument, compared as sorted lists. Beyond the brief's two lists (`sendTurn`, now every reference and not only calls; the pending-wake writers), from the PR's security review: raw `stdin.write` and `serializeUserTurn` sites, the writers of a wake owed across spawns, and every reference to `onTandemEvent`, `flushPendingWake`, `writeGreeting` and `requestWakeOnSpawn`.
- **Accepted, each stated.** Built and not called: flag on behaves as master until 3b-2b. A fault-killed spawn's unanswered turn is not carried across its restart (the crash-carry exclusion; fault kills set no `stopRequested`): when the restart's spawn binds, 3b-2b's take-back at that run start (D-own-1) wakes it if anything waits; when the delivery-kill streak or the windowed breaker trips instead, nothing restarts and the item waits. `launching > 0` and the delivery-kill grace owe nothing; the next bind's take-back covers both. One extra wake turn when the user's Start or a relaunch wins the lock ahead of a start-or-wake pass that finds waiting true. A mid-turn `requestWake`'s deferred write (`flushPendingWake`) does not re-check the licence, as `onTandemEvent`'s does not today. Each start pays D-own-2's cost and binds about 10 to 26 s after spawning (the smoke). `startOrWake` returns nothing; PR 5 reads outcomes from `status()`. From the plan's review: a chat that joins a start-or-wake pass queued ahead of an Off is started by that pass and then stopped by the Off, so the chat is not answered and the mode stays Off, where master would have queued the chat's own pass behind the Off and started Claude from Off with on-click saved (concurrency review M2; the brief's rule that a joining chat queues no second pass forces it; pinned by a test; flag on only, and only once 3b-2b calls `startOrWake`). The same reorder holds for any user operation queued between the start-or-wake call and the chat (the PR's diff review): with a relaunch or Start fresh in between, the chat's start runs first and that operation then replaces the child it started (two starts' cost); with the user's Start in between, the start is a message start, so the breaker and attempt-history resets the user's Start makes are skipped and the Start finds Claude running. The Off case is a flip-blocker row (the review session's round 1, item 5): 3b-2b answers a chat sent after an Off as master does, with a test; this PR does not change the pass. A start-or-wake start that a local-model period holds while another AI is attached makes `status()` report `heldForLocalModel` for a start that today's switch-back rule will not make (it does not start a held message start past another AI); it becomes right when 3b-2b builds the owner's answer 2. A chat in the few microtasks between a declining start-or-wake-only pass's body and its `finally` joins it and gets no start (its wake stays owed), where master would queue a pass (L1). The pass's log names the cause of the latest call that joined it.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Added: after a local-model period, a start Tandem's Claude owes for its own work is still handled as today's held message start (not while another AI is attached); Bryan's answer 2 is that it starts unless Off; closed by 3b-2b. Added from the review session's round 1: a chat sent after an Off, behind a queued start-or-wake pass, goes unanswered where master would start Claude from Off; closed by 3b-2b. No row is closed or edited.

**Amended 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1a) — a chat's start is never decided by a start-or-wake pass.** Still dark, and still reached by nothing in production: `startOrWake` stays uncalled (3b-2a's pins are unchanged), and 3b-2b4 is the first PR that reaches it, behind a waiting decision that is false in production until then. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts; this amendment records each change and does not edit the 3b-2a amendment above. 3b-2b is split (the reviewing session's ruling, 2026-11-17): this PR, then 3b-2b1b (waiting, and the wiring that forwards ownership's step 5 to the supervisor), the start held by a local-model period, 3b-2b3, 3b-2b4 and 3b-2b5, with 3b-2b2 (the widened arm) at any point after this PR. The plan's 3b-2b row becomes those rows.
- **What changed (R3).** A chat accepted by today's arm (the standby watch's chat, or a user reply on Claude's thread) is handled as on master, as if no start-or-wake pass existed. It coalesces with a pass today's arm already owes, queued or running (master's `if (messageStartQueued) return;`); otherwise it gets its own pass, queued at acceptance behind every lock operation queued before it, including when only a start-or-wake pass is queued or running. Mechanism: who owes today's arm is fixed per pass when it is queued (`startForMessage(owedByMessage)`: `true` from the watch only, `false` from `queueStartPass`), so a start-or-wake pass never decides a chat; `messageStartQueued` keeps its name and is raised only by the watch and lowered only by that pass's own `finally`, so `restartPending()`, `status()` and `startAfterSetup` read Starting (or "running") from the chat's acceptance until its own pass ends, and a start-or-wake-only pass still does not (3b-2a's ruling 3). 3b-2a's `passQueued` flag is replaced by a count, `passesQueued`: `startOrWake` joins any queued or running pass, the next pass to reach its body consumes `startOrWakeOwed`, and a `finally` re-queues for start-or-wake only when no other pass is in flight. Start-or-wake's share still runs only when today's arm declined. At most two passes are in flight, and then always a start-or-wake-only pass followed by the chat's.
- **Rejected alternatives (builder's choices).** For per-pass state: a queue of pass records, rejected because there is only ever one pass today's arm owes (the watch coalesces into it), so a boolean fixed at queue time plus the existing flag says everything, and because a record's fields would hide the state from the inventory's bare-identifier matchers. For counting passes: keeping one boolean and recomputing it in a `finally`, rejected because it is wrong whichever of the earlier pass's `finally` and the later pass's body runs first.
- **Flag off.** The watch, the pass, `restartPending()` and `startAfterSetup` are master's: only the watch queues a pass, so `owedByMessage` is always `true` there, as master's `messageStartQueued` always was at the guard; master's `if (passQueued) return;` in the watch was dead flag off and is removed; the counter moves 0→1→0 exactly where `passQueued` did and its two readers (`startOrWake`, which returns on the flag first, and the re-queue, which reads `startOrWakeOwed` first, never raised flag off) are unreached. No new await, timer, lock turn or log line. The two guards are structural: a flag-off lock turn is not observable to any test, so the evidence is the flag-off twins named on the PR ("Coalescing with a running pass" and the row's case's twin) plus the unchanged flag-off suites.
- **Items carried from #2452 (R0).** R0.1: the in-file inventory now lists the writers of `startOrWakeOwed`, the writers of `messageStartQueued`, and every reference to `startOrWakeStep`, `queueStartPass` and `startForMessage`, with each `startForMessage` call keyed by its argument (`true` is the authority to run today's arm, which starts from Off). R0.2: the flag-off start-or-wake case's evidence is the waiting double, not the flag-on-only pass counter. R0.3: the no-child `requestWake` case checks only that nothing spawns. R0.4: the brief expected a fresh spawn after `tripBreaker`; the harness gives a resumed one (the crash loop alternates fresh and resumed spawns, since a resumed child's unconfirmed non-zero exit clears the saved session and the fresh spawn after it saves a new one), so the "no wake" half stays, asserted behind an assertion that the spawn resumes. R0.5: the three breaker cases get an explicit 60 s timeout (measured at 36-80 ms each on Linux).
- **Recorded against the 3b-2a amendment and brief.** (1) The plan's 3b-2b row is split as above. (2) Builder choice (b), "A chat that finds a start-or-wake pass queued raises `messageStartQueued` and joins it", is reversed. (3) Drafter's ruling (2) is narrowed: a chat no longer joins a start-or-wake pass; only owed-by-start-or-wake re-queues, still. (4) Item (4), a coalesced from-Off pass, keeps its case, rebuilt: from idle-stopped, the user's Off, then a chat (accepted while the standby still reads idle), then a `startOrWake` that joins the chat's pass; with no other AI today's arm starts Claude from Off and saves on-click; with another AI attached by the pass today's arm declines and start-or-wake's share returns at its Off check, so there is no spawn and the mode stays Off. (5) The 3b-2a brief's "Facts" line ("When a chat joins a pass start-or-wake queued, it must ... also not queue a second pass") is reversed, and its R2.7 third bullet no longer holds for a pass only start-or-wake owes. (6) Design §8.4's mechanism ("the same single queued start ... sets the one queued-start flag") and §8.3's mechanism paragraph no longer hold, and are marked in the design; `docs/architecture.md`'s burst bullet says so.
- **The 3b-2a amendment's Accepted paragraph.** *Withdrawn:* M2 (a chat that joins a start-or-wake pass queued ahead of an Off goes unanswered); the chat half of the reorder sentence; and L1 (a chat in the microtasks before a declining pass's `finally`). *Kept, rescoped to the pass's own start:* with waiting true, a start-or-wake pass queued ahead of a relaunch, Start fresh or the user's Start still starts Claude first, which costs two starts; with the user's Start, the Start finds Claude running, and its breaker and attempt-history resets are skipped. *Unchanged here:* the `heldForLocalModel` sentence (the local-period PR's).
- **Accepted, each stated.** A pass's own start ahead of a queued user operation (above). The row's case with waiting true costs two spawns: the start-or-wake pass's, then the chat's own from Off; that is master's outcome for the chat, plus the pass's start. Reached only behind the waiting decision: until 3b-2b4 this behaviour is exercised by tests only. From the plan's review (not caused by this PR, noted so it is not read as covered): a chat accepted in the microtasks between the chat's own pass's body and its `finally` still coalesces into it and gets no start if that pass declined, which is master's live flag-off behaviour (L1's withdrawal covers only a start-or-wake pass), and flag on that window spans the share's respawn when a call has joined the chat's pass and today's arm declined there (for example, another AI attached), so a chat accepted during that respawn coalesces and gets no attempt of its own if the share's start fails, the same class as master's coalescing into a running pass whose attempt fails; and a chat accepted behind an Off then the user's Start gets no wake turn, on master too (the Off clears the owed wake, #1866, the Start resumes writing none, and the chat's pass finds Claude running).
- **Decided by the owner, 2026-11-17 (picked from options the reviewing session wrote; quoted as the option's text, not his words).** Neither changes what this PR builds. *For the local-period start:* shown a condition he had not been shown (at the moment chat switches back, Tandem checks again whether anything is still waiting for its Claude and starts it only if so, for example not if he resolved or deleted those comments during the local period), he picked "Yes, only if still waiting (Recommended)", whose description reads: "No point starting Claude for work that is gone. If something is still waiting, it starts as you picked (unless Off)." Not picked: "Always start it". This refines answer 2 above, "Yes, unless Claude is Off (Recommended)". *For 3b-2b2:* asked whether a reply he writes in a thread on his own comment (not one Claude started) starts Tandem's Claude when it is alone and idle-stopped, he picked "Yes, any thread reply (Recommended)", whose description reads: "A reply you write in any comment thread counts like a new comment: it is you asking Claude for something, so it starts a stopped Claude. Solo still holds it, and Off still means Off." Not picked: "Only Claude's threads". *The reviewing session's readings, open to his change:* it applies before his first Start too, and "any" includes threads on another session's comments.
- **The standing rule this PR restores** (#2196): a chat sent after an Off starts Claude from Off and saves the mode as on-click.
- **Drafter's rulings, unconfirmed until the reviewing session confirms them.** (1) 3b-2a's case "a chat during a pass whose respawn answers cli-unusable" gets master's outcome for the chat: its own pass retries today's arm, so two attempts (two resolves; likewise a doubled refusal and its log line for `no-integration` or a licence refusal). (2) R0.3 keeps the no-child case's spawn assertion only; R0.4 was to delete the half that cannot fail on a fresh spawn, and the builder kept it because the spawn is resumed (above).
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Closed and deleted: "With Claude idle-stopped and a start-or-wake pass queued (3b-2a), a user who turns Claude Off and then sends a chat gets no answer…". Left open: "After a local-model period…" (the local-period PR's). None added.

**Amended 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-R0) — the items carried from #2458's review.** Docs and tests only; no `src` file changes, so nothing a release does changes. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) amends the 3b-2b1a amendment above, which is not edited; each correction is recorded here.
- **Corrections to the 3b-2b1a amendment (R0.2).** (1) Its header's "3b-2b1b (waiting, and the wiring that forwards ownership's step 5 to the supervisor)" should read: waiting is two PRs, 3b-2b1b (the document half) and then the chat half, and the forward is 3b-2b1c's. Its "3b-2b4 is the first PR that reaches it" stays true, for that header's own reason ("behind a waiting decision that is false in production until then"): once 3b-2b1c forwards ownership's `startOrWake`, it is called in production flag on, and not only by the document arm (whose new owner must be Tandem's Claude, given to a non-Tandem caller only through the presence-gated default rule) but also through the message-owner seam (`messageOwnerChanged`, reached when any session's poll takes a chat message, or chat owners are dropped), which needs no presence; such a call queues a pass and counts `startPasses`, but the pass's share returns at `!workIsWaiting()`, and the waiting decision cannot be true without presence, which nothing in `src` sets until 3b-2b4 (review rounds 1 and 2 withdrew this PR's first reading). (2) Its R0.5's "(measured at 36-80 ms each on Linux)" should read: an explicit 60 s per-test timeout, headroom for a loaded Windows runner (not a wall-clock claim). (3) Its R0.1's "the writers of `startOrWakeOwed`, the writers of `messageStartQueued`" should read "the raises of": those lists use `raisesFlag` (`ai-surface-inventory-claims.test.ts`), which skips assignments of `false`. (4) Its Accepted paragraph's "(for example, another AI attached)" should say that the window then also needs that AI to detach during the share's respawn: the standby watch re-checks `anotherAiOn()` at acceptance, so while it stays attached no chat is accepted to coalesce.
- **The plan's rows (R0.3).** 3b-2b1b is the document half of waiting and the probe it installs; a new row, 3b-2b1b-chat, is the chat half (`tandemWorkWaiting`, and the composite step 5 will call); a new row, 3b-2b1c, is the forward, the sink, `workWaiting`, the replacement of 3b-2a's "uncalled" pins, and the licence and Solo proofs; 3b-2b4 no longer wires ownership's `startOrWake`.
- **The start pass's `isAlive()` term (R0.5): a behavioural case, not a source pin.** The brief ruled a source pin, arguing that no state has, under the lock, a standby set and a live child. The plan review found one, reproduced by this PR's case (`supervisor-presence.test.ts`, "3b-2b1b-R0, R0.5"), which fails with `isAlive() ||` deleted: a crash restart (unlocked) waits on its settings read; the user's Start is refused by the licence (`respawn` throws before `stopInternal`, so the stop epoch does not move); licence activation (`resumeAfterLicence`, locked) spawns a second child; a local period begins; the restart resumes past `planAndSpawn`'s epoch and stop re-checks, which do not look at `child`, and `holdForLocalPeriod("crash")` sets `awaiting-start` beside the live child. The switch back finds the child alive and leaves the standby, and a chat's pass is then declined only by `isAlive()`. So the brief's statement that `holdForLocalPeriod`'s callers run with no child is wrong for `planAndSpawn`'s holds after its awaits. The case runs flag on only (that file's default): its positive anchor that the standby watch accepted the chat is the flag-on pass counter, and flag off nothing tells that acceptance apart (its other effect, a wake owed across spawns, is also raised by the gap watch the held restart leaves armed, and `messageStartQueued` is not shown while a child runs); the guard term is the same code either way. The 3b-2b1a R3.5 cases' comments now point at this case.
- **Found, not fixed (no `src` change in this PR).** That state is itself wrong. `planAndSpawn` does not re-check `child` between `buildPlan`'s await and two of its holds, so they can run beside a live child: the first `holdForLocalPeriod` (shown by this PR's case) and, it may be, `holdInStandby("off")` (asserted, not shown: turning Off through the settings stops the live child first, so that route needs a settings read that disagrees with `updateSettings`, such as an external write of the settings file); the second `holdForLocalPeriod` (after `prepareRun`) is already guarded, because its `why` tests `child !== null` first. Beside a live child, the local hold leaves an `awaiting-start` standby with the standby watch armed, so an accepted chat raises `messageStartQueued`, read by `restartPending()` and `startAfterSetup` (not by `status()`, which returns its running branch), for a pass that declines; if reached, `holdInStandby("off")` would also clear `wakeOwedAcrossSpawns`, disarm the gap watch and make presence's `notOff` false. Whether a stale standby outlives the child is not examined. It is dark (the local period ships behind `BYO_MODELS_ENABLED`); this PR's reproduction uses an armed licence, which is one route, not necessarily the only one. A flip-blocker row records it (review round 1).
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Added: a crash restart can reach a local-period hold or the Off hold beside a live child (above). None closed.
- **Also in this PR.** R0.1: the pin that keeps the pending-state terms out of `recomputePresence` gains a positive anchor (each term's declaration in `supervisor.ts`) and a planted read. R0.4: the breaker timeout's comment drops its measured range. R0.6: the "no reaper anywhere" case also points `process.cwd()` away from a built reaper (it does not cover one beside the Node binary).
