**Amended 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b) — waiting on a document for Tandem's Claude, computed and not consumed.** Still dark, and reached by nothing in production: step 5 of `setDocOwner` asks the waiting probe only flag on, only when the new owner is Tandem's Claude and the caller is not, and the only such writers (the lapse and the user-open default, through `defaultRuleOwner()`) need presence, which no `src` file sets; ownership's `startOrWake` stays the no-op stub and 3b-2a's "uncalled" pins are unchanged. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) wins over merged text it contradicts; this amendment records each item and does not edit an older amendment. What lands: `src/server/mcp/inbox-selection.ts` (`isDocumentWaiting`, pure, over copies), `awareness.ts`' `documentWaiting(documentId)` (the adapter: non-creating getter, `structuredClone`d annotations and `channelVisibleReplies` copies, `readModeState()`, `isLocalModelRecord`, and a registry closure that builds the key with `ledgerKey`; total, one fixed log line), and `index.ts`' `setWaitingProbe(documentWaiting)` beside the inbox handover, before both transport branches.
- **The rule (Ruling K, the reviewing session's; it replaces Ruling I).** Document D has waiting work when it holds a user comment or user thread reply that a fresh cursor reader for Tandem's Claude (cursor `"start"`, mark null) polling D now would be handed: an item that passes the collector's selection, the Solo rule and the schema, and for which the registry holds no live **acknowledged** entry for any version at or above its current `editedAt` (what `seenBy` computes for a null mark). Reasons: (1) waiting decides whether to start or wake Tandem's Claude, and a fresh spawn, or any run after a server restart (stale cursors read as fresh), polls as a fresh reader (the skill says `"start"`; the bootstrap text names no cursor); most starts resume a saved session and continue their cursor, for which the rule only over-counts (Accepted below); (2) an item handed but never acknowledged was never confirmed received, so a start or wake for it is right; (3) it closes the unacknowledged half of the dead-run gap (flip-blocker row "An item a run of Tandem's Claude acknowledged ...").
- **Ruling G.** "Handed" has no `sinceSeq` threshold.
- **Ruling J.** The smallest design: nothing in `awareness.ts`' collector, in `annotations.ts`, or in any existing predicate, type or schema changes or moves. The code is new: one module, one adapter, one wiring line (and `setWaitingProbe`'s docblock).
- **The design sentences this brief overrides (each marked in the design README).** (1) §8.5's "computed by the inbox's own document collector with no cursor": that collector writes (ledgers, refreshes, quiet records), so waiting is a separate pure function, held to the collector by `tests/server/ownership-waiting.test.ts`'s agreement table. (2) §8.5's Solo gate: comments and replies count only when the mode reads `"tandem"`, the push rule (`shouldForwardExternally`); an unknown mode never counts. (3) §8.5's "no live registry entry above the document entry's `sinceSeq`" is replaced by Rulings G and K; `sinceSeq` stays written and unread. No same-owner rewrite reaches the probe, a change of owner retires every live entry but kept notices, and a threshold would answer wrong for good because `assign` reuses a version's entry and seq. (4) §8.5's "that pass the handled filter (§6.4)": no handled filter is built; the existing flip-blocker row on the handled filter covers it and is not closed here. (5) The glossary's "has not been handed to its current owner": the rule is "no acknowledged entry, whichever reader acknowledged it"; it differs for every item handed but not acknowledged (deliberately, Ruling K) and after a failed handover inbox step. (6) §8.5's "So an item the owner already has, and answered in some other way, does not keep starting it" does not hold for an unacknowledged item, which counts as waiting.
- **Also recorded.** (7) The 3b-2a amendment's item (5) ("gating starts on presence is 3b-2b's") is subsumed by 3b-2b4, which checks the two windows left: the 5-second settle, and `stop-failed` with a standby set. (8) The 3b-2a amendment's crash-carry Accepted sentence is narrowed by the new flip-blocker row on a dead run's acknowledged items: a fault-killed run's acknowledged items do not read as waiting, so the restart's take-back does not wake it for them until 3b-2b4.
- **Owner decisions that bind it.** Answer 4 of the 3b-2a amendment, "Yes, a comment starts it (Recommended)" ("Solo mode still holds comments, and Off still means Off"), quoted as option text: waiting counts a user comment, held in Solo. Counting user thread replies is the reviewing session's reading of "Yes, any thread reply (Recommended)" (picked for 3b-2b2, the 3b-2b1a amendment), flagged for Bryan.
- **The builder's choices where the brief was silent.** The module restates `channelVisibleReplies`' test on each reply (parent a Claude-facing comment, reply not `private === true`) as well as receiving the adapter's filtered copies, so a caller passing raw replies cannot count a private one (plan review); the agreement does not change, and the brief's wrong build "raw `listReplies`" no longer fails a row because it is harmless. The settled-comment term is exact: given no acknowledged version at or above `v`, the collector's `v > (newest acknowledged ?? 0)` is `v > 0 ||` any acknowledged version, asked as `handedAtOrAbove("a", id, -Infinity)`. The failure log line is not rate-limited (asked once per change of owner reaching step 5; a limiter's clock would be new state that `resetInbox` would have to reset, against Ruling J). D is opened through the user's route before presence is set, so the holding is Tandem's Claude's write-claim.
- **Flip-blocker rows added:** the restart question (closed by Bryan's answer); waiting's cost unmeasured (3b-2b4); step 5 asks the document half only (the chat-half PR); after a restart the registry is empty, so answered items read as waiting (3b-2b4); a dead run's acknowledged items are stranded (3b-2b4). None closed. "After a local-model period ..." stays.
- **Accepted, each stated.** Computed and not consumed until 3b-2b1c and 3b-2b4. No source pin can prove a function does not write (a call regex misses optional calls, bracket access and references passed on); no-write is judged by behaviour and by review of a short adapter and a module that receives no live object. A resumed run continues its old cursor, for which waiting can only over-count (a spare wake, never a missed one), except after a failed handover inbox step: the previous holder's entries stay live, and a continuing cursor of Tandem's Claude that passed them while the previous session held the document is not handed them, so nothing wakes Claude for an item no run of it was handed (it predates this PR; a dead run's acknowledged item has the same shape). The notice-key collision reads as handed on both sides (row 38). The adapter's copies have no test (the frozen-input case covers the module only), and a client-written value `structuredClone` rejects makes the whole document read as not waiting (fails closed). During a local period the poll returns nothing yet waiting can answer true; its consumers hold the start then (no row). Waiting builds the item before any range refresh, so a record whose stored range fails the schema but which a refresh would repair reads as not waiting (unmeasured). `listAnnotations` can log and write a dedup set for legacy records and log malformed keys, for every caller. `channelVisibleReplies` fails open on a non-boolean truthy `private` (#2460); waiting uses it as it is. Two records in D's annotation map with one sanitised `id` under different map keys (client-writable): the poll's refresh step re-keys candidates by id, so the later record stands in for both; with a pending user comment first and a non-answerable record after it, the poll hands nothing while waiting answers true (an over-count, a spare start or wake; found by the diff review). The agreement is with a poll that runs and completes: a poll refused or failed as a whole (the licence once armed, a scope read that throws, a document pass that throws, a response failing its output schema) hands nothing while waiting can answer true.
- **Found, not fixed (not this PR's).** A user comment carrying a stored `agentIdentity` (any client can write one) is handed by `tandem_checkInbox` with that field, which the advertised output schema does not declare, so an SDK client that checks structured content rejects the whole poll response (row 33 reads the server's answer through a request that skips the client's check).

**Amended 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-chat-R0) — the items carried from #2464's review.** Tests and wording only: the only `src` edits are two docblocks, so nothing changes in behaviour, flag on or off. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358) carries the items #2464's merge comment listed; this amendment records each and does not edit the 3b-2b1b amendment above. Everything is in `tests/server/ownership-waiting.test.ts` unless named; each new or changed assertion was seen failing on the wrong build it names (the PR body lists them).
- **R0.1, positive anchors.** A computed predicate selects every row whose answer is "not", with no recorded difference, except the two Solo rows (35, 36), since Solo holds any comment; rows 44 to 46 are included. After its agreement assertions each selected row writes a user comment (`prefix + "-anchor"`) on D and asserts that waiting answers true and that a new fresh poll hands exactly that comment.
- **R0.2, rows 44 to 46** (the count pin is 1 to 46). 44: a settled user comment, edited, then handed and acknowledged; not waiting (wrong build: a settled term that ignores an acknowledgement at the edited version). 45: a user comment stored with `status: "resolved"`, outside the status enum; not waiting on both sides, refused by waiting through the item schema, while the collector lets it past its settled gate and admit drops it, so it exercises the same term as the schema row and is named as unable to discriminate the settled term. 46: a user reply on Claude's comment, handed and acknowledged, then its text edited (a new act that rewrites the stored reply in the replies map, adding an `editedAt`); not waiting (wrong build: replies versioned by `editedAt` or by text; replies are versioned at 0).
- **R0.3, ask, change, ask.** A pending user comment: waiting answers true, then false after a cursor poll and its acknowledgement, then true after a user edit, a fresh poll agreeing each time (catches a cached answer).
- **R0.4, rows 39 and 40.** The `handover-throws` act's `console.error` spy is kept on the row world; it is asserted called exactly once (the log is rate-limited per key) with the handover-failed text as its only argument (copied, as the constant is not exported).
- **R0.5, rows 10 and 20.** The terminal's first poll is asserted to have handed the candidate (row 20's in `userReplies`).
- **R0.6, row 9** is renamed "(cannot discriminate: an undone poll leaves what no poll leaves)".
- **R0.7, #2465's companion.** Beside row 33, the same world polled through the SDK client's checked `callTool` is asserted to reject with the output-schema error; when #2465 is fixed it inverts to a resolving call that hands the candidate, and row 33's raw poll goes.
- **R0.8, `setWaitingProbe`'s docblock** adds "and the cause is not `take-back`" to the conditions under which step 5 asks the probe. The 3b-2b1b amendment's opening sentence ("only when the new owner is Tandem's Claude and the caller is not") is read with the same condition added.
- **R0.9, `documentWaiting`'s docblock** says it makes no Y.Doc write, rather than that it writes nothing: like every caller of `store.listAnnotations()`, it can log a legacy migration and add to the migration log's in-memory dedup set, and log a malformed key.
- **R0.10, the handover over-count, recorded here only.** At every handover the previous holder's entries are retired on purpose (design §6.3; `handOverDocumentInbox`, `awareness.ts` ~535-538), so every user item on the document reads as waiting to the new holder, and the fresh poll agrees; the answered-item half of that belongs to the existing handled-filter row (closed by #2360). The flip-blocker row "After a server restart the delivery registry is empty..." is unchanged.
  *Corrected 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-chat, carried from #2469's review):* "every user item on the document reads as waiting to the new holder" should read "every pending, Claude-facing user comment, every resolved comment that was ever edited, and every user reply on a Claude-facing thread reads as waiting to the new holder" (the handover amendment above: not resolved comments that were never edited; `commentWaits`' settled term and the collector's settled gate), and "(closed by #2360)" should read "(which #2360's switch work closes)": that flip-blocker row is still open.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** None added, none closed.

**Amended 2026-11-18 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-chat) — waiting for Tandem's Claude, the chat half and the decision, computed and not consumed.** Still dark, and reached by nothing in production: `tandemWorkWaiting()` has no caller until 3b-2b1c, its first line returns false while ownership is off, ownership's `startOrWake` stays the stub, and step 5 of `setDocOwner` is unchanged (it still asks the document half only). The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358, "ownership, the chat half of waiting") wins over the merged text it names; this amendment records each item and does not edit an older amendment.
- **The rule (Ruling K applied to chat; Rulings G and J stand).** A user chat message is waiting for Tandem's Claude when a fresh cursor reader for it (cursor `"start"`, mark null) would be handed it if it polled now. The cursor chat collector (`collectCursorChat`, `awareness.ts` ~2694) tests, in order: the author; the handled set (`settledChatIds`, flag on); local-period chat; the ownership scope (`chatScopeOf`, `ownership.ts` ~2119); owed, which for a null mark is `entry ? (!msg.read || view.held.has(entry.seq)) : !msg.read` with an empty `held` (`inbox-cursor.ts` ~491), so `!msg.read`; and the item schema (`admitChatItem`). So a message is waiting exactly when it is a user row, not handled, not local-period chat, not read, admitted by `inboxChatMessageSchema` (built as `admitChatItem` builds it, by a bare `safeParse`, since `admitChatItem` logs), and `chatScopeOf` would answer `"owns"` for Tandem's Claude: not carried over, and `messageStanding` is `owned` by Tandem's Claude (per message, by following a document it holds, or doc-less or on a closed document while it is present). Never gated by Solo or the mode (neither the poll nor the push gates chat by mode: `shouldForwardExternally` passes `chat:message` before it reads the mode); no local-model term beyond local-period chat.
- **The design sentences this overrides (each marked in the design README).** §8.5's chat clause, "for chat, no live chat entry at all, since every change of a message's owner retires its entry (§6.5)", and the glossary's "has not been handed to its current owner": both key chat on the registry, but the real fresh reader never reads the registry for chat (a null mark and an empty `held` reduce owed to `!msg.read`). The reviewing session's earlier notes carried the same error.
- **The acknowledgement ruling.** A reply with `replyTo`, in-progress included, stamps the message read (`markUserChatRead` after any accepted `tandem_reply` with `replyTo`, and the channel reply route), so a fresh poll does not hand it and it is not waiting. Agreement-table row 5 tells this read rule from a registry rule. Every other way `read` is set takes a message out of waiting the same way: a cursor acknowledgement (owned or handled messages only), a cursor-less poll's commit (read at hand-out), and `POST /api/channel-reply`, which stamps it for any session's reply, in-progress included, with no ownership gate, so a session that never owned the message can take it out of waiting (review round 1, R1.1). The consequence is a flip-blocker row (below).
- **The member and every place that builds it.** `OwnershipChatSide` gains a seventh member, `owedToFreshReader(): readonly string[]` (its interface docblock's count corrected from "four", which was already stale at six). Built by `NO_CHAT_SIDE` (`() => []`), by `awareness.ts`' frozen chat side (reads the CTRL chat map through `getOrCreateDocument(CTRL_ROOM)`, clones only user rows, each in its own `try`, and passes the copies, `settledChatIds` and `isLocalPeriodChat` to `inbox-selection.ts`' pure `chatRowsOwedToFreshReader`; it names no owner id and no flag, and adds no import from the ownership module), and by the "item 15" literal in `tests/server/ownership-chat.test.ts`. Every other test passes the chat side itself or a spread of it.
- **`tandemWorkWaiting()`** (`ownership.ts`). Flag off, false at its first line. Otherwise the document half, `heldDocumentIds(TANDEM_OWNER_ID).some((d) => waitingProbe(d, TANDEM_OWNER_ID))`, and the chat half, the private `tandemChatWaiting(() => true)`, which for each key of `owedToFreshReader()` skips a carried-over key, reads the message's facts and keeps it when its standing is `owned` by Tandem's Claude and the selector agrees. Both halves are always asked (the plan review's M1: so a throwing chat half is logged beside a waiting document), each in its own catch that reads its half as false and logs one fixed `logLimited` line carrying no id and no error text. Synchronous; it makes no Y.Doc, registry, read-stamp or ownership write (with the qualification the probe already carries: `documentWaiting` can log a legacy migration and add to an in-memory dedup set, and the chat member resolves the control room with the creating getter, which exists from boot); it never names the bare token 3b-2a's pin forbids. It has no licence term on purpose: refusal is the supervisor's, at the call that consumes it (3b-2b1c), as the start path already refuses. Before Tandem's Claude's first bind in a server run (no presence, no document held), only a message it owns per message can count (review round 1): `messageStanding`'s doc-less arm needs presence, and the document half and the follow-a-document arm need an entry it holds. `tandemChatWaiting` stays unexported for the step-5 PR and 3b-2b1c, and takes the message's facts because an `owned` standing carries no document id.
- **The step-5 composite moved to its own PR, 3b-2b1b-chat-step5.** The 3b-2b1b-R0 amendment's plan-rows item (R0.3) and the plan's 3b-2b1b-chat row said this PR builds it; it does not, and the flip-blocker row "Step 5 asks the document half of waiting only" stays open, now closed by 3b-2b1b-chat-step5. The plan gains that row, and its 3b-2b4 row says so.
- **Judged by a 32-row agreement table** (`tests/server/ownership-waiting-chat.test.ts`): rows 1 to 18 doc-less (Tandem's Claude present, except rows 16 to 18, which turn presence off), 19 to 26 following a document, 27 to 30 the document half, 31 and 32 added by the brief. Each asks the decision with every console method spied (silent), then polls as a fresh reader of Tandem's Claude with no `documentId`, and asserts the handed chat, comment and reply ids are exactly the candidate when waiting and none when not; rows 26 and 30 are the recorded differences (decision false; the poll hands the candidate and claims F); every agreeing "not" row ends with a positive anchor (a message on D: waiting, and handed alone to a new fresh reader). Rows 7 to 10 and 12 are constructed states no tool reaches. Each wrong build the brief names was run as a scratch edit and failed these rows: keyed on an acknowledged registry entry, 5; `delivered` read as handed, 3; `read` ignored, 2, 4, 5 and 20; `handedByRecord` read as handed, 3 and 23; handled ignored, 7 and 8; any Claude row's `replyTo` counted as handling, 8, 9, 10 and 32; local period ignored on both sides, 11; `carriedOver` ignored, 12; schema skipped, 13; gated by the mode, 14 and 15; presence ignored, 16; per-message owners ignored, 17 and 25; the document's holder ignored, 21; closed-document chat dropped, 22; per-message Tandem owners dropped, 24 and 31; document half missing, 27; document half unscoped, 28; privacy bypassed, 29. "A superseded acknowledgement counted" is not told apart by row 32 (`isFinalClaudeReply` refuses `inProgress` before it reads `superseded`); two terms of the pure function are each redundant with a check elsewhere (local-period chat, which `messageStanding` also refuses, and the author, which `message()` also refuses), so a build dropping only that term fails the unit case, not a row.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Changed: "Step 5 asks the document half of waiting only" is now closed by 3b-2b1b-chat-step5. Added: the decision's cost is unmeasured (3b-2b4's measurement; it names `documentWaiting`'s unthrottled failure line, which the decision can now ask per held document); read but unanswered chat is never started for, by any of the ways `read` is set (closed by a decision on a rule for read-but-unanswered chat, then its change with a test); claimable work does not count (closed by Bryan's answer, and if he answers B, the change with a test). None closed.
- **Accepted, each stated.** (1) Not consumed yet: computed, but nothing uses it until 3b-2b1c. (2) During a local period the poll hands nothing (`localPeriodInbox`), yet the decision can answer true; its consumers hold a start then (#1123 A2's message-start hold). (3) Read, unhandled rows after a restart are carried over and never read as waiting; the poll agrees. (4) A handed but unacknowledged message reads as waiting to a continuing cursor that was handed it: once a later PR wakes a running Claude on it, one spare wake. (5) A failed scope read in the poll's snapshot gives "neither", while the rule uses the live entry: the failure path only. (6) A row `structuredClone` rejects is skipped (read as not waiting; that row only). A row whose decoded prototype carries fields (a client-encoded own `__proto__` key) is read through the live row for its author and through the clone for the rest, so it can read as not waiting while the poll hands it: a missed start, never an extra one (security plan review, Low). (7) No-write is judged by behaviour, and the agreement is with a poll that completes. (8) #2465's shape reached chat: `admitChatItem` returned the unparsed item, so a stored `anchor` with extra keys would have passed the server's checks and the decision while an SDK client checking structured content rejected the whole poll (agreement plan review, M2). Closed by #2472 (merged before this PR), which makes `admitChatItem` return the parse's output; the item it builds, which the decision restates, is unchanged. (9) A presence source that throws is logged by `isTandemPresent`'s `total` with its error text, outside the halves' fixed lines (unreachable from today's sources).
- **Flag for Bryan: should an open document nobody holds count as Tandem's Claude's work?** A present Tandem's Claude's poll, with the document's queue idle, is handed that document's chat, comments and replies and claims the document. A (built): no, not until Tandem's Claude holds it. B: yes. Unverified: whether 3b-2b4's presence consumer gives unowned open documents to Tandem's Claude.


**Amended 2026-11-19 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-chat-step5) — step 5 asks the chat that follows the document, computed and not consumed.** Still dark, and reached by nothing in production: `setDocOwner` returns at its first line while ownership is off, the only changes that reach step 5's question with the flag on (a lapse or a user open giving a document to a present Tandem's Claude) need presence, which no `src` file sets, and ownership's `startOrWake` stays the stub. The builder brief (a comment on #2358, "ownership, waiting, the step-5 composite") wins over the merged text it names; this amendment records each item and does not edit an older amendment.
- **The composite and its order.** Step 5's last condition is now the private `waitingAtDocumentChange(documentId, next.ownerId)` (`ownership.ts`), in place of the bare `waitingProbe(documentId, next.ownerId)`. It asks the waiting probe first, exactly as before; only when the probe answers false does it ask `tandemChatWaiting` with a selector keeping a message whose facts name this document (`facts.documentId === documentId`) and whose standing is not per message (`perMessage === false`), i.e. chat that follows the document. Step 5 still runs after the handover's `reOweDocumentChat`, so chat the handover has just owed again counts. The four gates (flag, new owner Tandem's Claude, caller not Tandem's Claude, cause not `take-back`) still short-circuit before it. No new `startOrWake` site and no bare `workWaiting` token.
- **The per-message exclusion.** A message on the document that has a per-message owner is not counted at the document's change, even when that owner is Tandem's Claude (after a close while it held the document); `tandemWorkWaiting` counts that one. A doc-less message and a message on another document Tandem's Claude holds are not counted either: they are not this document's work.
- **Before the first bind.** Tandem's Claude is not present and holds nothing, so `defaultRuleOwner()` is null and no change gives it a document: step 5's second gate fails and the composite is never asked. Only a constructed `setDocOwner` giving it a document reaches the composite; then a message on that document counts and a doc-less message (claimable per message without presence) does not.
- **Its catch.** The chat half has its own catch: a throw reads it as false and logs one fixed, rate-limited line (key `waiting-step5-chat`) with no id and no error text, so a chat failure never reaches `total`'s line, which carries the error text. A probe throw is not caught by the composite: as before, it reaches `setDocOwner`'s `total`, and the chat half is not asked (tested). Unlike `tandemWorkWaiting`, which asks both halves each in its own catch; whether step 5 should do the same is put to Bryan on the PR. The wired probe, `documentWaiting`, has its own catch, so only a test stub throws today. No licence term, on purpose: refusal is the supervisor's, at the call.
- **Solo.** The probe keeps its Solo hold (a user comment on the document does not wake in Solo); chat is never Solo-held, so a chat message on the document does. Tested both ways.
- **Judged by** named cases in `tests/server/ownership-waiting-chat.test.ts` (S1 to S7, S2b, a probe-throw case, the selector cases, the pre-bind cases, the Solo split, the lapse's order and the dark pair), no numbered rows added. Every case that expects a wake from the chat half also asserts the wired probe answered false (S5's twin and the Solo anchor expect the probe's own wake). The `tandemChatWaiting` source pin counts three mentions, and a new pin holds the composite's single call in step 5's condition after the re-owe. Carried from #2474's review: the pin on `admitChatItem`'s fields now fails when either function is missing and asserts both sides parse with `inboxChatMessageSchema.safeParse`.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Closed: "Step 5 asks the document half of waiting only" (deleted). Changed: "Waiting's cost is unmeasured" now also names step 5's read of every user chat row when the probe answers false. None added.
- **The owner guard (from the PR's diff review).** The composite reads the chat half as false for any `ownerId` but Tandem's Claude, since `tandemChatWaiting` answers for Tandem's Claude only; step 5 never passes another.
- **Accepted.** Computed and not consumed: a waiting answer at step 5 reaches only the stub `startOrWake` until 3b-2b1c. The 3b-2b1b-chat amendment's accepted items (2) local period, (5) a failed scope read in the poll's snapshot, (6) a row the clone rejects and (7) a poll that completes apply to the composite's chat half as they do to the decision's.

**Amended 2026-11-19 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1b-claimable) — claimable work waits for Tandem's Claude while no other session is connected, computed and not consumed.** Still dark, and reached by nothing in production: `tandemWorkWaiting()` still has no caller until 3b-2b1c, presence is still unwired, and Tandem's own transports are not routed to the `tandem` record until 3b-2b3. The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358, "ownership, claimable work on an unheld document, and the accepted flip-blocker row") wins over merged text it names; this amendment records each item and does not edit an older amendment.
- **Decided by the owner, 2026-11-19 (picked from options the review session wrote; quoted as option text).** On a comment on an open document no session holds while Tandem's Claude is stopped (the 3b-2b1b-chat amendment's "Flag for Bryan", where A, "no", was built): *"Yes, unless another session is open (Recommended)"*; and before his first Start: *"Yes, even before my first Start"*. On a chat message Claude acknowledges ("I'm on it") and never answers: *"Known limit for 0.32; design it after (Recommended)"* (the option's description: "Ship 0.32 with the gap as it is today: if Claude says 'I'm on it' and never answers, you notice and send the message again. I write it down as a known limit, track it as an issue, and it gets a proper design pass after the release. The other half of this work (a comment on a document nobody holds) still goes ahead."), tracked as #2484.
- **Decided by the owner later the same day, 2026-11-19 (review round 1 of #2486; picked from options).** *Claude Desktop:* asked whether Claude Desktop should count as "another session" for rules like "a comment starts Tandem's Claude unless another Claude session is open", he picked *"No, Desktop doesn't count (Recommended)"* (the option: only agent sessions like Claude Code count; with Desktop open in the background Tandem's own Claude still starts; the named risk, both may look at the item). The rule is decided; only the hand check that Desktop really reports the name the code matches stays open (flip-blocker A3). *The grace window:* told that a session that has gone away still counts as connected for about two minutes (about 37 in the stream-less case) and that Tandem's Claude is not started in that window, he picked *"Yes, fine until the later piece (Recommended)"*; nothing more is built, and the row on asking the decision again when the last other session stops counting stays a flip blocker (3b-2b2). *"I'm on it" by the channel route:* asked whether the #2484 acceptance covers an acknowledgement sent through the channel route as well as through the tool, he picked *"Yes, both routes (Recommended)"*: the owning session's channel-route acknowledgement is its own accepted row (#2484), and the rest of row (ii), read with no acknowledgement and never answered, stays blocking.
- **The review session's readings, not his picks, open to his change.** (1) His option spoke of a comment; chat that follows such a document, and user replies on its Claude-facing threads, count the same way. (2) The 3b-2a pick *"Yes, unless Claude is Off (Recommended)"* ("after a local period, Tandem's Claude starts for the items left for it while another session is open") covers only items Tandem's Claude already owns; claimable work still waits for no other session to be connected.
- **The rule.** `tandemWorkWaiting()` keeps the held work of the 3b-2b1b-chat amendment, which ignores other sessions. While no other session is connected it also counts an open document nobody holds that Tandem's Claude could claim, when the waiting probe (`documentWaiting`) finds a user comment or user thread reply waiting on it, and a user chat message a fresh reader would be handed that follows such a document (the poll's `"claim"` arm, `messageStanding`'s `with-document`). "Could claim": while present, exactly the poll's `inboxScopeOf(...) === "may-claim"` (the queue's wait bound and a blocking loss included); while not present, before the first bind included, nobody holds it, nothing more. The open documents come through a seam, `setOpenDocumentIdsSource`, which `index.ts` wires to `getOpenDocs().keys()` beside the waiting probe, before both transport branches (importing the registry or `document-service` would close a cycle). The probe and the chat rule keep Solo, mode-unknown, ADR-027 and local-period chat out; doc-less chat claimable per message is not counted. It claims nothing and writes nothing.
- **"Another session is connected" (the brief's ruling).** Some record that is not `tandem` or `local` (the stdio-mode record, not the local model, which has none), has not lapsed, and whose self-reported `clientName` is not `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_CLIENT_NAME` (#2196's ruling, unverified), whatever its kind: Desktop's name on an `http` record is excluded, and a non-Desktop bridge or a header-less `http` terminal counts. A record that has not lapsed is attached or in its grace. The windows: a gone session that held a stream counts for its grace (about two minutes); one that never held one stays attached while registered, until the idle reaper (30-minute TTL, swept every 5 minutes), then its grace: up to about 37 minutes. A lapsed record stays lapsed until attached again, even if it holds a document (a grace is armed again, and it still does not count). Tandem's own child counts until 3b-2b3 (a flip-blocker row). It differs from the supervisor's `otherAiAttached` (#2196), which judges registry entries by session id and counts an entry with no open stream for 60 seconds after its last request (`LIVE_RECENT_MS`).
- **Corrections, dated 2026-11-19, to older amendments (recorded here, not edited there).** The 3b-2a amendment's reading that "a comment also starts Tandem's Claude before his first Start" is now his pick ("Yes, even before my first Start"), for claimable work as built here. The 3b-2b1b-chat amendment's "Flag for Bryan" is answered (B, with the condition above); its sentence "Before Tandem's Claude's first bind … only a message it owns per message can count" now holds only for held work and chat; its "32-row agreement table" has 53 rows (below), and rows 26 and 30 are differences because another session (A) is connected. The handover amendment's sentence "`tests/server/ownership-flag.test.ts` fails when the flag's literal is `true` while the list is not empty" (and "holds every gap the flip must not land without") is superseded by the accepted-row convention below.
- **Acknowledged chat.** Three picks were made on 2026-11-19, in order. *"Waiting again after 10 min (Recommended)"* was withdrawn because Claude sends nothing while it works, so every task over 10 minutes would be handed out twice. *"Only when the run has ended (Recommended)"* was withdrawn because the default lifetime is `"always"` (`src/shared/launcher/contract.ts`), so the run rarely ends. *"Known limit for 0.32; design it after (Recommended)"* stands (#2484). *"Stays with that session (Recommended)"* stands and is already built: its lapse re-owes (`lapse`, `releaseMessageOwners`, `reOweDocumentChat`). The flip-blocker row "Read but unanswered chat is never started for" is split: (i) acknowledged by a `tandem_reply` with `replyTo` and `inProgress`, **accepted** for the flip (#2484); (ii) read with no "I'm on it" (a cursor acknowledgement, or a cursor-less poll's commit at hand-out), **blocking** until Bryan answers; (iii) a channel-route reply from a non-owner, folded into PR 4's channel-reply row ("with no re-owe after it, such an acknowledgement takes the message out of waiting for good"). The rows "A live acknowledger that never answers" (PR 5) and the Ruling K row on a run that acknowledged and died (3b-2b4) stay blocking for their cases. The plan review found a fourth case, an "I'm on it" the owning session sends through `POST /api/channel-reply` (after PR 4 closes its row, nothing else would hold it); Bryan's "Yes, both routes (Recommended)" accepts it as its own row under #2484.
- **The accepted-row convention.** `FlipBlocker` gains an optional `acceptedFor`; `flipBlockerViolations` skips a row only when it is non-blank. An accepted row stays in the list, still open, and does not stop the flip; adding `acceptedFor` records a pick of Bryan's, cited here; removing it re-blocks the flip. `tests/server/ownership-flag.test.ts` pins the accepted rows' full text (so an edit to one, or a new acceptance, edits a named pin), refuses a blank `acceptedFor`, and requires every accepted row to name at least one issue, each named in this ADR.
- **Judged by** `tests/server/ownership-waiting-chat.test.ts`, whose agreement table grows to 55 rows: rows 33 to 51, 54 and 55 lapse A first; 38 to 42 (`another-session`) and 49 and 50 (`not-present`: the decision is true, a not-present poll hands nothing, and once present it hands the candidate and claims F) are new differences; 52 and 53 poll as A, which is handed the candidate and claims F; 54 (carried-over chat on F) and 55 (Desktop's name on a `claude` record, told by name and never by display kind) were added in review round 1. Every F row asserts F unowned after the decision. Other cases: the seam unwired and reset; a throwing seam (one fixed line); a throwing probe that hides no claimable chat; a lapsed record holding a document; no write; dark. Source pins: only `index.ts` installs the seam, once, before both branches; the helpers are unexported; the module imports nothing from `documents/` or `document-service`.
- **Builder's choices, where the brief was silent or departed from.** The open documents are read before both halves, in their own catch, not after the held arm inside the document half's catch as the brief ordered: so a throwing held probe cannot hide claimable chat (all three plan reviews). The seam's failure line is fixed: "the open documents could not be read for waiting; no unheld document is counted."
- **Flip-blocker rows.** Deleted: "Claimable work does not count" (closed here). Added: the gap that nothing asks the decision when claimable work appears or the other session goes (3b-2b2's widened start arm); Tandem's own Claude counting as another session (3b-2b3); Claude Desktop told apart by an unverified name (a hand check; the rule itself decided); and, from review round 1, after a restart a still-open Claude Code session is not seen as connected until it next initializes, so with the restart row Tandem's Claude could start at boot for old comments once something consumes the decision (closed by 3b-2b2 with a boot quiet period or a "written since boot" condition, and a test). Accepted (#2484): row (i) and the channel-route row. Split, as above. Widened: the decision's cost row (one claim read and one waiting probe per open, unowned document while no other session is connected) and the restart row (every open document nobody holds, while no other session is connected).
- **Accepted, each stated.** (1) Not consumed. (2) Before the first bind it counts what the poll does not hand (rows 49 and 50). (3) A stream-less gone session counts up to about 37 minutes. (4) True while Off or licence-refused: dropped at the call. (5) Not present, a busy queue and a loss are ignored (each pinned by a case). (6) Local period: the 3b-2b1b-chat amendment's Accepted 2. (7) From the security reviews: the Desktop exclusion trusts a self-reported name. Any loopback client that sends `clientInfo.name: "claude-ai"` in its own `initialize` is left out of "another session", and since a record's `clientName` is its latest `initialize`'s, a local process that knows another session's `claude:` or `bridge:` identity can relabel that record either way: as Desktop, so claimable work counts beside it (once consumed, a second AI and its cost, and the two contend for the document), or away from Desktop, so a real Desktop counts and starts are suppressed (a missed start). Nothing is disclosed: the work is the user's own and every gate still applies; loopback only while the LAN bind is dark. A3's hand check is where this is next looked at.

**Amended 2026-11-19 (#2358, #2440 first part) — one folder read and parallel run-file checks on Windows.** Still dark: `prepareRun` and the boot sweep run only under `ownershipEnabled()`, and `setRestrictiveAcl` is not edited, so flag off nothing changes. Bryan picked "Small fix now, rest later (Recommended)" from options on 2026-11-19: the small fix that weakens no security check now, and the faster ACL reader stays #2440, after 0.32. #2440 stays open.
- **C1, one folder read.** `ensureLauncherDir`'s Windows branch no longer calls `verifyNoBroadAce(dir)` after `lockDir(dir)`. `realRunFileAcl.lockDir` is `setRestrictiveAcl`, which already re-reads the folder's ACL after `icacls` (`await assertNoBroadAce(path, { signal })` at its top level, outside any catch) and throws on a refused entry, inside the same try that maps to `launcher-dir-acl`. So `lockDir` carries the folder's only read; the per-process cache is still set only after it resolved. The `RunFileAcl` docblock states that contract, and #2440's later reader must keep the proof spec's new case (an explicit Everyone entry on `launcher/` is refused by the lock's own read) passing unchanged. Two differences from the dropped read, both fail-closed or inside an accepted residual: the read shares `lockDir`'s 60 s abort budget with `whoami` and `icacls` (a slow host refuses sooner, never passes), and an entry added in the milliseconds between the two old reads is first seen at the next process (inside #2439's residual).
- **C2, the run files' per-file checks in parallel.** `prepareRun` starts both `writeRunFile` calls in one `Promise.allSettled`, pushes each fulfilled path into `written` (mcp first), and throws mcp's rejection if mcp failed, else settings'. `writeRunFile` is unchanged: each file is still `lstat`ed, created exclusively, checked while empty and only then written. `Promise.all` would leave the late file behind; awaiting the two in turn would leave one and can leave a rejection unhandled.
- **Timing, an estimate.** About 0.6 s off the first start and 0.25 s off later ones, estimated from timing child processes on one Windows 11 laptop, not measured on this build; the builder did not measure. The review session re-measures on Windows.
- **Register.** Neither change trips a void condition of #2439 or #2441 (`docs/security.md`): the per-file check stays before the write, the cache key and lifetime are unchanged, no file kind or reader is added, and no new caller or wrapper of the lock or the check appears (the two calls stand written out in `prepareRun`).
- **Accepted, each stated.** (1) When the mcp file fails, the settings file is now also created, checked and deleted (before, never attempted); it is never written unless its own check passes. (2) A settings `-acl` refusal now clears the folder cache even when mcp failed for another reason (fail-safe: the next spawn re-checks the folder). (3) An entry added to `launcher/` between the old two reads is first seen at the next process (inside #2439's residual). (4) The folder's read shares `lockDir`'s 60 s budget. (5) The savings are estimates until re-measured.
- **Not built.** The faster reader (#2440); the same duplicate read in `install-claude-cli.ts`; any change to `acl-win.ts`, the cache or the files' content.

**Amended 2026-11-19 (#2358 PR 3b-2b1c-a) — the document forward to the supervisor's start-or-wake.** Still dark, and reached by no production path: step 5 forwards only when a change gives Tandem's Claude a document and the caller is not Tandem's Claude, and the only such writers (the lapse, the user-open default) write `defaultRuleOwner()`, which is Tandem's Claude only while `isTandemPresent()`, which nothing in `src` sets. A message is Tandem's Claude's only by its presence, by following a document it holds, or through the `tandem` record, which nothing routes. Presence is not "running" (design §8.1). The builder brief for this PR (a comment on #2358, "ownership PR 3b-2b1c, the forward from ownership to the supervisor's start-or-wake, in two PRs") wins over merged text it names; this amendment does not edit an older one.
- **R1, ownership's `startOrWake` becomes the forward** (same name, export and signature). In order: it returns while ownership is off (R1.1); the test observer is told every cause; `user-open` is dropped (R1.3; an open is never a reason by itself, and this covers the boot's restored and startup documents, which get the default rule with this cause; a later Off-to-On default rule must not reuse `user-open`); in this PR `message-owner` is dropped too (3b-2b1c-b's); inside its own `try` (R1.5), a `lapse` returns while `anotherSessionConnected()` is true (R1.7); every other `OwnershipChangeCause` reaches the sink as `"document"` (R1.4), called inside `runOutsideMcpContext` so a forward made inside another client's request (an eviction in its `initialize`, `closeEntry`) neither reaches the sink nor starts the pass in that context. A throw logs one fixed, rate-limited line (key `forward-failed`: "[ownership] a change of owner could not be passed on to Tandem's Claude; it may not have been started or woken.") and is never rethrown. The sink is `let startOrWakeSink`, set by `setStartOrWakeSink`, reset by `resetOwnershipForTests`; ownership imports nothing under `launcher/`.
- **R1.7, one gate toward pick 2.** "Another session" is `anotherSessionConnected()` (the claimable amendment's rule): a record that is not `tandem` or `local`, not lapsed, and whose `clientName` is not Claude Desktop's. A detached record inside its grace has not lapsed, so it counts. The departing record never counts (`lapse` marks it lapsed before any move). Only `lapse` is a holder going away: no other cause reaches the sink with a caller that is not Tandem's Claude, so the others keep R1.4's mapping with no check, and a later writer handing Tandem's Claude a document decides its own rule. The gate is at the forward, not in the pass: the pass does not know the cause, and `otherAiAttached` (#2196) would change supervisor code and can count the departing session's transport. It leaves alone 3b-2a's rule for work Tandem's Claude already owns and the design §8.3 start rules (3b-2b2's).
- **R2, `index.ts`.** `setStartOrWakeSink((cause) => launcherSupervisor?.startOrWake?.(cause));` beside `setWaitingProbe(documentWaiting);`, before both transport branches, reading `launcherSupervisor` at call time (null in stdio, with a disabled or deferred launcher, after a throwing `createSupervisor`, and at boot before the supervisor exists: nothing). A supervisor whose `start()` threw stays assigned on purpose (so `shutdown()` can still reap what it spawned), so the sink still reaches it; its `startOrWake` then applies its own drops (shutdown, live Off, the licence) and its pass whatever state the failed start left. `workWaiting: tandemWorkWaiting,` in the `createSupervisor` options: the pass is the first consumer of the decision, claimable work included. **R3**, `supervisor.ts` comments only. **R4**, the local period is piece 36's: a pass that finds no child during a local period is held by `respawn` as today's `"message"` kind, and the switch back starts it as today's held message start; unchanged here.
- **Decided by the owner, 2026-11-19 (this brief's Flag; picked from options, quoted as option text).** *Pick 1:* "No, never by itself (Recommended)" (the comment or message reaches Claude the next time it looks or is started for something else): opening or closing a file never starts or wakes Claude by itself; built (R1.3's drop here; the close is 3b-2b1c-b's). A departure from design §4.4 (the open) and §8.3 (the close). *Pick 2:* "Only if no other session is open (Recommended)" (if another Claude session is still connected, the item waits for it or for Claude's next look; if none is, Tandem's Claude starts); not picked: "Yes, always" (the design's rule), "Only wake, never start". It replaces design §8.4's "whether or not another AI is on" and plan R2.4's override for a departed holder. The question said it stays switched off until Tandem can tell answered comments from unanswered ones (new blocker 2). **Not delivered by this piece**: one gate toward it (R1.7). *Pick 3:* asked where the document and its unanswered comments go when a session working on it goes away while a second Claude session is still connected, "To Tandem's Claude, quietly" (as built, the smaller build: the document passes to Tandem's own Claude without starting or waking it; the comments wait for its next look or start, which can be a long time if it is stopped; the other connected session is not offered them); not picked: "To nobody, so the other session can take it". Built while Tandem's Claude is present and the second session counts as connected (not lapsed; item 8 below) (row A18).
- **The review session's ruling** (no trade-off; recorded as such, not as Bryan's): while Tandem's Claude is not present (Off, or not yet started) the document cannot pass to it, so it goes to nobody under the existing default rule and another session may take it; "Off still means Off" is the reason. **The review session's reading**, not decided: pick 3 extends to a closed document's chat message (3b-2b1c-b's M13).
- **Earlier picks this piece rests on**, recorded by the claimable amendment above (pointed to, not recorded again): "Yes, unless another session is open (Recommended)"; "Yes, even before my first Start" (here a departure while not present starts nothing, item 6); "Known limit for 0.32; design it after (Recommended)" (#2484); "Stays with that session (Recommended)"; "No, Desktop doesn't count (Recommended)" (this rule too: row A20); "Yes, fine until the later piece (Recommended)" (covers the grace window); "Yes, both routes (Recommended)". From 2026-11-17: "Yes, unless Claude is Off (Recommended)", refined by "Yes, only if still waiting (Recommended)" (piece 36's; new blocker 1).
- **What stands between this piece and pick 2**, in plain words. "Another session" is any record that is not Tandem's own and not Claude Desktop: other apps and records with no client name count, though the option text says "Claude session". (1) Tandem's own Claude counts as another session while it runs and for a while after it stops, so if your terminal session goes away then, Tandem's Claude is not woken or started for that session's work; it gets to it at its next look or start (the own-Claude row, widened; 3b-2b3). (2) A session that has gone away still counts as connected for its grace, about 2 minutes, up to about 37 if it never kept a live stream; two sessions quitting together skip the first one's work, and nothing asks again unless the second held waiting work itself (claimable's A1 row, widened; 3b-2b2's re-ask). (3) After Tandem restarts, a still-open session is not counted until it reconnects, so a departure in that window can start Tandem's Claude beside it (the restart-session row, widened; 3b-2b2). (4) Decided, not a difference (pick 3): a departing session's document passes to Tandem's Claude by the default rule and every other session is refused its items (`refusalFor` reason `"default"`, `inboxScopeOf` `"neither"`), so the item waits for Tandem's Claude's next look or start; a closed document's message the same, by the review session's reading. (5) Tandem's own Claude can be the session that goes away: before 3b-2b3 its child sits on an ordinary `claude:<id>` record; if it opens a document Tandem's Claude holds by default it takes it as `"opened"`, and when it stops the record lapses, the document returns by the default rule, and the forward starts Tandem's Claude for its own leftovers, possibly after every idle stop (row A21; the own-Claude row). (6) A departure while Tandem's Claude is not present starts nothing: the document goes to nobody and a connected session is offered its items (row A5; claimable's A1). (7) Answered work reads as waiting after any handover (new blocker 2; the handled-filter row; #2360). (8) A session still open whose live connection dropped and stayed quiet past its grace has lapsed and stays lapsed while registered, so it does not count, and another session's departure starts Tandem's Claude beside it (new blocker 4; row A17c).
- **#2485's open questions, the review session's rulings.** A probe throw at step 5 does not ask the chat half (fail toward not waking). Chat waking Tandem's Claude in Solo is intended: Solo holds comments, never chat (rows A7b).
- **The chat owners amendment's ruling (13)** ("every change of a message's owner reaches the step 5 seam (`startOrWake`)") reaches the supervisor only from 3b-2b1c-b, at the release loop, and never for a close or `setMessageOwner`.
- **The boot trace.** (1) Startup opens run `applyUserOpenDefaultToDocuments` with cause `user-open`, before the supervisor exists and while Tandem's Claude is not present, so step 5 never runs; (2) were presence true, the cause is dropped and the sink finds a null reference; (3) no message has an owner at boot. So this piece starts nothing at boot (Bryan's standing rule: no unasked work except a one-time first-run greeting); today's boot start is unchanged. Later in the run old and answered work counts (new blocker 2), which blocks the switch-on.
- **Licence (Critical Rule 9).** Not a new AI surface: no external subscriber, `appendClaudeChatMessage` caller or turn writer. A new production caller of an existing admission point: the supervisor's `startOrWake` refuses at the call (`decideRefusal()`, row A10), the pass's wake is refused at the spawn's writer, and its start by `respawn`. `docs/licensing-explained.md` says so.
- **Judged by** `tests/server/launcher/ownership-forward.test.ts` (new; the supervisor harness copied from `supervisor-presence.test.ts`, the ownership world from `ownership-chat.test.ts` and `ownership-waiting-chat.test.ts`, `workWaiting: tandemWorkWaiting` and never a double, except the one R1.5 case that must watch the pass's context): rows A1 to A21 as the brief's table. A4 to A7 are regression rows, with A9 as their reachability witness: step 5's own checks stop them before `startOrWake`, so no wrong forward can fail them. The other "nothing" rows show the forward was reached: A3 and A8 by what the observer was told (`user-open`, `message-owner`), A17, A17b, A18 and A19 by the observer told `lapse`, A2 and A10 by the sink called. Every "nothing" row but A1 (flag off) also asserts that D's change reached the composite's step 5 (its `wake` step, which fires before step 5's checks); plus, from the plan reviews, A6's ADR-027 variants (a user reply on a private note and on a private comment, a highlight), A7b (Solo and mode-unknown chat wakes), A16b (a user reply in Claude's thread wakes), A17b (a session inside its grace counts) and A17c (a lapsed, still-registered session does not; new blocker 4, as built); direct calls for R1.1, R1.4 (a table over every cause) and R1.5 (no caller identity readable at the sink or in the pass); a throwing sink in a two-document lapse; a comment resolved before the pass (nothing) and one edited then resolved (still wakes, new blocker 2); the pass asking the whole decision; a session connecting between forward and pass; and new blocker 2 as built, in two shapes (a user reply in A's own thread answered there by A, and a user comment answered by a fresh comment, both acknowledged by cursor; a thread reply on a user's comment is refused today, `not-owned`, until #2360).
- **Pins** (`tests/server/ownership-source-pins.test.ts`). The 3b-2a describe is kept, retitled: `requestWake` is still named by no file outside the supervisor; `workWaiting` by `index.ts` only; `startOrWake` by `index.ts` (once, comments stripped) and ownership (its definition and two bare calls). New: only `index.ts` sets the sink, once, as the exact line before both branches; `workWaiting: tandemWorkWaiting,` inside the `createSupervisor({ ... })` object; the sink's declaration and exactly two assignments in ownership; no `launcher/` import specifier in ownership. The #2474 describe: `tandemWorkWaiting` is named by `index.ts` and ownership. Each changed pin was shown failing on a scratch edit.
- **Carried from #2485 and #2486.** Step 5's composite pin also requires `tandemChatWaiting(` in the composite's body, and a new case (S5b) puts carried-over chat on D (no wake); S2 is retitled as a guard (S2b is its positive arm); S5 proves the rate limit (a second throwing change inside the minute asks again and writes no second line); the cost row names `chatRowsOwedToFreshReader`'s non-local-period filter. **Correction, dated 2026-11-19, to the claimable amendment (recorded here, not edited there):** its "Corrections" bullet says the agreement table "has 53 rows"; it has 55, as its own "Judged by" says.
- **Flip-blocker rows.** None closed. Widened: claimable's A1 (a gone session counts for its grace at a departure; two sessions gone together), the own-Claude row (a departure beside a running or just-stopped Tandem's Claude starts nothing for that work; the child's own lapse starts it for its own leftovers, which with new blocker 2 can repeat after every idle stop; and, from the plan review, while the child sits on a `claude:` record the work it is woken for is refused to it, so it stays waiting and later forwards wake it again), and the restart-session row (this piece is the first consumer; its closer is now 3b-2b2's boot quiet period or "written since boot" condition, extended to a lapse in that window). Stale words fixed in the handover gap (d), close-drops-round, banner, lapse-to-unowned, never-answers and decision-cost rows (each stays open: this piece lands the re-owe's start-or-wake and opens no delivery round); the local-period row's closer names piece 36. Added as the brief words them: new blockers 1 (the local-period switch back does not re-ask waiting), 2 (answered work starts Tandem's Claude; its "after a holder lapses with no other session connected" reworded to "after any handover, at the next forward" by the review session's answer in review round 1), 3 (the presence gate 3b-2a deferred) and 4 (a still-open session whose stream dropped and lapsed does not count). Not added: the open and the close (pick 1), Claude Desktop (decided), where a departed document goes (pick 3).
- **Accepted, each stated.** (1) Work that becomes waiting while the supervisor reference is null is never asked about again. (2) A call-time licence refusal drops the forward; a later activation starts nothing for it (A10). (3) The pass asks the whole decision: a forward for D can start Claude for other work; one whose work is gone by the pass starts nothing. (4) 3b-2a's starting cases (Off, a pending crash restart) and R4's local-period hold stand, proved by `supervisor-presence.test.ts`' existing rows, not re-run here. (5) A session that connects between a lapse forward and its pass does not stop the pass (a short race; asserted); likewise a lapse caused by an eviction inside a client's `initialize`, whose client may not be registered yet. (6) A departure while Tandem's Claude is not present starts nothing; the document goes to nobody and a connected session is offered it (A5). (7) Each item of the list above that has a row, until it closes (item 4 is decided).
**Cross-references:** #2358, design v5 (`docs/spikes/multi-session-ownership/README.md`), the plan (`docs/plans/2358-ownership.md`), ADR-011, ADR-040 and ADR-053 (the flag pattern), ADR-045, ADR-049, ADR-051, ADR-056, #1906, #1952.

## ADR-055: Cowork Setup Ships Dark

**Status:** Accepted (2026-10-02)

**Context.** Bryan decided on 2026-10-02 to cut Cowork out of v1.0: "im not sure we can make cowork actually function for most of the scenarios in the stories". Asked how deep the cut should go, he chose to ship it dark over a docs-only descope or deleting the code.

What is and is not known matters for how this is worded everywhere else:

- **Cowork reaching Tandem is not the unknown.** It was verified end to end at v0.7.1 (2026-04-20, recorded in `roadmap-history.md`), over the plugin-stdio bridge of ADR-023. #1598 (2026-08-24) measured a Cowork session reaching Tandem 0.24.1 through an MCP entry registered on the desktop and proxied into the session, request and response only.
- **The route Tandem's "Enable Cowork" setup builds is the unknown.** That setup writes a plugin entry into every Cowork workspace, pointing at `http://host.docker.internal:3479` with the auth token, and adds a Windows firewall rule for the VM subnet. The desktop sidecar has since been pinned to `TANDEM_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1` (`SIDECAR_BIND_HOST` in `src-tauri/src/sidecar.rs`, passed explicitly so an ambient value cannot move it). Whether a VM can reach a loopback-only listener through that route has never been settled: decision E in the 2026-09-02 review is still "Unknown", and the transport matrix that would decide it (#1455, Test A) was never run. The one measurement there is points the wrong way: #1598 found the Cowork desktop VM with `lo` only and no route to the Windows host. That is evidence, not the Test A verdict.
- Separately, the firewall rule can only be written from an elevated process and Tandem never elevates, so for a non-admin user Enable has always ended in the admin-declined state, or earlier at the subnet probe when no Cowork session was running.

So v1.0 would have shipped a Windows toggle, a wizard row and an onboarding step for a setup nobody has shown to work on a current build, while polling `cowork_get_status` every 30 seconds on every desktop run.

**Decision.** Ship Tandem's Cowork setup dark behind a flag, the way `.docx` ships (ADR-053). Don't delete it. Make no claim about Cowork in either direction: user-facing text says the setup is not in this version and Cowork is not a supported surface, never that Cowork cannot connect.

- **The flag.** `COWORK_ENABLED` is a literal `false` in `src/shared/constants.ts` and in `src-tauri/src/cowork_commands.rs`. There is no build define and no environment override: no server or CLI behaviour depends on it, and the client reads the literal directly.
- **Client: nothing Cowork mounts or polls.** Five sites read the literal: the Settings → AI Assistant tab's lazy `CoworkSettings` block, the integration wizard's status poller and its "More integrations" row, `App.svelte`'s `CoworkAdminDeclinedModal`, `useTutorial`, and `OnboardingTutorial`. A dark desktop build sends no `cowork_*` invoke.
  - `useTutorial` needs more than a gated poller. It holds its completion index at `Infinity` until the Cowork status settles, and on desktop "settled" used to mean a status or an error had arrived. With the poller off neither does, so the settled test is keyed on the same value as the poller. Gate one without the other and the desktop tutorial never completes.
- **Rust: no new writes into Claude Desktop's files.** `refuse_if_dark()` returns an error from the enable branch of `cowork_toggle_integration` and from `cowork_rescan`, `cowork_apply_token`, `cowork_install_into_workspace` and `cowork_set_lan_ip_override`. `cowork_retry_admin_elevation` only delegates to the toggle's enable branch, so it is refused there. The five-minute heal task is not spawned, and `cowork_heal_pass` itself returns `Ok(0)` before doing anything, so a second caller would stay dark too.
  - Disable, `cowork_uninstall_from_workspace`, the read-only commands and the uninstall scrub are untouched. The toggle's gate sits inside `if enabled` for that reason: any earlier and it would refuse disable too.
  - All eleven commands stay registered.
- **People who already enabled it keep what was written.** This was Bryan's decision; the alternative was a one-time removal pass at first launch. The consequences, stated exactly:
  - The Tandem entries in each Cowork workspace, `cowork-meta.json` with `enabled: true`, and the firewall rule (where an elevated run ever wrote one) all stay.
  - Nothing maintains them. A workspace created later gets no entry, and the existing entries stay pinned at the `tandem-editor` version that wrote them.
  - There is no in-app way to turn it off after the update. The removal is to uninstall the desktop app (and install it again to keep using Tandem). `tandem --uninstall-scrub` was a second route only on some machines: its Cowork scan covered the `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\Claude_*` location and not `%APPDATA%\Claude\local-agent-mode-sessions`, which the desktop scan added under ADR-044 (#2136). Which Claude Desktop installs use which location was not established here: ADR-044 calls the second "the common case", while a winget-installed Claude Desktop measured on 2026-10-02 used the first. **Resolved by #2136 (2026-10-05):** the CLI scan now covers both roots, so it is a second route for the workspace entries (not `cowork-meta.json`) wherever `%APPDATA%` matches its Windows Known Folder and both `%APPDATA%` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%` point inside the user folder.
  - Under the desktop app the leftovers expose nothing, because `:3479` is loopback-only. The same machine running the npm server with a routable `TANDEM_BIND_HOST` is a different composition: the rule then admits the VM subnet to whatever listens on 3479. The token in the entries is usually dead against that server, because the desktop keeps its token in the OS keychain and the npm server uses its own token file; they match only if the desktop had adopted the file token. That composition is user-initiated and predates this ADR; what changed is that only removal, not a toggle, clears it.
  - **`tandem rotate-token` does not retire the entries' token.** It rotates the npm token file, and a running desktop server refuses the call. The desktop's keychain token has no supported rotation path, so removing the entries is how that token is retired.
- **The desktop uninstall scrub now deletes `cowork-meta.json`.** It is the one piece of Tandem's own data the scrub removes, at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\tandem\Data\cowork-meta.json` (the npm data location, although the desktop app writes it). Nothing deleted it before, so `enabled: true` outlived an uninstall, and a later build with Cowork lit would have written the token into every workspace on its first heal tick with nobody having asked. The CLI scrub is unchanged and still leaves app data, and an upgrade never runs the scrub, so the file can survive by either route; the second re-enable item covers that.
  - The scrub's firewall removal runs unelevated like everything else, so a rule that an elevated run wrote can survive an uninstall. `docs/data-locations.md` gives the `netsh` command.

**Consequences.**
- `tests/build/cowork-dark-gate.test.ts` pins the Rust half as text, because no cargo test calls a command: the two literals agree, every `#[tauri::command]` in the module has a `gated`/`ungated` row (a new command with no row fails), each gate sits where its row says, the gate reads the literal and nothing else, every reference to the gate and the literal across the crate is counted, the heal pass and its spawn both sit under the literal, and a census lists every function that calls a write primitive or writes meta. These are shape checks: nothing invokes a command and observes the refusal, because the commands are Windows-only and no cargo test calls one.
- `tests/client/cowork-ship-dark.test.ts` and `tests/client/use-tutorial-cowork-dark.svelte.test.ts` run the parents at the shipped literal with Tauri genuinely on. `App.svelte` is pinned from source, since nothing mounts it in tests. A client census lists every module that imports a Cowork component, starts a status or pre-flight hook, calls an invoke wrapper or names a `cowork_*` command, so a new parent is a row to add. The three suites that exercise the lit path mock the literal on.
- **This supersedes the "Core" mark on #316** in `docs/v10-triage.md`, whose rows are frozen and so still show it. Cowork on macOS and Linux is no longer a v1.0 item.
- **#1455 and #1727, both dated gates, are retired** into the checklist below rather than kept. #1727's own text says a descope means "consolidate now"; with all three confirmation flows dark there is nothing live to consolidate, so that work moves to re-enable. That overrides one carve-out #1727 marked "NOT deferred", the duplicated `cowork-enable-confirm-btn` testid: neither copy is reachable while dark (`CoworkSettings` is not mounted, and the wizard's Cowork sub-view cannot be opened), so it moves too. Both issues are closed when this ADR's PR merges.
- **#1952, #1884 and #1906 are not dormant.** They are properties of a non-loopback bind, which `TANDEM_BIND_HOST` produces on an npm-started server with or without Cowork. (The desktop sidecar pins loopback, so they were never reachable there.) `NON_LOOPBACK_ALLOWED` stays for the same reason: the channel shim and the plugin monitor use it.
  - **Amended 2026-10-05 ([ADR-056](#adr-056-the-non-loopback-bind-ships-dark)).** That bind now ships dark too, so #1952 and #1906 moved to Accepted (bounded) and #1884 stays open for its local-caller half only. The sentence above was true when written: Cowork never set the bind, and ADR-056 is what removes it. (#1884's `GET` later began refusing a non-loopback peer, so the LAN half that ADR-056's re-enable item 3 still owes is the `POST` carve-out alone.) *(2026-11-05: #1952 is fixed, not accepted — `wakeUrl` is now withheld per request from any non-loopback peer, so it no longer depends on the bind staying dark. #1884 was closed by #1885 on 2026-10-28. Of the three, only #1906 remains, accepted under this ADR.)*
  - **Amended 2026-10-28 (#1885).** #1884 is closed at the source: the shim no longer declares the permission capability and the relay routes are deleted. Neither its local-caller half nor its LAN half remains.
- User-facing docs no longer describe a Cowork setup. Internal docs (`architecture.md`, `gotchas.md`) keep describing the code under a ships-dark banner.
- ADR-038's canonical paragraph listed "cowork" among what the Claude integration ships with. It no longer does; see the amendment there.

**Re-enable checklist** (tracked in #2134).
1. Settle whether the installer route works at all: run Test A in `docs/plans/archived/cowork-transport-test-matrix.md` and answer decision E. A pass on Test A may delete the firewall and per-workspace installer surface rather than re-enable it.
2. Make a lit build ask again before acting on a `cowork-meta.json` that predates it. `enabled: true` written before the dark release is not consent to a heal pass now.
3. Decide the `firewall.rs` failure enum on its merits (#1373).
4. Consolidate the three enable-confirmations (#1727), including the duplicated `cowork-enable-confirm-btn` testid.
5. Flip both literals together; the gate test checks they agree.
6. Remove the `COWORK_ENABLED: true as false` mocks from the three lit-path suites and retire the two dark tests.
7. Undo the docs side. `grep -rn 'ADR-055\|ships dark\|while dark' docs CLAUDE.md README.md src src-tauri/src src-tauri/windows` and resolve every Cowork hit: the user-facing docs restored from the PR that shipped this ADR, the banners and notes on ADR-023, ADR-038 and ADR-044 dropped, `data-locations.md`'s past-enabler section rewritten, and the comments that say "while dark" brought up to date.
8. Give the Cowork status UI a state for an AppData folder on a network share. Since #2144 the workspace scan never enters a UNC Known Folder, so such a machine reads as "no Claude" or "no workspace yet" rather than "blocked"; the old "blocked" answer came from touching the share.
9. Tell the website project that Cowork copy is allowed again, and close #2134.

**Cross-references:** ADR-023 (the plugin-stdio bridge), ADR-044 (workspace detection and the heal pass), ADR-038 (the integration policy this amends), ADR-053 (the flag pattern), #316, #317, #1373, #1455, #1598, #1727, #1827 (decision E), #2134.

## ADR-056: The Non-Loopback Bind Ships Dark

**Status:** Accepted (2026-10-05)

**Context.** Cutting Cowork from v1.0 (ADR-055) left one Cowork-era surface standing: `TANDEM_BIND_HOST`, which lets the npm server listen on a LAN address. Asked whether that mode should leave v1.0 too, Bryan chose to drop it, and then asked what the real use case was. The answer on record:

- It was built in v0.7.0 (2026-04-20) as part of the "Cowork foundation", so a Cowork VM could reach the server. The half that would have set it ("Cowork mode binds `0.0.0.0`") never shipped. Nothing in the product sets it to a non-loopback value: the desktop sidecar passes `SIDECAR_BIND_HOST = "127.0.0.1"` explicitly, and nothing else sets it at all.
- What it gives is narrower than "sharing a session on a LAN", which is what `docs/configuration.md` called it. Hocuspocus is hardcoded to `127.0.0.1`, so nobody on the LAN can open the editor. A token holder on the LAN can read `/api` and call every MCP tool over `POST /mcp`.
- The one plausible user is Claude Code in a VM, a container or WSL2 with NAT networking, driving a Tandem on the host. That is unverified: no issue or report records anyone doing it.
- It is where three open findings lived (#1952, #1884's LAN half, #1906; #1884 was closed at the source by #1885 on 2026-10-28, and #1952 was fixed per request on 2026-11-05, leaving #1906), and every security audit has had to reason about the LAN Host allowlist, the `NON_LOOPBACK_ALLOWED` carve-outs and the token-on-LAN checks.

Bryan accepted the recommendation to refuse the bind behind a ships-dark flag, keeping the code, and by approving the plan for this ADR decided the two acceptances recorded below.

**Decision.** A release build of the npm server never listens beyond this computer.

- **The flag.** `LAN_BIND_ENABLED` is a literal `false` in `src/shared/constants.ts`, with no build define and no environment override. An override would be a second opt-in to the exposure this removes.
- **The refusal runs first.** `resolveBindHostEnv` (`src/server/bind-check.ts`) is called at the top of `main()`, before the #1758 probe, the app-data claim, the store lock and session restore. While dark, a value other than `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `::1`, unset or empty is:
  - **refused** in HTTP mode: the message names ADR-056 and the process exits 1 having claimed, locked and killed nothing;
  - **ignored** in any other transport (the stdio branch): nothing listens on the bind host there, so refusing would only break an MCP client's start. The effective host becomes `127.0.0.1`, which also keeps the token and multi-homed checks from firing on a bind that never happens. Both `#1758` probe sites ask the decided host rather than the env's, so an ignored LAN value is not probed and then read as "nobody here".
  - The refusal is written with `writeSync`, because on a Windows terminal stderr is asynchronous and `process.exit` would drop it. The message quotes the value with `JSON.stringify` and names the three accepted values.
  - This changes two edges deliberately. Values that are not an IP (`LOCALHOST`, `[::1]`, garbage) used to exit with "not a valid IP" and now take the same arms. `127.0.0.2` and `::ffff:127.0.0.1` used to work once a token existed and are now refused, the cost of an exact three-value loopback set.
- **A guard at the listener.** `startMcpServerHttp` throws on a non-loopback host while dark, so a caller that never goes through `main()` cannot open a LAN socket either. It is a backstop, not the refusal: reached from `main()` it would fire after the store lock and the port kills, and exit without `shutdown()`. The early refusal keeps that path unreachable.
- **Kept, unreachable from another machine:** `NON_LOOPBACK_ALLOWED`, the `resolvedLanIP` Host allowlist, the `/health` and `/api` non-loopback scrubbing, `TANDEM_LAN_IP` and `TANDEM_ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_LAN`. "Unreachable from another machine" is the precise claim: a same-machine process connecting from another 127/8 source address still counts as non-loopback, needs the token, and reaches strictly less than it would from `127.0.0.1`.

**Consequences.**
- **Findings.** #1952 and #1906 move to Accepted (bounded), decided by Bryan 2026-10-05, each void if the flag flips. #1952's remaining reach needs a user-built port forwarder with a port mismatch. #1906's hole-or-intended decision is still owed and becomes re-enable item 2; its tracker issue was closed by #2059, which scoped the docs without making it. #1884 stays open: its `GET` half is a local-caller exposure that never needed the bind. *(2026-10-28: #1885 deleted the relay, closing #1884 entirely.)* *(2026-11-05: #1952 is fixed and leaves Accepted: `wakeUrlField()` returns the address only when the request's TCP peer is loopback, independent of the flag, which also settles re-enable item 3.)*
- **Port forwarding is the new thing to warn about.** A user who needs VM or WSL2-NAT access will reach for a forwarder. One that connects with a loopback `Host` (an `ssh -L` tunnel, for instance) arrives from `127.0.0.1` and can look like a local program: no token needed, and write access, which is more than LAN mode gave. (A forwarder that passes the client's LAN `Host` through is refused by the `/api` and `/mcp` Host checks.) `docs/configuration.md` and `docs/troubleshooting.md` say not to do it.
- **Docs.** User-facing docs (`configuration.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.env.example`, `cli.md`) no longer describe LAN exposure; `configuration.md` keeps its `## LAN exposure` heading because `cli.md` links to it. Internal docs (`security.md`, `architecture.md`, `mcp-tools.md`, ADR-050) keep describing the code under a ships-dark note. `tests/docs/mcp-mutation-asymmetry-claims.test.ts` no longer reads the two user-facing files.
- **`tandem doctor`** no longer conditions its non-loopback-url warning on `TANDEM_BIND_HOST`: in this version that url can never be this Tandem.
- **Ownership stories.** The five remaining Cowork-actor stories in `docs/spikes/multi-session-ownership/stories.md`, all set under a non-loopback bind, are suspended the way OWN-X-02 was under ADR-055.
- **Desktop.** No behaviour change: the sidecar already passed a constant `127.0.0.1`.
- **Tests.** `tests/server/lan-bind-ship-dark.test.ts` pins the literal, table-tests `resolveBindHostEnv` dark and lit, checks the listener guard, pins both probe sites to the decided host, and spawns the server entry in an isolated home on free ports twice: HTTP mode with `0.0.0.0`, asserting the ADR-056 message and that no app-data directory was created, and stdio mode with a LAN IP, asserting the "Ignoring" line and that it keeps running. The HTTP message is the discriminator: with the refusal unwired the child still exits 1, at the token check, and that was measured.

**Re-enable checklist** (tracked in #2141).
1. Flip `LAN_BIND_ENABLED`, and retire `tests/server/lan-bind-ship-dark.test.ts`'s dark assertions. Lit, an empty `TANDEM_BIND_HOST` still reaches the IP validation and exits there, as before this ADR; decide whether that should stay.
2. Decide #1906: gate mutating MCP tools on loopback, carve out an MCP equivalent of `NON_LOOPBACK_ALLOWED`, or record remote writes as intended.
3. ~~Fix #1952 (suppress `wakeUrl` under a non-loopback bind).~~ **Done 2026-11-05**, per request rather than per bind: `wakeUrl` is withheld from any non-loopback peer, so nothing is owed here at the re-enable. *(#1884's LAN half, the `POST` carve-out, was retired by #1885 on 2026-10-28, which deleted the route and its `NON_LOOPBACK_ALLOWED` entry.)*
4. Restore the user-facing docs from the PR that shipped this ADR, and put `docs/configuration.md` and `docs/troubleshooting.md` back on the asymmetry test's lists.
5. Re-derive the five suspended ownership stories against design v5 (`docs/spikes/multi-session-ownership/README.md`) rather than un-suspending them as written: v5 does not re-status v4.1's stories, so they describe a design that no longer exists.
6. Restore `tandem doctor`'s `TANDEM_BIND_HOST` condition on its non-loopback-url warning.
7. Move #1906 back out of Accepted in `docs/security.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. (#1952 already left Accepted when it was fixed, 2026-11-05.)
8. Decide what multi-session ownership (ADR-054) gives a LAN peer. Under a non-loopback bind, LAN MCP peers become owner records that can hold and take documents and read other sessions' labels, through the #1906 path; record that or refuse it before re-enabling.
9. The launcher's run MCP config (ADR-054, #2358 PR 3a) carries no auth token, because a loopback caller needs none; a non-loopback bind must revisit that before Tandem's Claude's child can reach it.
10. The owner handle's destination rule (the bridge's `ownerHandleForUpstream`) must not widen with the bind, as `tokenMayGoTo` does: the handle goes only to `http:` on `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`, at the URL the launcher named.

**Cross-references:** ADR-050 (the `/api` invariant), ADR-055 (Cowork setup ships dark), ADR-053 (the flag pattern), #1758 (the startup probe), #1884, #1906, #1952, #2059, #2141.
